Spoken of one who has been sadly disappointed in his expectations. The proverb is said to refer to a parrot that on seeing a green pod on a silk-cotton tree believed it would ripen into fruit, wheres it eventually burst and the cotton was scattered to the winds.





* * *





iluppaip pūvait tiruppiṉāl irupuṟamum pottal.

If the flower of ilupai (Bassia Longifolia) be turned, it will be found hollow on both sides.





* * *





ilai tiṉṉi kāy aṟiyāṉ.

He who feeds on leaves knows not the flavor of fruit.





* * *





ilaiyum paḻuppum eṅkum uṇṭu.

Leaves green and sear are found every where.





* * *





ilaukīkam vaitīkam iraṇṭum vēṇṭum.

The secular and the sacred are both necessary.





* * *





illatu vārātu uḷḷatu pōkātu.

That which is not will not come, that which is, will not go.





* * *





illaṟam peritu tuṟavaṟam ciṟitu.

Domestic life is better than that of the ascetic.





* * *





illaṟam peritu tuṟavaṟam paḻippu.

Domestic life is honourable, that of the ascetic is disgraceful.





* * *





illātavaṉ pollātavaṉ.

He that is destitute is wicked.





* * *





illātatu piṟavātu aḷḷātatu kuṟaiyātu.

That which is not, will not come out; that from which nothing is taken; will not lessen.





* * *





illātu colli allaṟ paṭutal.

Uttering a falsehood and suffering the evil thereof.





* * *





illāḷai viṭṭu vallāṇmai pēcukiṟatā?

What! is it to speak manfully after deserting thy wife?





* * *





illātum illaṉ iruppatum illaṉ.

He lacks nothing, nor does he possess any thing.





* * *





illātavaṉ peṇcāti ellārukkum tōḻiyā?

Is the wife of the destitute the friend or companion of all?





* * *





illai eṉṟa vīṭṭil palliyum cērātu.

Even a lizard will not enter a house occupied by the niggardly.





* * *





illōr irappatu iyalpu.

It is natural for the destitute to beg.





* * *





ivaḷ vilaimōril veṇṇey eṭuttut talaimakaṉukkuk kaliyāṇam paṇṇuvāṉ.

She will make butter out of the butter milk-given for sale, and make provision for the marriage of her first-born son.





* * *





ivaṉukkum avaṉukkum ēḻām poruttam.

There are seven points of agreement between this person and that.





* * *





ivaṉukkuc collum putti kaṭaliṟ peruṅkāyam karaittatu pōl irukkiṟatu.

The advice given to him is like assafœtida dissolved in the sea.





* * *





ivaṉ ūrārukkup piḷḷai.

He is the child of the whole village.

Said of a self-willed wandering youth.





* * *





ivaṉ makā periya kaḷḷaṉ, kālālē muṭintataik kaiyālē aviḻkkiṟatu aritu.

He is a clever rogue, what he ties with his feet it is difficult to untie with the hand.





* * *





ivaṉ kallātu kaṟṟavaṉ, uḷḷaṅkaiyil vaikuṇṭam kāṭṭuvāṉ.

He has attained the unattainable; he can show Vaikundam Paradise in the palm of his hand.





* * *





ivaṉ putti ulakkaikkoḻuntu.

His wit is as sharp as a wooden pestle.





* * *





iḻavukku vantavaḷ tāli aṟuppāḷā?

Will she who attends a funeral remove her marriage symbol?





* * *





iḻukkuṭaiya pāṭṭiṟku icai naṉṟu.

Harmony sets off a faulty stanza.





* * *





iḻuvai kaṇṭāl aṭi pārppāṉēṉ?

When the trail marks are before you, why look for footprints?





* * *





iḻai āyiram poṉ peṟṟa intiravarṇappaṭṭu.

A scarlet cloth each thread of which is worth a thousand gold pieces.





* * *





iḻaiyat tīṭṭik kuḻaiya vaṭittal.

Thoroughly pounded and properly boiled.





* * *





iḷakiṉa irumpaik kaṇṭāṟ kollaṉ kuṇṭiyait tūkki aṭippāṉ.

When the smith perceives that the iron is soft, he will raise himself to the stroke.

The Indian smith squats at the forge, and rises to his feet only when he uses a two-handed hammer.





* * *





iḷaṅkaṉṟu payaṉ aṟiyumā?

Does a young calf know fear?





* * *





iḷamaiyiṟ cōmpal mutumaiyiṉ miṭimai.

Indolence, in youth will bring poverty in old age.





* * *





iḷamaiyil muyaṟci mutumaiyiṟ kākkum.

Industry in youth will support one in old age.





* * *





iḷamaiyiṟ paḻakkam eppōtum maṟavātu.

The habits of early life will never be forgotten.





* * *





iḷamaiyum mutumaiyum cariyāṉa vayatalla.

Youth and senility do not properly belong to a man’s age.





* * *





iḷaveyil kāyāta nīyā tī pāyappōkiṟāy?

Do you who cannot endure the early beams of the sun presume to walk over fire?





* * *





iḷaiñaṉ āṉālum āṭuvāṉ mūppu.

Although young he will play the man.





* * *





iḷaittavaḷ talaiyil īrum pēṉum.

In the tresses of a poor woman are found nits and lice.





* * *





iḷaittavaṉ ciṉēkitaṉaic cēr.

Associate with the friend of the poor.





* * *





iḷaittavaṉē eḷḷai vitai.

O you weak fellow, sow the sessamum seed.





* * *





iḷaiyāḷ ilaitiṉṉi mūttāḷ kāyaṟivāḷ.

The younger sister feeds on leaves, the elder is accustomed to fruit.





* * *





iḷayāḷē vāṭi malaiyāḷam pōvōm, mūttāḷē vāṭi muṭṭikkoṇṭu cāvōm.

Come my younger sister, we will proceed to Malayalam; come my elder sister, we will strike our heads together and die.





* * *





iṟaku muṟṟip paṟaivai āṉāl, ellām taṉ vayiṟṟait tāṉ pārkkum.

When mature and on the wing, all birds will look after their own food.





* * *





iṟakkum kālam vantāl piṟakkum īcalukkuc ciṟaku.

When death approaches, white-ants take wing.





* * *





iṟakkum kālam varāmal piṟakkumā īcalukkuc ciṟaku?

Will white-ants take wing except on the approach of death?





* * *





iṟaṅkum tuṟaiyilē nīttāṉāla, inta āṟṟai eppaṭik kaṭakkiṟatu?

If on stepping into the river one is obliged to swim, how will he get across?





* * *





iṟaṅku poḻutilē maruntu kuṭi.

Take medicine at sun-set.





* * *





iṟantavaṉ iruppavaṉukku vaḻikāṭṭi.

The dead is the pilot of the living.





* * *





iṟantavaṉ piḷḷai iruntavaṉ aṭaikkalam.

The child of the dead is the ward of the living.





* * *





iṟantavaṉukku eḷḷum taṇṇīrum.

Sessamum and water offered to the dead.





* * *





iṟutta kuṭikku aṉarttam illai.

A family out of debt is out of danger.





* * *





iṟuppāṉukkup paṇamum kiṭaiyātu. uḻaippāṉukkup peṇṇum kiṭaiyātu.

He who pays his debts cannot obtain money, nor can the labourer obtain a wife.





* * *





iṟaicci tiṉkiṟavar kaṭuppukku maruntu aṟivār.

They who live on flesh are acquainted with the medicine for tooth-ache.





* * *





iṟaicci tiṉṟālum elumpaik kōttup pōṭṭukkoḷḷalāmā?

Is one to wear a neck-lace of bones because he eats flesh?

This and the preceding proverb are somewhat singular but not unnatural among a vegetarian people.





* * *





iṟaitta kiṇaṟu ūṟum, iṟaiyāta kiṇaṟu nāṟum.

The water of a well always drawn, is fresh, that of a well not drawn is fetid.





* * *





iṟaitta kiṇaṟu curakkum.

The water of a well always drawn springs up a-fresh.





* * *





iṉam pirinta māṉpōla.

Like a deer separated from its herd.





* * *





iṉimēl oru teyvattaik kaiyeṭukkiṟatā?

Hereafter is it to lift up the hands to a god?





* * *





iṉimēl emalōkapariyantam cātikkalām.

Hereafter he may effect his purpose as far as the realms of Yama.





* * *





iṉpamum tuṉpamum poṟumaiyilē

Joy and grief must be regulated by moderation.





* * *





iṉpattil ācai evarkkum uṇṭu.

Happiness is desired by all.





* * *





iṉpamum tuṉpamum eṭutta uṭalukku.

The body it is that is affected by pleasure and pain.





* * *





iṉṟu iruppār nāḷaikku illai.

Those who are alive to-day may not be on the morrow.





* * *





iṉṟaikku ilai aṟuttavaṉ nāḷaikkuk kulai aṟuppāṉ.

He who cut off the leaves to-day may possibly cut off the bunch to-morrow.





* * *





iṉṟaikku ākiṟatu nāḷaikku ākaṭṭum.

That which is practicable to-day may be so on the morrow.





* * *





iṉṟaikkuc cettāl nāḷaikku iraṇṭu nāḷ.

If one die to-day, tomorrow will be the second day.





* * *





iṉṟai eṉpatum nāḷai eṉpatum illai eṉpataṟku aṭaiyāḷam.

To say to-day, to-morrow, indicates refusal.





* * *





ī.



ī kalaiyāmal tēṉ eṭuppārkaḷ, eṭukkāmaṟ piṭippārkaḷ.

They will take the honey without dispersing the bees, they will take it without raising it up.





* * *





īkai uṭaiyavaṉ ekkaḷippu aṭaivāṉ.

The liberal giver will be happy.





* * *





īkku viṭam talaiyil, tēḷukkuk koṭukkil.

In a fly, the head is the seat of poison, in a scorpion, the tail.





* * *





īcalukku ellām pakai.

All are against the winged white-ants.





* * *





īcal iṟaku ellāvaṟṟilum mirutu.

The wing of a white-ant is the softest of all things.





* * *





īcal maṭintāṟpōl māṇṭatē cēṉai.

The army perished like winged white-ants.





* * *





īcaṟ perumpōkkil tavaḷai tatti viḻuṅkukiṟatu.

The frog leaps and swallows the swarming winged white-ants.





* * *





īcaṉukku oppu iṅku oṉṟum illai.

Nothing here is equal to God.





* * *





īccaṅkaḷḷu etilum kuḷircci

Date-palm toddy is the coolest of beverages.





* * *





īccam kāṭṭil erumai kuṭiyirunta katai.

The story of a buffalo that lived in a date-palm grove.





* * *





īṭaṉ pāṭañcāṉ kūḻaiyerutu nuḷampañcātu.

A strong man fears not hardship, nor does a bullock with a stunted tail fear gnats.





* * *





īṭākātavaṉai etirākkātē.

Do not oppose your inferior.





* * *





īṭuḷḷa kuṭikkuk kēṭu illai.

A wealthy family is not exposed to danger.





* * *





īṭṭiya poruḷilum eḻuttē uṭaimai.

Learning is more substantial than accumulated riches.





* * *





īṭṭi eṭṭiyamaṭṭum kuttum, paṇam patiṉkātamum kuttum.

A spear wounds as far as it reaches, the effect of money reaches ten kathams (100 miles).





* * *





īṭṭukku īṭum cōṭṭukkuc cōṭumāy iruntāl vāci.

Like for like, and equality in a pair are desirable.





* * *





īṭṭukkum pāṭṭukkum iruntāḷ iṭukumari.

The maiden who was given in marriage was equal to all the exigencies of life.





* * *





īntu pārttāl immi veḷi ākum.

The smallest fraction will come out by division.

immi = 1,075,200th part of a unit.





* * *





īppiciṉi irappatukūṭak kakkicam.

A miser will find even begging difficult.





* * *





īyattaik kāyccalām irumpaik kāyccalāmā?

Lead may be melted; can iron?





* * *





īyam piṭittavaṉ etu colliṉum kēḷāṉ.

He who has taken up lead will not listen, no matter what is said to him.





* * *





īyāta pullaṉai evviṭattilum kāṇōm.

A niggardly savage is nowhere found.





* * *





īyār tēṭṭait tīyār koḷvār.

The wicked obtain by rapacity what the miserly accumulate.





* * *





īyār poruḷukkut tīyār.

The wicked enjoy the property of the miser.





* * *





īyum eṟumpum eṅkum uṇṭu.

Flies and ants abound every-where.





* * *





īyaip piṭittāl kai vēṟu, kāl vēṟu.

If you catch a fly, its limbs will be separated.





* * *





īyaippōlē cuttamum eṟumpaippōlē palamum.

Clean as a fly and strong as an ant.





* * *





īraccīlaiyaippōṭṭuk kaḻuttu aṟuppāṉ.

He will cut the throat after tying a wet cloth round it.





* * *





īra nāvukku elumpu illai.

There is no bone in a slanderous tongue.





* * *





īram aṟṟa iṭattilē ī moykkumā, īram uḷḷa iṭatitlē ī moykkumā?

Do insects swarm in a dry place or in a wet place?





* * *





īraveṅkāyattiṟku irupatteṭṭup purai.

In an onion there are twenty-eight coats.





* * *





īrai niṉaippāṉ, pērai maṟakkāṉ.

He who thinks of a nit will not forget its name.





* * *





īraip pēṉākkip pēṉaip perumāḷ ākkiṉēṉ.

I magnified the nit into a louse, and the louse into Perumál.





* * *





īr uruvap pēṉ akappaṭumā?

Will lice be obtained when drawing nits between the finger and the thumb.





* * *





ī viṣṭittatum nāy tiruṭittiṉṟatum.

That which a bee ejects and that which a dog has stolen.





* * *





ī viḻunta cātam eṭuttāloḻiyap pōmā?

Will a fly that has fallen into the boiled rice go unless it be taken out?





* * *





īvōṉukku oru pōcaṉam, irappōṉukku ērāḷam.

To the giver a meal, to the beggar plenty.





* * *





īḻattiṟ cekkāṭa iṅkē patam pārkka.

The press is turned in Lanka, and the material under pressure is examined here.





* * *





īṉavum teriyātu nakkavum teriyātu

It knows not how to bring forth, nor how to lick.





* * *





īṉṟavaḷ tāy pāṭṭi, ivaḷ tāyiṉ tāy pīṭṭi.

The mother of one's mother is his grand-mother, her mother is his great grand mother.





* * *





īṉṟōr nañciṟ cāṉṟōr illai.

Indulgence in the parent is not good for the child.





* * *





u.



ukamuṭiya maḻai peytālum ōṭṭāṅkiḷiñcal payir ākumā?

Though it should rain to the end of the world, would broken oyster shells vegetate?





* * *





ukirccuṟṟiṉmēl ammi viḻuntatupōl.

As a grind-stone fell on the whitlow.





* * *





uṅkaḷ uṟavilē vēkiṟataippārkkilum oru kaṭṭu viṟakilē vēkiṟatu nallatu.

It is better to be consumed by a faggot than by your friendship.





* * *





uṅkaḷ peṇṭukaḷ koṇṭal aṭittāl kaṇkaḷ koḷḷātu.

The eyes will be over-powered by the dancing of your women.





* * *





uṅkaḷaik kaṭalilē kaikaḻuviṉēṉ.

I have washed my hands of you in the sea.





* * *





ucu piṭi eṉṟāl nī piṭi eṉkiṟatām nāy.

It is said that when he urged his dog to seize, it replied, you do so.





* * *





uccantalaiyiṟ ceruppāl aṭittālum ucci kuḷirumā?

If slippered on the head, will the crown of the head become cool?





* * *





uṭampu ellām puḻuttavaṉ ammaṉ kōvilai keṭuttakatai.

The story of a man who defiled Amman covil by his ulcerated body.





* * *





uṭampuḷainta kaḻutai uppukaḷattiṟkup pōṉatupōla.

As the wearied ass went to the salt-pit.





* * *





uṭampu eṭuttavaṉ ellām ōṭu eṭuttāṉ.

All who have taken a body have taken an alms-dish.





* * *





uṭampu tēṟṟikkoṇṭu allavā yōkattil pōkavēṇṭum?

Must one not invigorate the body and then proceed to meditation?





* * *





uṭampu eṅkum cuṭukiṟa taḻalai maṭiyilē kaṭṭukiṟāy.

Thou huggest up in thy bosom the embers that warm every part of the body.





* * *





uṭampu muḻutum naṉaintavarukkuk kūtal eṉṉa?

Of what consequence is cold to him whose whole body is drenched?





* * *





uṭalukkō pāl vārttu uṇpatu, ūrukkō pālvārttu uṇpatu

Do we feed on milk for the benefit of the body, or that our neighbours may know it?





* * *





uṭal iraṇṭu uyir aṉṟu.

Divided as regards body, one in soul.





* * *





uṭal oruvaṉukkup piṟantu nā palarukkup piṟantatu.

The body is born for one, the tongue for many.





* * *





uṭal uḷḷavaraikkum kaṭal koḷḷāta kavalai.

Anxieties which the ocean cannot contain attend the body as long as it exists.





* * *





uṭaṉpiṟappu illā uṭampu pāḻ.

The body of one who has no kindred is exposed to desolation.





* * *





uṭāppuṭaivai pūccikku irai.

A garment not worn is a prey to moths.





* * *





uṭutta puṭaivaitāṉē pāmpāyk kaṭikkiṟatu.

The cloth one has put on bites as a snake.





* * *





uṭumpu pōṉālum pōkiṟatu kaiyaiviṭṭāl pōtum.

The guana may go, it is enough if the hand be free.





* * *





uṭumpukku iraṇṭu nākku maṉitaṉukku oru nākku.

The guana has two tongues, man only one.





* * *





uṭumpukku iraṇṭu nākku, maṉitaṉukku iraṇṭu nākku uṇṭā?

The guana has a double tongue, has man the same?





* * *





uṭaimuḷḷukku etirē utaikkalāmā?

Can you kick against the thorns of the Acacia?





* * *





uṭaimai eṉpatu kalviyuṭaimai.

Learning is real wealth.





* * *





uṭaimaiyum vaṟumaiyum oru vaḻi nillā.

Wealth and poverty are not abiding.





* * *





uṭaimaiyum koṭuttu arumaiyum kulaikiṟatā?

What! give one’s property and sacrifice one’s reputation?





* * *





uṭaiyavaṉ pārāp payir uruppaṭumā?

Will a crop not looked after by the owner flourish?





* * *





uṭaiyavaṉ coṟpaṭi uralaiccuṟṟik kaḷaipaṟi.

Pluck up the weeds about the mortar as your master bids you.





* * *





uṭaiyavaṉiṟ kaippaṟiṉavaṉ miṭukkaṉ.

An embezzler is more obstinate than the owner.





* * *





uṭaiyavaṉ coṟpaṭi kamukaṭi kaḷaipaṟi.

Weed around the areca tree at thy master’s biding.





* * *





uṭaiyavaṉ illāc cēlai oru muḻam kaṭṭai.

The cloth whose owner is absent is a cubit too short.





* * *





uṭaiyār vīṭṭu mōrukku akappaik kaṇakku eṉṉa?

What necessity is there for estimating the quantity of butter-milk by the ladle in the homestead of the chief?





* * *





uṭkārntu allavō paṭukkavēṇṭum?

You must sit before lying down, must you not?





* * *





uṭkāntavaṉaik kaṭṭa māṭṭātavaṉ ōṭukiṟavaṉaik kaṭṭuvāṉā?

Can he tie a man that is running who cannot tie one that is sitting!





* * *





uṭcuvar irukkap puṟaccuvar pūcalāmā?

What! beautify the outside of a wall while the inside is neglected?





* * *





uṭcuvar tīṟṟip puṟaccuvar tīṟṟu.

Garnish the inside of the wall and then the outside.





* * *





uṭpuṟattukku veḷippuṟam kaṇṇāṭi.

A mirror reflects whatever is before it.





* * *





uṇarvillāk karuviyum uppillāc cōṟum cari.

A mere instrument void of feeling and food without salt are alike.





* * *





uṇkiṟa cōṟṟilē nañcaik kalakkiṟatā?

What, is it to mix poison with the rice one is eating?





* * *





uṇṭa uṭampiṟku uṟuti, uḻuta pulattil nellu.

There is strength in a body well fed, and grain in the ploughed field.





* * *





uṇṭa piḷḷai urañceyyum.

A child that eats well thrives.





* * *





uṇṭa vayiṟṟukkuc cōṟum moṭṭaittalaikku eṇṇeyumā?

Is rice required for a full stomach, or oil for a baldhead?





* * *





uṇṭatutāṉ ēppam varum.

That which has been eaten will be evidenced in the belching.





* * *





uṇṭa vīṭṭukku iraṇṭakam paṇṇalāmā

May hospitality be requited by treachery?





* * *





uṇṭa uṭampu urukum tiṉṟa pākkuc civakkum.

The body nourished by another’s bounty will melt, arecanut when chewed turns red.





* * *





uṇṭa cuṟṟam urukum.

Friendship cemented by food will compassionate.





* * *





uṇṭār mēṉi, kaṇṭāl teriyum.

The body shews who is well fed.





* * *





uṇṭāl tīrum paci, kaṇṭāl tīrumā?

Hunger is appeased by eating, will it be so by looking at food?





* * *





uṇṭāl tiṉṟāl uṟavu, koṇṭāl koṭuttāl uṟavu.

Friendship is promoted and strengthned by hospitality and also by intermarriage.





* * *





uṇṭālum uṟutippaṭa uṇṇa vēṇṭum.

When you eat, do so to keep up your strength.





* * *





uṇṭāṟ kollum viṣam.

Poison destroys when taken.





* * *





uṇṭi curuṅkutal peṇṭirkku aḻaku.

Moderation in food becomes women.





* * *





uṇṭu tiṉṟu uḷḷē iruveṉṟāl uyara eḻumpi ēṉ kutikkiṟāy?

When you are told to eat heartily and remain in-doors, why do you rise up and jump?





* * *





uṇṭu uṟiyil iru eṉṟāl uruṇṭu taraiyil viḻukiṟāy.

If I say eat and sit on the hanging-shelf, you fall to the ground.





* * *





uṇṭu koḻuttāl naṇṭu vaḷaiyil irātu.

If a crab gets fat, it will not stay in its hole.





* * *





uṇṭu tiṉṟu uyaram āṉāl ūrilē kāriyam eṉṉa?

What business has he in the village seeing that he is grown up?





* * *





uṇṇak kai calittu irukkiṟāṉ.

His hand is weary with eating.





* * *





uṇṇa vā eṉṟāl kutta varukiṟāy.

When I call you to eat, you come to strike me.





* * *





uṇṇākkai aṟuttuc cuṇṇāmpukkuṟi iṭuvēṉ.

I will cut your uvula and smear it with chunam-lime.





* * *





uṇṇāc cottu maṇṇāyp pōkum.

Property not enjoyed comes to nought.





* * *





uṇṇāmal ūr ellām tiriyalām uṭukkāmal oru vīṭṭukkum pōkalākātu.

One may wander over the whole country without eating, but one may not go to a single house without dressing.





* * *





uṇṇāmal oṉpatu vīṭu pōkalām, uṭukkāmal oru vīṭum pōkal ākātu.

One may go to nine houses without eating, but not to one without garments.





* * *





uṇṇum kīraiyilē naṇṇum pulluruvi.

Mistleloe attached to edible greens.





* * *





uṇpatu irukka oru karumam ceyyēl.

Do not go to work without eating.





* * *





uṇpāṉ tiṉpāṉ civappirāmaṇaṉ, kuttukku niṟpāṉ vīra muṣṭi.

The Saiva Brahman will freely eat, the ferocious mendicant will show fight.





* * *





uṇpāraip pārttālum uḻuvāraip pārkkal ākātu.

Though you look at those who are eating, you may not look at those who are ploughing.





* * *





uṇmai collik keṭṭārum poy colli vāḻntārum illai.

None ever perishes by speaking the truth, none flourishes by uttering falsehood.





* * *





utaṭu maṉṟāṭap pōy uḷḷirunta pallum pōṉāṟpōla.

As if the teeth also went out when the lips had gone to beg.





* * *





utaṭṭilē uḷḷa vāḻaippaḻattai uḷḷē taḷḷuvār uṇṭō?

Are there any who push into the mouth the plantain that is already between the lips?





* * *





utaṭṭukku miñciṉa pallum tiruṭṭukku miñciṉa kaiyum ākātu.

Protruding teeth and a hand given to thieving are bad.





* * *





utaṭṭilē puṇ āṉāl pāl kaṟakkātā?

Will the cow not yield its milk because it has sore lips?





* * *





utaṭṭut turumpu ūtap pōkātu?

A small particle adhering to the lip cannot be removed by blowing.





* * *





utara nimittam veku kirtavēṣam.

Divers disguises for the sake of the belly.





* * *





utavāp paḻaṅkalamē ōcai illā veṇkalamē?

Thou art a useless vessel, a piece of brass without sound!





* * *





utavāta ceṭṭikkuc cīṭṭu eḻutiṉatupōla.

Like writing a chit to a thrifty merchant.





* * *





utavi ceyvārkkuc cīṭṭu eḻutiṉatupōla.

What, accident can harm those who help others?





* * *





utaṟukāli vantāḷ uḷḷatum keṭuttāḷ.

A woman with a bustling gait came and destroyed what there was.





* * *





utārikkup poṉṉum turumpu.

Even gold is a thing of nought to the generous.





* * *





utirattukku allō urukkam irukkum?

Sympathy arises, does it not, from consanguinity?





* * *





utaitta kāl puḻukkiṟatupōtu allavō puḻukkam?

Worms will breed in due course, will they not, in a foot accustomed to kick?





* * *





utaitta kāl puḻukkiṟataṟkumuṉṉē aṭivayiṟu cīḻkaṭṭukiṟatu.

Before his kicking feet were eaten of worms his abdomen had ulcerated.





* * *





utaippāṉukku veḷuppāṉ cātivaṇṇāṉ.

He that washes for him who kicks him is a first-rate washerman.





* * *





uttamaṉukkum tappilikkum uṭaṉpaṭikkai vēṇṭām.

Contracts are needless as regards honest men and cheats. : The one needs no binding, and the other admits no obligations.





* * *





uttamaccēvakaṉ peṟṟatāykku atikam.

A faithful servant is more than a mother.





* * *





uttamaṉukku ettālum kēṭu illai.

The good are exposed to no danger.





* * *





uttiyōkam puruṣa laṣaṇam.

Exertion is a manly quality.





* * *





uttiyōkattukkut takka cukam.

Facilities suited to one's office.





* * *





upakārattukku apakāram varuvatu turatiṣṭam.

It is unfortunate when evil is returned for good.





* * *





upacariyāka vīṭṭilē uṇṇātiruppatē oru kōṭi taṉam.

It is a crore of wealth to abstain from entertainment in a house void of civility.





* * *





upacāram ceytavarkku apacāram paṇṇukiṟatā?

What! are the civil to be requited by incivility?





* * *





upacāra vārttai kācākumā, uṇṭāloḻiyap paci tīrumā?

Will mere ceremony pass for money, will hunger be appeased otherwise than by eating?





* * *





upāyattāl ākiṟatu parākkiramattāl ākumā?

Can that which may be realized by cunning be obtained by force?





* * *





uppu irunta pāṇṭamum upāyam irunta neñcamum taṭṭi uṭaiyāmal tāṉē uṭaiyum.

A salt-cup and a deceitful heart will of themselves come to destruction.





* * *





uppu irunta pāṇṭamum uṇmai illā neñcum taṭṭi uṭaiyāmal tāmē uṭaiyum.

A vessel impregnated with salt, and a mind without truth need none to destroy them, they destroy themselves.





* * *





uppu illāp paṇṭam kuppaiyilē.

Unsalted food is fit only for the rubbish heap.





* * *





uppu iṭṭavarkaḷai uḷḷaḷavum niṉai.

Remember through life those who have given you salt.





* * *





uppu mutal karppūram varaikkum vēṇṭum.

Every thing from salt to camphor is required.

Said of things domestic and sacred.





* * *





upput tiṉṟavaṉ taṇṇīraik kuṭippāṉ.

He who has eaten salt will drink water.





* * *





uppukkaṇṭam paṟikoṭutta pārppāttipōl viḻikkiṟāy.

Thou starest as the Brahmanee who had lost her salted mutton.

In her bewilderment she had no remedy because she dared not make known the loss of a thing she ought not to have had in her possession.





* * *





uppu vāṇikaṉ aṟivāṉā karppūra vilai?

Does the salt merchant know the price of camphor?





* * *





uppuk kaṭṭiṉāl lōkam kaṭṭum.

If there be a supply of salt the world will be sustained.





* * *





uppum karppūramum oṉṟāy vaḻaṅkumā?

Are salt and camphor used together?





* * *





uppu nīr mēkam uṇṭāl ulakiṟ piravākam.

If the ocean were to become clouds, the world would be flooded.





* * *





upput taṇṇīrukku ilāmiccam vēr pōṭavēṇṭumā?

Must the root of cusa grass be added to sea-water to make it fragrant?





* * *





uppu miñciṉāl taṇṇīr, taṇṇīr miñciṉāl uppu.

Excess of salt calls for water, excess of water requires salt.





* * *





uppaic cintiṉaiyō tuppaic cintiṉaiyō?

Did you spill the salt or the food?





* * *





uppaittoṭṭu uppait tiṉṉātē.

Do not use salt both as food and condiment.





* * *





uppait toṭṭuk koṇṭu uralai viḻuṅkalām.

Using salt as a condiment you may swallow a mortar.





* * *





uppōṭu oṉpatum paruppōṭu pattum vēṇṭum.

Nine things are required with salt, ten with pulse.





* * *





umakku eṉṉa, vayatukku naraittatō mayirukku naraittatō?

Have you grown grey by age, or is your hair prematurely grey?





* * *





umiyaik kuṟṟik kaicalittatupōla.

As the hand was wearied by beating husks in a mortar.





* * *





uyarap paṟantālum ūrkkuruvi karuṭaṉ ākumā?

Though it may fly high, will a common bird become a hawk?





* * *





uyirum uṭalumpōla.

Like soul and body.





* * *





uyirai vaittirukkiṟatilum cettāṟ kuṇam.

It is more natural to die than to continue in life.





* * *





uyirōṭu tirumpip pārātavar cettāl muttam koṭuppārā?

Will he who would not turn to look at me when alive kiss me when dead?





* * *





uyirōṭu oru muttam tarātavaḷ cettāl uṭaṉkaṭṭai ēṟuvāḷā?

Will she who would not kiss me when alive ascend my funeral pyre when I am dead?





* * *





uyir iruntāl uppumāṟit tiṉṉalām.

If one is alive, he may subsist by bartering salt.





* * *





uralil akappaṭṭatu ulakkaikkut tappumā ?

Will that which is in the mortar escape the pestle?





* * *





uralilē talaiyai māṭṭikkoṇṭu ulakkaikkup payappaṭṭāl tīrumā ?

Will your fear of the pestle avail aught after your head has become fast in the mortar?





* * *





uralukku oru pakkam iṭi mattaḷattukku iraṇṭu pakkamum iṭi.

The mortar is beaten at one end, the drum at both.





* * *





ural pañcam aṟiyumā ?

Does the rice mortar know what a famine is?





* * *





ural pōy mattaḷattōṭē muṟaiyiṭṭatupōla.

As the mortar went to the tomtom with its complaints.

Used when one complains of his misfortunes to another who is in greater distress. The mortar is beaten at one end only, whereas the tomtom is beaten at both ends.





* * *





uru ēṟat tiru ēṟum.

Supernatural power increases according to the intensity of the incantations.





* * *





urukkam uḷḷa ciṟṟāttai otukkil vāṭi kaṭṭi aḻa.

O my loving aunt, come with me apart that we may embrace each other and weep.





* * *





uruci kaṇṭa pūṉai uṟiyait tāvum.

The cat that has tasted nice things will leap on the swinging-tray.





* * *





uruṭṭap puraṭṭa uḷḷatum uḷḷukku vāṅkum.

When one deals fraudulently, even that which he has diminishes.





* * *





uruṭṭum puraṭṭum oṭukkum ciṟappai.

Frauds and tricks will destroy reputation.





* * *





uruṭṭum puraṭṭum miraṭṭum collum.

Tricks and frauds and threats.





* * *





uruttirākṣap pūṉai oṉṟum teriyātupōl irukkum.

Like a beaded cat he appears as if he knew nothing.





* * *





uruttirākṣap pūṉai upatēcam paṇṇiṉatupōla.

As a beaded cat gave divine instruction.





* * *





uruppaṭat tiruppaṭum.

As the figure is formed, beauty will become manifest.





* * *





uruvattai alla kuṇattaippār, paṇattaiyalla caṉattaippār.

Look to the temper, not beauty, to the connections not at money, when choosing a wife.





* * *





uruvattiṉāl alla, iṉpap pēcciṉāl kiḷi naṉkumatikkappaṭum.

It is not for its form, but for its sweet prattling, that a parrot is esteemed.





* * *





uruviya vāḷai uṟaiyil iṭāta vīraṉ.

A hero who does not sheath his sword.





* * *





ulakattukku ñāṉam pēy, ñāṉattukku ulakam pēy.

To the world, wisdom is folly, to wisdom, the world is folly.





* * *





ulakam pala vitam.

The world has many forms.





* * *





ulakilē kaḷḷaṉukku ūrār yāvarum pakai.

The whole country is at enmity with the thief who dwells there.





* * *





ulakkaippūcaikku acaiyātatu tiruppāṭṭukku acaiyumā ?

Will that which has resisted the strokes of the pestle shake at a sacred chant?





* * *





ulakkaitēyntu uḷippiṭi āyiṟṟu.

The pestle by wearing away has become the handle of a chisel.





* * *





ulakkai peruttu uttiram āyiṟṟu.

The pestle grew and became a beam.





* * *





uluttaṉ viruntiṟku oppāṉatu oṉṟum illai.

Nothing can be compared to the banquet of a miser.





* * *





ulaivāyai mūṭiṉālum ūrvāyai mūṭalāmā?

You may cover the mouth of a rice-pot, but can you cover the mouth of the country?





* * *





ulōpikku iru celavu.

The avaricious are subject to double expense.





* * *





ullācanaṭai melukkukkukkēṭu miṉukku eṇṇey talaikkuk kēṭu.

An affected gait is injurious to graceful movement, and varnish is bad for the head.





* * *





uḻak kuḷir aṭittāl nāṟṟuppiṭuṅkappaṭātā?

If it be too cold to plough, why not put out the young plants?





* * *





uḻakku uṟṟārkkup patakkup paratēcikku.

A quarter of a measure to one’s friends, and sixteen measures to a religious mendicant.





* * *





uḻakkilē kiḻakku mēṟkā?

Is the distinction of east and west observable in an ullak a small measure of capacity?





* * *





uḻakkilē vaḻakku.

A dispute about an ullak.

See the preceding.





* * *





uḻakku miḷaku koṭuppāṉ ēṉ, oḷittiruntu miḷaku nīrkuṭippāṉ ēṉ?

Why give a measure of pepper, why drink the pepper water in a corner?





* * *





uḻaviṟku ēṟṟa koḻu.

A plough share adapted to the tillage.





* * *





uḻaviṉ mikutta ūtiyam illai.

Nothing more advantageous than tillage.





* * *





uḻavu oḻinta māṭu paṭṭippuṟattilē.

Cattle unfit for the plough are kept outside.





* * *





uḻavu māṭu āṉāl ūrukkuḷḷē vilaippaṭātā?

If the ox be fit for the plough, will it not fetch a price in its own village?





* * *





uḻavukku oru cuṟṟum varātu, ūṇukku muṉṉē varum.

At the plough it will not go a single round, at foddering time it comes first.





* * *





uḻavukkup piṇaittuviṭukiṟa māṭum, kūṭṭukkup piṭittuviṭukiṟa āḷum utavātu.

The bullock yoked to the plough by force, and a man acting as a subsitute are alike useless.





* * *





uḻukiṟataiviṭṭu naḻuvukiṟavaṉ teyvam āṭiṉāṟpōla.

Like one who, to avoid ploughing, feigned to be demonized.





* * *





uḻukiṟa nāḷaiyil ūrukkup pōy, aṟukkiṟa nāḷaiyil arivāḷ koṇṭuvantāṟpōla.

As if one retired into the interior in the ploughing season, and returned with a sickle at the approach of harvest.





* * *





uḻukiṟamāṭu paratēcam pōṉāl, aṅku oruvaṉ cuṭṭi uḻuvāṉ iṅku oruvaṉ kaṭṭi uḻuvāṉ.

If a ploughing bullock go astray, it will be yoked wherever found.





* * *





uḻukiṟa māṭṭai nukattāl aṭittāṟpōla.

Like beating the ploughing ox with the yoke.





* * *





uḻukiṟa māṭṭai erutu nakkiṉatupōla.

As an ox licked a yoke of oxen when ploughing.





* * *





uḻukiṟavaṉ iḷappamāṉāl erutu macciṉamuṟai koṇṭāṭum.

The ox treats the unskilful ploughman as his brother-in-law.





* * *





uḻutavaṉukku ūrkkaṇakkup paṇṇat teriyātu.

A ploughman is unable to keep village accounts.





* * *





uḻutavaṉ kaṇakkup pārttāl uḻakkēṉum miñcātu.

When a ploughman balances his accounts, not even an ullak of grain remains.





* * *





uḷavaṉ illāmal ūr aḻiyumā?

Can a country be destroyed if there be no spy?





* * *





uḷavu illāmal kaḷavu illai.

No spy, no theft.





* * *





uḷaivaḻiyum, aṭaimaḻaiyum, poti erutum, oruvaṉumāy alaikiṟāṉ.

He wanders alone after a pack-bull, through deep mud and incessant rain.





* * *





uḷḷīṭṭuk kaṭaṉum, uḷḷaṅkaic ciraṅkum keṭṭatu.

To be in debt to an inmate and to have the itch in the palm of the hand are intolerable evils.





* * *





uḷḷa piḷḷai uralai nakkikkoṇṭirukka, maṟṟoru piḷḷaikkut tiruppatikku naṭakkiṟāḷ.

While her child licks the mortar, she goes on a pilgrimage to Tirupati in expectation of another.





* * *





uḷḷaṅkaip pāṟcōṟṟai viṭṭup puṟaṅkai nakkiyatu pōla.

Like putting rice-milk in the palm and licking the back of the hand.





* * *





uḷḷataik koṇṭu illataip pārāṭṭalām.

With what we have we may pretend to have that which we have not.





* * *





uḷḷataic coṉṉāl uṭampellām puṇṇām.

It is said that the whole body breaks out into ulcers when the truth is told.





* * *





uḷḷatai viṟṟu nallataik koḷḷu.

Sell what you have and buy what is really good.





* * *





uḷḷaṉum kaḷḷaṉumāy irukkiṟāṉ.

He is both an inmate and a thief.





* * *





uḷḷaṅkaiyil oṉpatu koṇṭai muṭippēṉ.

I will tie nine knots of hair in the palm of the hand.





* * *





uḷḷaṅkaiyil nellikkaṉipōla.

Like the Nelli berry (Phyllanthus simplex) in the palm of the hand.

Said of something unmistakably evident.





* * *





uḷḷaṅkaiyil urōmamuḷaittatāyiṉ, aṟivilāṉ aṭaṅkuvāṉ.

If it be that hair has sprung up in the palm of the hand, a fool may yield to discipline.





* * *





uḷḷaṉum kaḷḷaṉum kūṭiṉāl viṭikiṟamaṭṭum tiruṭalām.

When an inmate of a house joins the thief, stealing may be carried on till day-break.





* * *





uḷḷataic coṉṉāl noḷḷaikkaṇṇikku nōppāḷam.

If the truth be told, the blind woman will be greatly afflicted.





* * *





uḷḷatai ellām koṭuttu noḷḷaikkaṇṇiyaik koṇṭāṉām.

It is said that he took to wife a blind girl having given away all he had.





* * *





uḷḷavaṉ piḷḷai uppōṭu uṇṇum, illātavaṉ piḷḷai carkkaraiyōṭu uṇṇum.

The child of the wealthy takes salt with his food, the child of the poor, sugar.





* * *





uḷḷakkaruttu vaḷḷalukkut teriyum.

Our inmost thoughts are known to God.





* * *





uḷḷataic coṉṉāl ellārukkum pakai.

When the truth is told, every one takes it ill.





* * *





uḷḷataiyum keṭuttāṉ koḷḷik kaṇṇaṉ.

The evil-eyed destroyed even what there was.





* * *





uḷḷataic coṉṉavaṉ ūrukkup pollātavaṉ.

He who speaks the truth is regarded as a wicked person by the country.





* * *





uḷḷāḷum kaḷḷāḷum kūṭṭamā?

Is association between an inmate and a thief to be tolerated?





* * *





uḷḷiruntārkkut teriyum uḷvaruttam.

Internal distress is known to the inmates.





* * *





uḷḷūr marumakaṉum uḻukiṟa kaṭāvum cari.

A son-in-law of the same village and a ploughing buffalo are overwrought.





* * *





uḷḷē koṭṭiṉa tēṉē oru mantiram collukiṟēṉ kēḷē.

O thou scorpion thou hast thrust thy sting into me, listen, I will utter an incantation.





* * *





uḷḷē pakaiyum utaṭṭilē uṟavumā?

What! is it enmity at heart and friendship on the lips?





* * *





uḷḷē vayiṟeriya utaṭu paḻam coriya.

Bowels burning within, lips shedding ripe fruit.





* * *





uṟavilē nañcu kalakkiṟatā?

What! is poison to be mixed with friendship?





* * *





uṟavupōla iruntu kuḷavipōlak koṭṭukiṟatā?

What! to feign friendship and to sting as a wasp?





* * *





uṟavu uṟavutāṉ paṟiyilē kai vaikkātē.

No doubt about friendship, but do not put your hand into my basket.





* * *





uṟavukkum pakaikkum poruḷē tuṇai.

Wealth may aid both in friendship and enmity.





* * *





uṟavukku oṉpatu paṭi ūrukkup pattup paṭi.

Nine measures for your friends and ten for the country.





* * *





uṟi aṟa mūḷināykku vēṭṭai.

The snapping of the cord of the swinging-tray is like a hunting excursion to the cropped dog.





* * *





uṟiyilē tayir irukka ūr eṅkum pōṉāṟpōl.

As if one sought curds throughout the village when a supply was on his own tray.





* * *





uṟiyilē veṇṇey irukka neykku alaivāṉēṉ?

Why go about in quest of ghee when you have butter on your own tray?





* * *





uṟiyiṟ paṇam pōyt teruviṟ caṇṭaiyai iḻukkiṟatu.

The money on the swinging-tray goes out and picks a quarrel in the public road.

Money is not infrequently put on a swinging tray in a cup or small basket.





* * *





uṟutiyāṉa kāriyam orupōtum keṭātu.

An affair firmly settled will never fail.





* * *





uṟutīṅkukku utavātavaṉ uṟṟavaṉā?

Is he a friend who helps not in adversity?





* * *





uṟcākam ceytāl maccait tāṇṭuvāṉ.

If encouraged, he will jump over the house top.





* * *





uṟṟa cinēkitaṉ uyirkku amirtam.

A true friend is the nectar éf life.





* * *





uṟṟatu coṉṉāl aṟṟatu poruntum.

When the truth is told, a breach between friends may be healed.





* * *





uṟṟatu colla ūrum alla, nallatu colla nāṭum alla.

This is not the village in which a man may speak the truth, nor the country in which, to give advice.





* * *





uṟṟār tiṉṟāl puṟṟāy viḷaiyum, ūrār tiṉṟāl pērāy viḷaiyum.

If friends consume one’s grain an ant hole, if strangers consume it, fame.





* * *





uṟṟārukku oṉṟum koṭāṉ, namaṉārukku nālum koṭuppāṉ.

He will give nothing to friends, but to Yama the regent of the dead he will give even four i.e., all.





* * *





uṟṟār utavuvārō aṉṉiyar utavuvārō?

Will friends help or will strangers?





* * *





uṉakkum pēpē uṉ appaṉukkum pēpē.

Pè, pè to you, and pè pè to your father also.

The following story is told about this proverb:—a debtor had two creditors. One of them said if you will pay me I will suggest an expedient for getting rid of the other creditor. He then instructed him when again applied to for payment, to feign madness, and to say Pé, Pé a demon, a demon. The creditor not satisfied had him summoned before a Magistrate where he did as before. The Magistrate thinking the debtor mad, decided that he was not amenable to a court of justice.

The other creditor then went to the debtor expecting to be paid according to promise. To his surprise the man said Pé, Pé, whereupon he said, do you deal with me also in this way, when, the debtor replied as the proverb stands.





* * *





uṉakku yāṉ apayam eṉakku nī upayam.

I am under your protection, you are my patron.





* * *





uṉ camarttilē kuṇṭu pāyātu.

Bullets will not fly by your strength.





* * *





uṉ collilē uppum illai puḷiyum illai.

In your words there is neither salt nor acid.





* * *





uṉ muṟṟukkut tirukku ellām eṉ uṭuppukkuḷḷē.

Your petulance and fraud are all in my dress.





* * *





uṉṉai vañcittavaṉai orupōtum nampātē.

Never trust one who has deceived you.





* * *





uṉṉaik koṭuppēṉō ceṉṉaik kūṉi, nī cumai cumantallō kūṉippōṉāy.

O thou hunch-backed woman of Madras, how can I give thee up, hast thou not become crooked by bearing burdens?





* * *





ū.



ūcal āṭiyum taṉ nilaiyil niṟkum.

Though the swing oscillates, it will resume its proper centre.





* * *





ūci oru muḻattuṇiyaiyāvatu koṭukkum uṟṟār eṉṉa koṭuppār.

A needle will give a cubit of cloth, what will your friends give?





* * *





ūci koḷḷap pōyt tulām kaṇakkup pārkkiṟatā?

When going to buy a needle, is the weight to be regarded?





* * *





ūci kōkkiṟataṟku ūril tuḷāvāram ēṉ?

Why such a stir in the village about threading a needle?





* * *





ūcikkuk kaḷḷaṉ uṭaṉē iruppāṉ.

Where there is a needle there will be a thief.





* * *





ūcikku ūci etirēṟip pāyumā?

Can one needle penetrate another when brought point to point?





* * *





ūci poṉṉāṉāl eṉṉa peṟum?

Though made of gold what will a needle fetch?





* * *





ūci pōkiṟatu kaṇakkup pārppāṉ pūcaṉikkāy pōkiṟatu teriyātu.

He notes the loss of needles, but not that of pumpkins.





* * *





ūcipōla miṭaṟum tāḻipōla vayiṟum.

A throat like a needle, a belly like a caldron.





* * *





ūci maliveṉṟu cīmaikkup pōkalāmā?

May one go to a foreign country to buy needles because they are there cheap?





* * *





ūci muṉaiyil tavam ceytālum uḷḷatutāṉ kiṭaikkum!

Though penance be performed by standing on the point of a needle, that which is predestined only will be obtained.





* * *





ūciyiṉ kātil oṭṭakam nuḻaiyumā?

Can a camel pass through the eye of a needle?





* * *





ūciyai ūcikkāntam iḻukkum, uttamaṉaic ciṉēkam iḻukkum.

A magnet attracts a needle, friendship, the good.





* * *





ūṇaṉ karumam iḻantāṉ, uluttavaṉ peyar iḻantāṉ.

The glutton failed in his object, and the miser lost his fame.





* * *





ūṇiṉāl uṟavu pūṇiṉāl aḻaku.

Friendship is promoted by entertainments, and beauty is set off by ornaments.





* * *





ūṇiṉāl putti pūṇiṉāl cāti.

Good sense is indicated by food, and rank by ornaments.





* * *





ūṇukku munta vēṇṭum kōḷukkup pintavēṇṭum.

Be first at a feast, and the last to slander.





* * *





ūṇum illai uṟakkamum illai.

Neither food nor sleep.





* * *





ūṇ aṟṟapōtē uṟavum aṟṟuppōm.

When entertainment is discontinued, friendship ceases.





* * *





ūṇpākku oḻiya vīṇpākku āṉatu.

Betel is not good except after food.





* * *





ūta aṟintavaṉ vāti uppu aṟintavaṉ yōki.

He is an alchemist who knows how to infuse metals, and he is a Yogi who knows the quality of salt.





* * *





ūtārikkup poṉṉum turumpu.

Even gold is a mere rush to the spendthrift.





* * *





ūtiṉāṟ pōm uṟiñciṉāl varum.

If blown it will fly off, if sucked it will go in.





* * *





ūtukiṟa caṅku ūtiṉāl viṭikiṟapōtu viṭikiṟatu.

If you blow the conch, the day will break as it is wont.





* * *





ūttaivāyaṉ tēṭa nāṟṟavāyaṉ tiṉṉa.

Acquired by the filthy mouthed and consumed by the fetid mouthed.





* * *





ūttai tiraṇṭu accāṇi ākumā?

Will the dirt on one’s body when collected form a linch-pin?





* * *





ūttaivāyaṉ tēṭak karppūravāyaṉ tiṉṉa.

Accumulated by the foul mouthed and consumed by a mouth fragrant with camphor.





* * *





ūttai pōkak kuḷittavaṉum illai pacipōkat tiṉṟavaṉum illai.

No one performs ablutions so as not to repeat them, nor does any one eat so as not again to suffer hunger.





* * *





ūmai ūraik keṭukkum āmai kiṇaṟṟaik keṭukkum.

The dumb destroys a village, a tortoise or turtle destroys a well.





* * *





ūmai ūraik keṭukkum peruccāḷi vīṭṭaik keṭukkum.

A community is injured by the dumb, and a house by bandycootes.





* * *





ūmai kaṇṭa kaṉāppōl irukkiṟatu.

It is like the dream of the dumb.

He is unable to relate his dream and therefore cannot obtain its interpretation.





* * *





ūmaikku uḷaṟuvāyaṉ uṟpātapiṇṭam.

The dumb regards a babbler as a wonder.





* * *





ūmaiyaṉ pēccu paḻakiṉa pērukkut teriyum.

The speech—gesticulation—of the dumb is known to those accustomed to it.





* * *





ūmaiyāyiruntāl ceviṭum uṇṭu.

If one is dumb he is deaf also.





* * *





ūmaiyum ūmaiyum mūkkaic coṟintāṟpōla.

As the dumb on meeting the dumb scratch their noses.

If a person on meeting a dumb man scratches his own nose the dumb man becomes very angry.





* * *





ūmaiyum alla ceviṭum alla.

Neither dumb nor deaf.





* * *





ūrāraip pakaittu uyirōṭu iruntavar illai.

No one at enmity with the whole community ever preserved his life.





* * *





ūrār kaṇakku uṭaiyavaṉ piṭariyilē.

The liabilities of a village are on the neck of its chief.





* * *





ūrār uṭaimaikkup pēyāyp paṟakkiṟāṉ.

He flies on the property of others like a demon.





* * *





ūrār erumai pāl kaṟakkiṟatu nīyum ūṭṭu nāṉum uṇṇukiṟēṉ.

The buffalo of the village is in milk, you suck and I will also suck.





* * *





ūrilē kalyāṇam mārpilē cantaṉam.

Marriage ceremonies in the village and sandal paste on the breast.





* * *





ūril oruvaṉē tōḻaṉ ārum aṟṟatē tāram.

Have one only of the community as a friend, and a woman without ties as a wife.





* * *





ūril eḷiyārai vaṇṇāṉ aṟivāṉ cātippoṉ pūṇṭārait taṭṭāṉ aṟivāṉ.

The washerman knows the poor of a village, the goldsmith knows whose ornaments are made of fine gold.





* * *





ūrukkellām oru vaḻi uṉakku oru vaḻiyā?

While all the people of the village have one road in common, do you expect one for yourself?





* * *





ūrukkup pālvārttu uṇṇukiṟāyā uṭampukkup pālvārttu uṇṇukiṟāyā?

Do you take milk on account of the community or to promote your own health?





* * *





ūrukkellām cāstiram collukiṟa palli kūḻpāṉaiyil viḻuntāṟpōla.

As if a lizard, the oracle of the whole village, should fall into a pot of gruel.

Spoken reproachfully, when a person remarkable either by position or profession, has done something injurious to his reputation.

The lizard—palli a newt—here referred to, is seen on the walls of houses in India and Ceylon, and its chirp may be frequently heard. Its voice is never disregarded since it may bear a message of great consequence to the family or individual concerned. A small book called the Shaster of the Lizard contains all needed information regarding its chirping. On hearing it the facts to be observed are the point of the compass, the hour of the day &c. These being known the party has recourse to his formula and ascertains whether the omen is auspicious or not. The premonitions of this little domestic monitor derive their significance from the character of the preternatural regent who may at the time preside over the region indicated by the chirp. Thus it appears that the shaster of the domestic lizard is based on the dogmas of Astrology.





* * *





ūrukku ākātavaṉ vīṭṭukkum ākāṉ.

He who is useless abroad is useless at home.





* * *





ūrukku ākāta piḷḷai tāykku ākumā?

Is a child injurious to the community, of any use to its mother?





* * *





ūrukku ōmal vīṭṭukku vayiṟṟericcal.

Fame abroad and famine at home.





* * *





ūrukku iḷaittavaṉ piḷḷaiyār kōvil āṇṭi

He who was too feeble for the service of the community became a devotee in the temple of Genèsha.

In Sanskrit a similar proverb exists; “The dancing girl being old has become a devotee.” It is a case not of breaking away from sinful habits, but the power of sinning is gone.





* * *





ūruṭaṉ pakaikkil vēruṭaṉ keṭum.

If one is hated by the whole community he will be rooted out.





* * *





ūreṅkum pēr vīṭu paṭṭiṉi.

Fame throughout the country, at home starvation.





* * *





ūrellām vāḻkiṟateṉṟu vīṭellām puraṇṭu aḻutāl varumā?

Can you gain anything by wallowing and weeping because the village is prosperous?





* * *





ūrellām cuṟṟi eṉakku eṉṉa putti eṉkiṟāṉ.

Having wandered all over the country he says, what advice do you give.





* * *





ūrellām uṟṟār antipaṭṭāl poti cantiyilē.

He has friends in every part of the village, yet his bundle is left in the public road after sun-set.





* * *





ūreṉṟu iruntāl paṟaiccēriyum irukkum.

Wherever there is a village there is a Parachéri.

A Parachery is a hamlet of Pariahs—non caste Natives. Pariahs are employed in the lowest offices, as beaters of the tomtom or Native drum on festive and mournful occasions. They are employed to publish proclamations &c, as a bell-man in Europe.





* * *





ūrai uḻakkāl aḷakkiṟāḷ nāṭṭai nāḻiyāl aḷakkiṟāḷ.

She measures the village with an ulak and the province with a nàli.

The uḻakku is a quarter of a paṭi. uḻakkāḻākku three eighths of a measure. A tāḻi is a measure, of which there are eight in a marakkāl, marcàl.





* * *





ūraip pārkkaccoṉṉāl paṟaiccēriyaip pārkkiṟāṉ.

When told to visit the village, he goes to the Pariah quarter.





* * *





ūraip piṭitta caṉi piḷḷaiyāraiyum piṭittatu.

Saturn that seized the village seized also Ganesha.

One thing happens to all.





* * *





ūrai vaḷaittālum uṟṟa tuṇai illai, nāṭṭai vaḷaittālum nalla tuṇai illai.

Though one goes round the village he finds no help, though he goes round the country no real aid can be got.





* * *





ūrōṭa okka ōṭu nāṭōṭa naṭu ōṭu.

If the people of the village flee, join them, if the whole country flee, flee in their midst.





* * *





ūrōccam vīṭu paṭṭiṉi.

Distinguished abroad, starving at home.





* * *





ūr āḷukiṟavaṉ peṇṭu piṭittāl āruṭaṉ colli muṟaiyiṭukiṟatu?

When the ruler of a village forcibly takes away another man’s wife, to whom is he to make his complaint!





* * *





ūr irukkiṟatu ōṭu irukkiṟatu.

The village still exists and also the alms-dishes.





* * *





ūr iraṇṭupaṭṭāl kūttāṭikkuk koṇṭāṭṭam.

If the village be divided into two parties, it will be advantageous to the commedians.





* * *





ūr iḷappam vaṇṇāṉukkut teriyum.

That which is bad or exceptionable in a village is known to its washerman.

The clothes of all pass through his hands, he has therefore the opportunity of inferring much from their condition.





* * *





ūr uṇṭu piccaikku, kuḷam uṇṭu taṇṇīrukku.

There is a village for alms, and a tank for water.





* * *





ūr ōrattiṟ kollai uḻutavaṉukkup payir illai.

He that cultivates a field on the outskirts of a village loses its produce.





* * *





ūr kūṭic cekkut taḷḷiṉāṟpōla.

As if a whole village united to put an oil-press in motion.





* * *





ūrkkuruvimēl rāmapāṇam toṭukkiṟatā?

What, discharge Ráma's arrow at a sparrow?

Why lavish means so precious on objects so mean?





* * *





ūrkkōḻiyum nāṭṭukkōḻiyum kūṭi uraliṟ puḻuṅkal aricikkuc cētam.

When the poultry of the village and the poultry of the country at large come together, the rice in the mortar is spoiled.





* * *





ūr naṣṭam ūrilē tēr naṣṭam teruvilē.

Loss occurring in a village affects the community, injury occasioned by a temple car is apparent in the street.





* * *





ūr nallatō vāy nallatō?

Is the village good or your description?





* * *





ūrppiḷḷaiyai muttamiṭṭāl, utaṭṭukkuk kēṭu.

To kiss the child of another is bad for the lips.





* * *





ūrvāyaip paṭaliṭṭu mūṭalāmā?

Is it possible to shut the mouth of a village by a hurdle?





* * *





ūr vāḻntāl oṭṭuppiccaikkum vaḻi irukkum.

If the village be prosperous—there. will be an opening for alms-dishes.





* * *





ūḻikkāyccal atikam āṉāl cūṉiyakkāraṉ koḷḷai.

When epidemic fever prevails, sorcerers abound.





* * *





ūḻi pēriṉum ūkkamatu kaiviṭēl.

Though the world come to an end do not remit your efforts.





* * *





ūḻiṟ peruvali oṉṟu uṇṭō?

Is there any power greater than destiny?





* * *





ūḻum uṟcākamum ottukkoḷḷavēṇṭum.

Destiny and exertion must go together.





* * *





ūṉṟak koṭutta taṭi ucciyai uṭaikkiṟatu.

The staff I gave for his support breaks the crown of my head.





* * *





e.



eṅkaḷāl oṉṟum illai ellām uṅkaḷ karmam.

Nothing lies in our might, all must be done by you.





* * *





eṅkum maṭamāy irukkiṟatu irukkattāṉ iṭam illai.

Choultries everywhere, but no place of accommodation.





* * *





eṅkē aṭittālum nāy kālait tūkkum.

No matter where hit, a dog when struck, lifts up his leg.





* * *





eṅkē pukai uṇṭō aṅkē neruppu uṇṭu.

Wherever there is smoke there is fire.





* * *





eccilait tiṉṟu ēppam iṭṭāṟpōla.

Like belching after eating leavings.





* * *





eccilait tiṉṟu paci tīrumā?

Can hunger be appeased by eating leavings?





* * *





ecciṟkallaikku maṇṇāṅkaṭṭi ātaravu maṇṇāṅkaṭṭikku ecciṟkallai ātaravu.

A clod is the support of the leaf-plate, and the leaf-plate is the support of the clod.





* * *





eccil irakka aṭikkum, paṟṟu paṟakka aṭikkum.

Spitting about will drive one to beggary, and ceremonial impurity will scatter a family.





* * *





ecciṟkaiyāl kākkai ōṭṭamāṭṭāṉ.

He will not scare away a crow with an unwashen hand.





* * *





eccil eṭukkac coṉṉārkaḷā ettaṉaipēr cāppiṭṭateṉṟu kaṇakkup pārkkac coṉṉārkaḷā?

Did they order the leavings to be removed, or an account to be taken of the number who had eaten?





* * *





eccil tiṉṟālum vayiṟu niṟaiyat tiṉṉa vēṇṭum, ēccuk kēṭṭālum poḻutu viṭikiṟamaṭṭum kēṭka vēṇṭum.

If you eat offal eat heartily, if you listen to abusive language, listen till day-break.





* * *





eccivilaikku nāy aṭittukkoṇṭu niṟkiṟatupōla.

As dogs impatiently wait for leaf-plates.





* * *





eccilaik kaḻuvi uṉ cuttattilē vār.

Wash off the refuse and pour the water on your clean person. : Spoken in derision of affected cleanliness.





* * *





eṭukkamuṉṉam kaḻutai iṭuppu oṭintu viḻuntatām.

It is said that the ass fell down with a broken hip as soon as it was loaded.





* * *





eṭukkap pōṉa cīmāṭṭi iṭuppu oṭintu viḻuntāḷām.

It is said that the wealthy matron, whom one had gone to take ‘'in marriage, fell down, her waist having ‘'broken.





* * *





eṭuttālum paṅkāruppeṭṭiyai eṭukkavēṇṭum iruntālum ciṅkārakkaḻuvil irukkavēṇṭum.

If you steal, take away a golden casket; if you are impaled, endure the punishment on an ornamented stake.





* * *





eṭutta cumai cumantallō iṟakkavēṇṭum?

A burden must be first carried, must it not, before it is put down?





* * *





eṭuttu āḷāta poruḷ utavātu.

Things unused, spoil.





* * *





eṭutta aṭi maṭaṅkumā?

Will the lifted foot be drawn back?





* * *





eṭuttuviṭṭa erutu ettaṉai nāḷ uḻum?

How long can an ox plough that requires to be lifted up?





* * *





eṭuppatu piccai ēṟuvatu pallakku.

His occupation is begging, his conveyance a palanquin.





* * *





eṭuppārum piṭippārum uṇṭāṉāl iḷaippum tavippum uṇṭu.

Where many are in attendance to lift up and support, there will be weariness and fainting.





* * *





eṭuppār kaik kuḻantai.

A baby in the arms.





* * *





eṭuppār maḻuvait taṭuppār puliyaik koṭuppār arumai.

There are who take up the battle axe, and there are who stop tigers, but givers ate scarce.





* * *





eṭuppuṇṭa kalappai iruntu uḻumā?

Will a worn out plough last long?





* * *





eṭṭāp paḻattaip pārttuk koṭṭāvi viṭṭatupōla.

As one gazed and gaped at a fruit beyond his reach.





* * *





eṭṭāp pū tēvarkku eṭṭum pū taṅkaḷukku.

Flowers beyond reach are sacred to God, but those within reach are for themselves.





* * *





eṭṭik kuṭṭi iṟaṅkik kālaip piṭittukkoḷḷukiṟatā?

What! is it to reach up and cuff, and cower and cling to the feet?





* * *





eṭṭi paḻuttu eṉṉa, īyātār vāḻntu eṉṉa?

What if the fruit of the etti tree (strychnos nux vomica) ripens; of what use is the prosperity of the niggardly?





* * *





eṭṭimaramāṉālum pacceṉṟu irukkavēṇṭum.

Though a poisonous tree, it should be green.





* * *





eṭṭimaramāṉālum vaitta marattai veṭṭātē.

Cut not down the tree you planted though it is the (strychnos nux vomica.)

I have observed among many natives a remarkable disinclination to cut down trees: though not rational creatures they are said to have one sense .





* * *





eṭṭikkup pālvārttu vaḷarttālum tittippu uṇṭākumā?

Although you may nourish an etti tree by pouring milk at its root, will it become sweet?





* * *





eṭṭiyilē kaṭṭumāmpaḻam uṇṭāmō?

Will an etti tree bear graft-mangoes?





* * *





eṭṭiyuṭaṉē cērnta ilavum tīppaṭum.

Even the silk-cotton tree growing by the etti, will also be consumed by fire.

Evil association brings destruction.





* * *





eṭṭiṉāl kuṭumiyaip piṭikkiṟatu. eṭṭāviṭṭāl kālaippiṭikkiṟatu.

If it can be reached, seizing by the kudumi knot of hair, if not, clinging to the feet.





* * *





eṭṭi eṭṭip pārttuk kuṭṭiccuvarilē muṭṭikkoḷḷalāmā?

Can you strike your head against a dwarf wall when peeping on tiptoe?





* * *





eṭṭi eṭṭip pārppārum ēṇi vaittup pārppārum kuṭṭiccuvarālē kuṉintu niṉṟu pārppārum uṇṭu.

There are who peep on tiptoe, there are who peep on a ladder, there are those who peep over a dwarf wall crouching.





* * *





eṭṭimaramāṉālum vaittavarkkup pakṣam.

Though an etti tree, he who planted it will like it.





* * *





eṭṭippaḻattai iccikkiṟatā?

What, is an etti fruit to be desired?





* * *





eṭṭuk kiḻavarum oru moṭṭaikkiḻaviyaik kaṭṭikkoṇṭārkaḷ.

Eight old men conjointly took to wife a bald-headed old woman.





* * *





eṭṭuk kuñcu aṭittālum caṭṭikkaṟi ākātu.

Eight chickens are not sufficient for a chatty of curry.





* * *





eṭṭu varuṣattu erumaikkaṭā ērikkup pōka vaḻi tēṭukiṉṟatu.

A buffalo eight years old is at a loss to find its way to the tank.





* * *





eṇcāṇ uṭampum oru cāṇāṉēṉ.

My eight-span body is reduced to one span.





* * *





eṇcāṇ uṭampilē eḷḷattaṉai nāṇam illai.

There is not a grain of modesty in ‘'his'’ eight-span body.





* * *





eṇcāṇ uṭampiṟkum ciracē piratāṉam.

The head is the chief part of the eight-span body.





* * *





eṇṇam ellām poy emaṉ ōlai mey.

All ‘'our'’ thoughts are vain, the ola of Yama is real. : Ola is the name of the Palmyra palm leaf. On this material, books, accounts, correspondence &c., are written with an iron pen. Several of the Tamil books I first read were written on ola. Yama, the regent of the dead, is graphically represented as stretching out his hand to deliver the order for departure.





* * *





eṇṇam ellām poy ēḷitam mey.

Expectations are all unreal, a despicable condition is a reality.





* * *





eṇṇaṟak kaṟṟu eḻuttaṟa vācittālum peṇ putti piṉ puttiyē.

No matter how skilled a woman may be in numbers and letters, her judgment will be second rate.

Many modern Hindus of the day (1873) though themselves earnest about University honors, evince but very little interest regarding the intellectual and moral culture of their daughters. The Rev K.M. Banerjee, a learned brahman of Calcutta, very recently gave a sad account of the disabilities to which native ladies in that city are subject.





* * *





eṇṇappaṭṭa kutirai ellām maṇṇaip pōṭṭukkoḷḷa, taṭṭuvāṇikkutirai vantu koḷḷukku aḻukiṟatām.

Whilst horses of the first blood lick the ground, the miserable tattoo is neighing for gram.





* * *





eṇṇam aṟṟa irācaṉ paṉṟivēṭṭai āṭiṉāṟpōl.

As an incautions king went to hunt wild hog.





* * *





eṇṇam ellām poy eḻutiya eḻuttu mey.

All imaginations may be false, that which is written is true, certain.





* * *





eṇ illātavar kaṇ illātavar, eḻuttu illātavar kaḻuttu illātavar.

Those who are ignorant of figures have no eyes, those who are ignorant of letters have no head.





* * *





eṇṇic ceykiṟavaṉ ceṭṭi eṇṇāmaṟ ceykiṟavaṉ maṭṭi.

He who acts after due consideration is a chitty ‘'a superior man, he who acts without consideration is a matti ‘'a fool.





* * *





eṇṇic ceyvatu ceṭṭu eṇṇāmaṟ ceyvatu vēḷāṇmai.

Trade requires forethought, agriculture requires none.





* * *





eṇṇiya eṇṇam eṉṉaṭi aṇṇā eṉṟu aḻaitta muṟai eṉṉaṭi?

What was our expectation, and what led you to call me elder brother?





* * *





eṇṇit tuṇivatukarumam, tuṇintapiṉ eṇṇuvatu iḻukku.

An action undertaken after due consideration may prove successful, consideration after action may end in disgrace.





* * *





eṇṇiya kuṭikku oru miṉṉiya muṭi.

A brilliant crown for an illustrious family.





* * *





eṇṇey muntukiṟatō tiri muntukiṟatō ?

It is questionable which will be first consumed, the oil or the wick.





* * *





eṇṇey pōka muḻukiṉālum eḻuttup pōkat tēyppār uṇṭā ?

Although oil may be washed off, can the writing of Brahma be rubbed off?





* * *





eṇṇeyc celavoḻiya piḷḷai piḻaittapāṭu illai.

As regards the child's recovery, nothing has resulted but an expenditure of oil.





* * *





eṇṇeyk kuṭattaic cuṟṟum eṟumpupōla.

Like ants round a pot of oil.





* * *





eṇṇeyp piḷḷaiyō vaṇṇap piḷḷaiyō ?

Is the beauty attributable to oil, or is it real?





* * *





eṇ mikuttavaṉē tiṇ mikuttavaṉē.

He who excels in figures excels in strength.





* * *





etākutala pēciṉāl akappaic cūṉiyam vaippēṉ.

If you speak a word, I will place before you an empty ladle.





* * *





etārttavāti veku caṉa virōti.

He who is truthful may be the enemy of many.





* * *





etiri iḷaippamāṉāl kōpam caṇṭappiracaṇṭam.

If the opposite party be inferior in position, the anger of his enemy will rage like a tempest.





* * *





etirikku iḷakkāramāyc collukiṟatā ?

What, is it to yield to an opponent?





* * *





etirtta vīṭu ēkāli vīṭu, aṭutta vīṭu ampaṭṭaṉ vīṭu.

The house opposite is the washerman’s, and my next door neighbour is a barber.





* * *





etirvīṭu ēkāli vīṭu, pakkattu vīṭu paṇiceypavaṉ vīṭu, aṭutta vīṭu ampaṭṭaṉ vīṭu.

The opposite house is the washerman’s, the adjoining house is the goldsmith’s, and the next to mine is that of the barber.





* * *





etirttavaṉ ēḻai eṉṟāl kōpam caṇṭāḷam.

When the opposed is poor, the anger of his opponent ends in cruelty.





* * *





etu eppaṭip pōṉālum taṉ kāriyam taṉakku.

No matter how any thing may go, each should mind his own business.





* * *





ettaṉaitāṉ tulakkiṉālum pittaḷai nāṟṟam pōkumā ?

No matter how frequently it may be polished, the bad odour of brass will not leave it.





* * *





ettaṉai puṭam iṭṭālum irumpu pacumpoṉ ākātu.

Though iron may be heated never so much, it will not become gold.





* * *





ettaṉai ēḻai āṉālum elumiccaṅkāy attaṉai poṉ illāmaṟpōmā ?

However poor one may be, will he not possess gold, at least of the value of a lemon?





* * *





ettāṟ piḻaikkalām ottāṟ piḻaikkalām.

How may we subsist? if united we may subsist.





* * *





ettilē piḷḷai peṟṟu iravalilē tālaṭṭukiṟatu.

Bringing forth a child without cost, and rocking it in a borrowed cradle.





* * *





ettēca kālamum vaṟṟāp peruñ camuttiram.

An ocean that knows no ebb anywhere at any time.





* * *





enta āyutamum tīṭṭattīṭṭak kūrum.

The more an instrument is whetted, the sharper it becomes.





* * *





enta ilai utirntālum īccam ilai utirātu.

Though the leaves of other trees may fall off, those of the date-palm will not.





* * *





entap puṟṟilē entap pāmpu irukkiṟatō teriyātu.

It is not known what species of snake may be found in any particular hole.





* * *





enta vākkup poyttālum cantavākkup poyyātu.

Whatever else may fail the words, marks, of low breeding will not fail.





* * *





ennēramum avaṉpēril kaṇṇāy irukkiṟāṉ.

His eye is always upon her.





* * *





emaṉukku vaḻikāṭṭuvāṉ.

He can pilot Yama, the regent of the dead.





* * *





emaṉ piḷḷaiyaip pēy piṭikkumā ?

Will a demon seize the child of Yama, the regent of the dead?





* * *





eykiṟavaṉ eytāl ampu eṉṉa ceyyum ?

What can the arrow do if discharged by a skilful archer?





* * *





eykiṟavaṉ irukka ampai nōvāṉēṉ ?

Why blame the arrow, letting the archer go free?





* * *





erikiṟa koḷḷiyai ēṟat taḷḷiṉatupōla.

As a burning firebrand was pushed into the hearth.





* * *





erikiṟa koḷḷiyil entak koḷḷi nalla koḷḷi ?

Which of the burning firebrands is the best?





* * *





erikiṟataip piṭuṅkiṉāl kotikkiṟatu aṭaṅkum.

If the burning fuel be removed, the bubbling will cease.





* * *





erikiṟa neruppai eṇṇey viṭṭu avikkalāmā ?

May a burning fire be extinguished by pouring oil on it?





* * *





erikiṟa neruppilē ney viṭṭatupōla.

As if ghee were poured on a flaming fire.





* * *





erikiṟa vīṭṭai avikkak kiṇaṟu veṭṭa nāḷ pārttatu pōla.

Like seeking an auspicious day, to dig a well in order to quench the flames of a house on fire.





* * *





erikiṟa vīṭṭil piṭuṅkukiṟatu lāpam.

Whatever is abstracted from a burning house is a gain.





* * *





erutiṉ puṇṇiṟkuc cāmpal maruntu.

Ashes are medicine for the sores of a bull.





* * *





erutukkum taṉ puṇ aḻaṟci kākkaikkum taṉ paci aḻaṟci.

The buffalo suffers from the smarting of its sores, and the crow that picks them suffers from the smarting of hunger.





* * *





erutu koḻuttāl toḻuvattil irātu, paṟaiyaṉ koḻuttāl pāyil irāṉ.

If an ox grow fat, it will not remain in a stall; if a Pariah becomes rich, he will not sit on a mat.





* * *





erutu nōyai niṉaikkum, kākkai paciyai niṉaikkum.

The ox feels the pain, the crow its hunger.





* * *





erutu nōy kākkaikkut teriyumā ?

Is a crow aware what pain it occasions when picking the sore of an ox?





* * *





erutu īṉṟatu eṉṟāl tōḻattilē kaṭṭu eṉkiṟatu.

If it be reported, that the bull has a calf, he will say, O, tie it up in the stall.





* * *





erutu keṭṭārkkum eṭṭē kaṭukkāy, iḷampiḷḷaittāyccikkum eṭṭē kaṭukkāy.

Giving eight gall nuts to him who has lost a bull, and eight to a woman in the first stage of pregnancy.





* * *





erutu vāṅkummuṉṉē puṉceykku uḻu eṉkiṟāy.

You bid me plough the dry land before the ox is bought.





* * *





erutu niṉaitta iṭattilē tōḻam kaṭṭukiṟā ?

Is its shed put up where the bull may think fit?





* * *





erutu kōpaṅkoṇṭu paratēcam pōṉatupōla.

As a bull went away in a rage to a foreign country.





* * *





erutu maṟaiviṟ pullut tiṉkiṟāyā ?

Dost thou eat grass screened by a bull?





* * *





erutukku nōy vantāl koṭṭāyaic cuṭukiṟatā ?

Is its shed to be burnt dawn because the ox is sick?





* * *





erutum vaṇṭiyum ottāl mēṭu paḷḷam ētu ?

What matters the ruggedness of the road if the bullocks and bandy-a country cart-hold together?





* * *





erumaikkaṭā eṉṟālum kuḻantaikkup pāl oru pīr illaiyā eṉkiṟāy.

Though it is a male buffalo, you ask me if there be not a drop of milk for the child.





* * *





erumai vāṅkumuṉ neyvilai kūṟukiṟāy.

You publish the price of ghee before buying a buffalo.





* * *





erumaik kompu naṉaikiṟataṟkumuṉṉē eḻupatu taram maḻai varukiṟatu.

It will rain seventy times before a buffalo’s horns will be wet.





* * *





erumaik kōmayam ekkiyattiṟku ākumā ?

Will the dung of the buffalo serve for sacrificial purposes?





* * *





erumai māṭṭiṉmēl maḻai peytatupōla.

As it rained on a buffalo.





* * *





erumai māṭṭiṉmēl ettaṉai cūṭu iruntālum teriyātu, pacumāṭṭil oru cūṭu iruntālum teriyum.

Brands on a buffalo however numerous will scarcely appear, but a single brand on a cow will be visible.





* * *





erumai iruntallō pāl kaṟakkavēṇṭum ?

We may milk may we not, provided there be a buffalo?





* * *





erumaiyilum veḷḷāṭu ēṟakkaṟakkumā ?

Will a goat yield more milk than a buffalo?





* * *





eruvukkup pōṉavaṉ iḷaiyāḷaik kaippiṭittāṟpōla.

As he who went in search of manure, seized the hand of a maiden.





* * *





eruvukkup pōṉavaṉ elumiccampaḻam eṭuttatupōla.

As he who went to pick up cow-dung, gathered lemons.





* * *





eli ammaṇattōṭē pōkiṉṟatu eṉkiṟāṉ.

He says that the rat goes naked.





* * *





eli aḻutāl pūṉai viṭumā ?

Will the cat leave its hold on the crying of the rat?





* * *





eli aḻutu pulampiṉālum pūṉai piṭittatu viṭātu.

Although the rat may cry and lament, the cat will not relinquish its hold.





* * *





eli aṟukkum tūkkamāṭṭātu.

The rat nibbles the grain, but it does not carry off the basket.





* * *





eli irukkiṟa iṭattil pāmpu irukkum.

Where there is a rat, there will also be a snake.





* * *





elikkup payantu vīṭṭaic cuṭṭatupōla.

Like burning down the house for fear of rats.





* * *





elikkup pirāṇavastai pūṉaikkuk koṇṭāṭṭam.

The death-struggle of a rat is the sport of a cat.





* * *





elikku aṉukūlam pāmpu piṭāraṉukku añcutal, eḷiyārkku aṉukūlam valiyār irācaṉukku añcutal.

The snake’s fear of him who catches it, is favourable to the rat; when the strong fear the king, the weak are benefited.





* * *





elikku maṇiyam cuvarai aṟukkiṟatutāṉ.

The business of the rat is to burrow in the wall.





* * *





eli talaiyilē kōṭāli viḻuntatupōla.

As an axe fell on the head of the rat.





* * *





eli talaiyiṟ kōpuram iṭintu viḻuntāṟpōla.

As if the tower of the temple should fall on the head of a rat.





* * *





eli pūṉaiyai vellumā?

Can a rat overcome a cat?





* * *





eli pūṉaikkuc calāmpaṇṇuvatupōla.

As a rat makes salaam to a cat.





* * *





elippiḻukkai iṟappil iruntu eṉṉa, varappil iruntu eṉṉa?

What matters it whether the droppings of a rat are in the inside of a sloping roof, or on the ridge of a cornfield?





* * *





eliyum pūṉaiyumpōla irukkiṟatu.

Living like a rat and a cat.





* * *





eliyum pūṉaiyum iṇaintu viḷaiyāṭiṉatupōla.

As the rat and the cat united in sport.





* * *





eliyaik kaṇṭu pūṉai ēkkam aṭaiyumā?

Will the cat be alarmed at the sight of a rat?





* * *





eliyaik kaṇṭu pūṉai ēṅki ēṅkik kiṭakkumō?

Will a cat be greatly alarmed at the sight of a rat?





* * *





elivaḷai āṉālum taṉivaḷai vēṇṭum.

A separate hole is to be preferred though it be a rat-hole.

One’s own cot is desirable however humble.





* * *





eli vīṭu kaṭṭap pāmpu kuṭikoḷḷum.

A rat makes the hole, a snake inhabits it.





* * *





eli vēṭṭaikkut tavil aṭippārā?

Do they beat a tomtom when hunting rats?





* * *





elumiccaṅkāykkup puḷi ēṟṟukiṟatupōla.

Like infusing acidity into a lemon.





* * *





elumiccañceṭikku erup pōṭṭāṟpōla.

Like manuring a lemon tree.





* * *





elumpu kaṭikkiṟa nāykkup paruppuccōṟu ēṉ?

Why give pulse to a dog that gnaws bones?





* * *





elumpu kaṭikkiṟa nāy irumpu kaṭikkumā?

Can a dog that gnaws bones bite iron?





* * *





elumpu illā nā ellām pēcum.

A boneless tongue may say any thing.





* * *





ellām aṟintavaṉum illai, oṉṟum aṟiyātavaṉum illai.

No one knows everything, nor is any one ignorant of everything.





* * *





ellām irukkiṟatu peṭṭiyilē ilaikkaṟi kaṭaiyac caṭṭi illai.

Everything wanted is in the box, but there is no chatty in which to macerate the vegetable curry.





* * *





ellāt talaiyilum eṭṭu eḻuttu, eṉ talaiyilē pattu eḻuttu.

On all heads there are eight letters, on mine there are ten.





* * *





ellārukkum collumām palli, tāṉ pōyk kāṭippāṉaiyil viḻumām.

It is said that the lizard which uttered prognostications, will go and fall into the refuse pan.





* * *





ellā macakamum cāyantarattiṟṟāṉ varukiṟatu.

It is in the evening that all the mosquitos come.





* * *





ellārukkum caṉi turumpupōla, eṉakkuc caṉi malaipōla.

Saturn who is a mere straw to all other men, to me is as a mountain.





* * *





ellārum pākku ivaṉ oru tōppu.

All other men are areca-nuts, but this man is a grove of areca-trees.





* * *





ellārum taṭukkiṉkīḻ nuḻaintāl avaṉ kōlattiṉkīḻ nuḻaivāṉ.

If all can hide themselves under a small mat, he can conceal himself under the ornamental drawing on the floor.

The kōlam here mentioned is a floor ornament very commonly formed at the entrance of a native house: the form is circular, These ornaments are renewed every morning. They contain various devices. In some a temple car as delineated, in others trees, birds, flowers. They are generally striking because the combinations indicate considerable skill. It may be that they are connected with the prevalent ideas regarding the evil eye.





* * *





ellāt tāṭṭōṭṭum kullāvukkuḷḷē irukkiṟatu.

All the skill in steering centres in the out-rigger.

Out-rigger boats or canoes are common on the south western coast of Ceylon. They are very swift and most safe. In 1848 when at Colombo duty called me daily to a large ship at the time out in the offing anchored in seven fathoms of water. On the occasion under notice I went on board in an ordinary Cingalese out-rigger canoe. In the evening after having completed the berths, for my two daughters who were going home for education, I betook myself to the boat, and soon after we got clear of the ship the boat was struck in the stern, filled, and went down. When I emerged from the waves I found myself near one of the two brackets of the out-rigger. The canoe was submerged but could not sink lower than the ends of the brackets tied to her side. The out-rigger at the other end of the brackets was floating on the surface of the sea, the canoe being suspended by it like a pair of scales from a beam. Aided by one of those brackets, which I pressed to my bosom, and a Chubb's Palanquin box, I gained the rocks soon after sunset and escaped to land.





* * *





ellā ōṭṭum kullāvilē.

All our progress depends on the outrigger.





* * *





ellārum nallārā, kal ellām māṇikkamā?

Are all men good men, are all stones rubies?





* * *





ellā vēlaiyum ceyvāṉ, cettāl piḻaikkamāṭṭāṉ.

He can do all things, but when dead he cannot resuscitate himself.





* * *





ellōrum kappal ēṟiyāyiṟṟu iṉi ammaṉār poṟpaṭṭam kaṭṭappōkiṟār.

All have embarked, my uncle is about to receive a golden mark of distinction.

Spoken of aspirations beyond one’s merits.





* * *





ellārum ēṟi iḷaitta kutiraiyil cāstiriyār ēṟic caṟukki viḻuntār.

On mounting a jaded horse that all had ridden, the astrologer slipped and fell down.





* * *





ellām aṟintum, kaḻunīrppāṉaiyil kaiyiṭukiṟatā?

What, is one who knows all things, to put his hand into filthy water?





* * *





ellai pāḻpaṭṭālum kollaikkaṭamai viṭār.

Though the landmarks be destroyed, they will not remit the ground-rent.





* * *





evaṉ ākilum tāṉ cāka maruntu uṇpāṉā?

Will any one take medicine to poison himself?





* * *





eḻutāk kaṭaṉukku aḻutāl tīrumā?

Will crying avail for money lent of which no account has been kept?





* * *





eḻuti vaḻaṅkāṉ vāḻkkai kaḻutai puraṇṭa kaḷam.

The condition of the man who keeps no accounts is like the place in which an ass has rolled itself.





* * *





eḻutiya viti aḻutāl tīrumā?

Will destiny be averted by weeping?





* * *





eḻutuvatu arumai eḻutiṉataip paḻutaṟa vācippatu arumai.

Writing is important, reading correctly what is written, is more so.





* * *





eḻutukiṟatu peritu alla, iṉam aṟintu cērkkiṟatu peritu.

To be able to write is no great matter; to compose harmoniously is great.





* * *





eḻuttu aṟinta vaṇṇāṉ kuṟittu eṟintāṉ ōlai.

The washerman who had learnt his letters threw away his ola.





* * *





eḻuttu aṟinta maṉṉaṉ kiḻittu eṟintāṉ ōlaiyai.

The king who had learnt his letters tore and threw away his ola. : It would appear that the two preceding proverbs relate to persons who suppose that ability to read supercedes all records.





* * *





eḻuttukkup pālmāṟiṉa kaṇakkaṉum uṭukkaikkup pālmāṟiṉa tāciyum varuttam aṭaivār.

The curnum who neglects his accounts, and the dancing girl who is careless about her dress, will come to grief.





* * *





eḻuntiruppāṉ kāl illai.

He would rise, but he has no feet.





* * *





eḻupatu ceṉṟālum paṟai ēviṉāṟṟāṉ ceyyum.

Though seventy years old, a Pariah will do nothing unless he is prompted.





* * *





eḻupatu pēraik koṉṟa paṭunīli.

A cruel Níli-woman-who killed seventy persons.





* * *





ēḻaikkum pēḻaikkum kāṭukāḷ ammai.

The mother of Vairavi is the patroness of the poor and of boats.





* * *





eḷiyavaḷ piḷḷai āṉālum ceyyum caṭaṅku ceyyavēṇṭum.

Though the child of a poor woman, the accustomed rites must be performed.





* * *





eḷiyavaṉai aṭittup puḷiyaṅkāy paṟittāṟpōla.

Like beating a poor man, and plucking his tamarind fruit.





* * *





eḷiyavaṉukkup peṇṭāy irukkiṟatilum valiyavaṉukku aṭimai ākiṟatu nallatu.

It is better to be the slave of the wealthy, than the wife of the indigent.





* * *





eḷiyavaṉaik kaṇṭu vāyāl ēykkiṟāṉ.

Seeing that the man is poor, he deceives him by fair words.





* * *





eḷiyavaṉ peṇṭāṭṭi ellārukkum maittuṉicci.

The wife of a poor man is sister-in-law to all.





* * *





eḷiyārai valiyār aṭittāl valiyārait teyvam aṭikkum.

If the powerful oppress the weak, God will punish them in return.





* * *





eḷiyārai etir iṭṭukkoṇṭāl pirāṇhāṉi.

Inferiors if opposed become mortal enemies.





* * *





eḷ attaṉaiyai malai attaṉai ākkukiṟatu.

Magnifying a rape seed into a mountain.





* * *





eḷḷukkāy piḷanta vivakāram

A dispute as easy to decide as the splitting of a rape seed.





* * *





eḷḷukkāy piḷantāṟ pōlap pēcavēṇṭum.

One ought to speak as evenly as a rape-pod splits.





* * *





eḷḷukkut takka eṇṇey, eḷḷukkut takka piṇṇākku.

Oil is proportioned to the sessamum, refuse proportioned to the pressed seed.





* * *





eḷḷukkup puḷḷu varum ecciṟku eṟumpu varum.

Birds are attracted by rape seed, ants by leavings.





* * *





eḷḷukkuḷ eṇṇey pōla.

As oil in sessamum seed.





* * *





eḷḷukkuḷ eṇṇeypōl eṅkum niṟaintirukkum.

Everywhere present as oil throughout the sesamum seed.





* * *





eḷḷukku ēḻu uḻavu koḷḷukku ōr uḻavu.

Ploughing seven times for sesamum seed, once for gram.





* * *





eḷḷuttāṉ eṇṇeykku ularukiṟatu, elippiḻukkai eṉṉattukku ularukiṟatu?

Sesamum seed is dried for oil; but why dry rat-dung?





* * *





eḷḷum paccai ariciyumpōla irukkavēṇṭum.

It must be as sesamum seed and raw rice.





* * *





eḷḷum karumpum iṟukkiṉāl payaṉ tarum.

Sesamum seed and sugarcane yield a profit when pressed.





* * *





eḷḷukku ēḻu uḻavu uḻukiṟavēḷai, veḷḷāḷā koḷḷukku ōr uḻavu uḻutu payircey.

O, Vellala who ploughest seven times to sow rape-seed, plough once for horse-gram and thus cultivate the ground.





* * *





eṟikiṟatu muyalukkup paṭukiṟatu paṟṟaikku.

Throwing at a hare and hitting a bush.





* * *





eṟivāṉēṉ coṟivāṉēṉ?

Why throw, why scratch?

Why provoke a nuisance?





* * *





eṟumpu kaṭikkap poṟukkātā?

Canst thou not bear the bite of an ant?





* * *





eṟumpiṉ kaṇ ataṉ aḷavukkup peritu, āṉaiyiṉ kaṇ ataṉ aḷavukkuc ciṟiyatu.

The eyes of an ant are large in proportion to its size, the eyes of an elephant are small for its size.





* * *





eṟumpukkuk koṭṭāṅkacci taṇṇīr camuttiram.

A cocoanut shell-full of water is an ocean to an ant.





* * *





eṟumpup puṟṟil pāmpu kuṭikoḷvatupōla.

As a snake occupies an ant-hole.





* * *





eṟumpukkum taṉ kaiyāl eṇcāṇ uṭampu.

Even an ant is eight spans long as measured by its own hand.





* * *





eṟumpu ūrak kal tēyum.

By the continual creeping of ants a stone will wear away.





* * *





eṟumpiṉ kaṇṇukku erumai mūttiram ēkapperuveḷḷam.

The urine of the buffalo is as a perfect deluge in the eye of an ant.





* * *





eṟumpu ūrak kal kuḻiyum.

By the continual creeping of ants a stone will become hollow.





* * *





eṟumpu ūra iṭaṅkoṭuttāl erutum potiyum uḷḷē celuttuvāṉ.

If room be given sufficient for ants to creep in, he will drive a loaded bullock that way.





* * *





eṟumpu eṭuttup pōvataṟkut taṭi eṭuttu niṟkiṟatu, periya pūcaṉikkāy pōkiṟatu teriyātā?

You stand armed with a club to watch that which may be carried away by ants, are you unable to see when a large pumpkin has been taken away?





* * *





eṟumpu mutal eṇṇāyiram kōṭikkum teriyum.

It is known to the eighty thousand millions of creatures from an ant upwards.





* * *





eṟumpu muṭṭai koṇṭu tiṭṭai ēṟiṉ maḻai peyyum.

If ants carry their eggs to a higher place, it will rain.





* * *





eṉakku aṭṭamattuc caṉi.

With me Saturn is in the eighth sign.





* * *





eṉakku ūṇum illai uṟakkamum illai.

I have neither food nor sleep.





* * *





eṉatu nāṭkaḷ ellām ūmai kaṇṭa kaṉā pōl āyiṉa.

My days are like the dream of the dumb.





* * *





eṉ īralaik karukku arivāḷ koṇṭu aṟukkiṟatu.

It cuts my liver with a sharp sickle.





* * *





eṉ kāriyam ellām nantaṉ paṭaivīṭāy pōyiṟṟu.

My affairs are like Nandan’s camp.

The name of a shoemaker who is reputed to have reigned as a king for three hours, and to have issued leather coin.





* * *





eṉ kuṭi keṭṭatum uṉ kuṭi keṭṭatum poḻutu viṭintāl teriyum.

It will be known at day-break whether my family or your's has been ruined.





* * *





eṉpait tiṉṟu cataiyaik koṭuttu vaḷarttāḷ.

Eating bones herself, she has brought up her children on flesh.





* * *





eṉmakaḷ vārattōṭē vāram muḻukuvāḷ, eṉ marumakaṉ tīpāvaḷikkut tīpāvaḷi muḻukuvāṉ.

My daughter bathes once a week, my son-in-law bathes at dèpàveli.





* * *





eṉ marumakaṉukku vēppeṇṇeyām tūkkeṇṇey.

Superior oil is said to be Mangosa oil to my son-in-law.





* * *





eṉ mukattiṟ karipūciṉāyē.

Thou hast smeared my face with charcoal.





* * *





eṉ mutukiṉ tōl uṉakkuc ceruppāy irukkum.

The skin of my back will serve you for shoes.





* * *





eṉ vīṭṭukku vantāl eṉṉa koṇṭu varukiṟāy, uṉ vīṭṭukku vantāl eṉṉa koṭukkiṟāy?

When you come to my house, what do you bring, when I come to yours, what do you give?





* * *





eṉṟum maṟaintiruppatilum etirē pōtal uttamam.

It is better to come to the front at once than to be always concealed.





* * *





eṉṟu niṉṟum poṉṟuvar ōr nāḷ.
