No matter how long one may live, the day of death will come.





* * *





eṉṉa coṉṉālum eṉ putti pōkātu.

Say what you may, I will not change my mind.





* * *





eṉṉaṭā tātā puraṭṭācimātam muppatum oru kantāyam.

O! mendicant, the thirty days of September are all days of receipt.





* * *





eṉṉaṭā kuccuk kaṭṭip pēcukiṟāy.

You fellow, you affect to speak in an elegant style.





* * *





eṉṉamāyc colli itamāy uraittālum kaḻutaikku upatēcam kātil ēṟātu.

No instruction however explicit or agreeable will enter the ear of an ass.





* * *





eṉṉa māyam iṭaicci māyam, mōrōṭu taṇṇīr kalanta māyam.

What is the deception practised by the dairy maid? It is mixing water with butter-milk.





* * *





eṉṉaṭā keṭṭuppōkiṟāy eṉṟāl, iṉṉamum keṭṭup pōkiṟēṉ pantayam pōṭu eṉkiṟāṉ.

If one say, fie on thee, thou art destroying thyself, he replies, I will take a bet that I will exceed my former course.





* * *





eṉṉa tiṉṟālum ataṟkumēlē nālu pērīccampaḻam tiṉṉa ellām aṭipaṭum.

No matter what may be eaten; if four dates be taken afterwards, the whole will be digested.





* * *





eṉṉuṭaiya vīṭṭiṟkup pūvāy varap poṉṉum turumpāccu.

Since the woman came to my house, even gold has become a common thing.





* * *





eṉṉaippōlak kuralum eṉ akkāḷaippōla oyilum illai eṉkiṟatām kaḻutai.

The ass boasted that there was no voice equal to his, and no gait equal to that of his elder sister.





* * *





eṉṉaik kaṇṭāl caṇalukkuḷḷē oḷikkiṟāy, eṉ peṇ cātiyaik kaṇṭāl caṭṭikkuḷḷē oḷikkiṟāy.

On seeing me, thou hidest among the hemp, and on seeing my wife, thou hidest in a chatty.





* * *





ē.



ēkātacikku mā iṭittāṟpōla.

Like pounding flour for Ekàdasi.

Ekàdasi, the eleventh of December which is observed by the Hindus as a fast and therefore the preparation of food is not required. Said of something irrelevant.





* * *





ēkātacit tiruṭiyai ēṟṟaṭā ratattiṉmēlē.

O fellow, put the pretended observer of Ekádasi on the car.





* * *





ēkāli vākaṉam poti cumantāṟpōla.

As a washerman’s vehicle (a donkey) carries its load.





* * *





ēccilum pēccilum vallavaṉē.

He is indeed clever in abuse and empty talk.





* * *





ēṭā kūṭākkāṟaṉukku vaḻi eṅkē eṉṟāl, pōkiṟavaṉ talai mēlām.

If it be asked where is the way for the stubborn, it is replied, on the head of the way faring man.





* * *





ēṭu aṟiyātāṉ pīṭu peṟātāṉ.

The ignorant man is not held in estimation.





* * *





ēṭṭuc curaikkāy kaṟikku ākumā?

Will the word suraikai a gourd serve as a curry?





* * *





ēṇikkompukku kōṇakkompu pōṭalāmā?

Is a crooked pole fit for a ladder?





* * *





ēṇaikkaḻikkuk kōṇaṟkaḻi veṭṭukiṟatā?

Do you cut a crooked stick for a litter.





* * *





ēṇṭā teṉṉamarattil ēṟiṉāy eṉṟāl, kaṉṟukkuṭṭikkup pul piṭuṅka eṉṟāṉ.

You fellow! why did you go up the Cocoanut tree? when thus addressed, he replied, I went to get grass for the calf.





* * *





ēṇṭā karuṭā cukamā? irukkaṟa iṭattil iruntāl cukam tāṉ.

O Garuda, are you well? I should be well enough if I were in the place where I ought to be.





* * *





ēṇṭā paṭṭappakalil tiruṭukiṟāy ? eṉ avacaram uṉakkut teriyumā?

What! do you steal in broad day light? He replies, do you know how pressing my necessities are?





* * *





ēṇṭā puḷiyamarattil ēṟiṉāy? pūṉaikkuṭṭikkup pul paṟikka.

Why, man, have you got up into the Tamarind tree? he replied, to pluck grass for my kitten.





* * *





ēṇṭā muṭiccu aviḻkkiṟāy? eṉpaci uṉakkut teriyumā?

Why, you fellow, do you untie the knot? Do you know how hungry I am?





* * *





ēṇṭi ciṟukki pullu āccā? oru noṭikkumuṉ kaṭṭāccē.

Well, my girl, have you cut the grass? she replied, it was tied up before one could snap his fingers.





* * *





ēṇṭi peṇṇē cōrntirukkiṟāy? cōṟu pattiyam.

Why, my girl, do you faint? I have not had rice enough.





* * *





ēṇṭi peṇṇē kuntiyirukkiṟāy? cōṟu paṟṟāmal.

Why my girl do you squat! For want of sufficient rice.





* * *





ētu piriyam? illātatu piriyam.

What do you wish! That which I have not.





* * *





ētum aṟṟavaṉukku ēṉ iraṇṭu peṇṭāṭṭi?

Why two wives to one who has nothing?





* * *





ēteṉṟu kēṭpārum illai, eṭuttup piṭippārum illai.

None to enquire, none to help.





* * *





ēri uṭaikiṟataṟkumuṉṉē aṇai pōṭavēṇṭum.

Before the bund bursts, it must be strengthened.





* * *





ēri niṟaintāl karai kaciyum.

If the tank be full, its bank will be moist.





* * *





ēri perukil eṅkum perukku.

When the tank overflows, the flood will spread all around.





* * *





ēri mitantāl iṭaiyaṉai matiyātu.

If the hunch of the ox grow high, he will not fear his keeper.





* * *





ēriyiṉ nīraik kaṭṭutal aritu uṭaittal eḷitu.

It is difficult to confine the water of a tank, but easy to break the bund.





* * *





ēriyiṉ taṇṇīr itō cūriyatēvā?

O divine sun, behold the water of the tank?





* * *





ēriyōṭu pakaikoṇṭu cavucam ceyyātirukkiṟatā?

Do you abstain from ablution because you are dissatisfied with the tank?





* * *





ēr uḻukiṟa piḷḷai iḷaittupōṉāl pōkiṟatu, pariyam pōṭṭa peṇṇaip pārttu vaḷar.

Never mind, should the youth at the plough become lean, take care of the girl who has received the nuptial presents.





* * *





ēr piṭittavaṉ eṉṉa ceyvāṉ pāṉaipiṭittavaḷ pākkiyam.

Comfort depends on her who has charge of the cooking pots, not on the ploughman.





* * *





ēlavē tolaintatu eṅkaḷait toṭṭa karmam.

The sin which affected us is already removed.





* * *





ēlēlam! ēlēlam! erumaiccāṇi kāykiṟatu.

Elélam, Elélam, the buffalo dung is drying.

The sound élélam is uttered by boatmen and others on the Coromandel coast when pulling together, as savas is, by boatmen on the Hoogly: some derive it from valikkiṟatu.





* * *





ēlēlaciṅkaṉporuḷ ēḻu kaṭaliṟ pōṉālum tirumpum.

The property of Elélasingam, though it pass over seven seas, will return.

Elélasingam a wealthy merchant, the disciple of Tiruvalluvar.





* * *





ēvaṟpēy kūraiyaip piṭuṅkum.

The excited demon will take off the thatch.





* * *





ēvā makkaḷ mūvā maruntu.

Children who do their duty unprompted are as a life-preserving remedy.





* * *





ēvukiṟavaṉukku vāyccol, ceykiṟavaṉukkut talaiccumai.

To the taskmaster a word, to the servant a burden on the head.





* * *





ēḻai aḻuta kaṇṇīr kūriyavāḷai okkum.

The tears of the poor are as sharp swords.





* * *





ēḻai pākkut tiṉṉa eṭṭuvīṭu aṟiyavēṇṭumā?

Because the poor man uses betel-nut, is it to be made known at eight houses?





* * *





ēḻai pēccu araṇmaṉaikku ēṟumā?

Will the speech of the poor go up to the palace?





* * *





ēḻaip piḷḷaikkut teyvamē tuṇai.

God is the helper of the helpless child.





* * *





ēḻaip piḷḷaikku evarkaḷum tuṇai.

Every one is a helper to a helpless child.





* * *





ēḻaiyaik kaṇṭāl mōḻaiyum pāyum .

Even a beast without horns will attack the poor.





* * *





ēṟac coṉṉāl erutukkuk kōpam, iṟaṅkac coṉṉāl noṇṭikkuk kōpam.

When told to mount, the bull is angry, when told to dismount, the lame man is dissatisfied.





* * *





ēṟappaṭāta marattilē eṇṇappaṭāta māṅkāy.

Innumerable mangoes on a tree no one can climb.





* * *





ēṟa viṭṭu ēṇiyai vāṅkukiṟatā?

What! remove the ladder after allowing one to mount?





* * *





ēṟiṉa kompāl iṟaṅka vēṇṭum.

One must come down on the branch by which he ascended.





* * *





ēṟukiṟa kutiraiyilum uḻavumāṭu atika uttamam.

A plough bull is superior to a saddle horse.





* * *





ēṟuneṟṟi cūṟutalai etirkka vantāl ākātu.

It is a bad omen to meet one with a high forehead or curly hair.





* * *





ēṟumāṟāyp pēcukiṟatā?

What, is it to speak at random?





* * *





ēṟum tēmal iṟaṅkum paṭartāmarai kūṭum puruvam kuṭiyaik keṭukkum.

Ascending cutaneous spots, descending ring-worm and eyebrows contiguous are ominous.





* * *





ēṟkaṉavē māmi pēykkōlam atilum koñcam mākkōlam.

The mother-in-law is frightfully ugly already, and the flour on her face makes her more so.





* * *





ēṟkaṉavē varukiṟa vikkiṉaṅkaḷait taṭukka vēṇṭum.

Approaching evils should be provided against in due time.





* * *





ēṟṟakkōlukkup piṭittāl arivāṭpiṭikku varum.

If you measure sufficient for a well-bucket pole, it may suffice for the handle of a sickle.





* * *





ēṟṟappāṭṭukku etirppāṭṭā?

Is the song of the picotta responsive?





* * *





ēṉ koḻukkaṭṭai cavukkiṭṭāy? oru kācu vellam illāmaṟ cavukkiṭṭēṉ?

Cake! why so insipid? Because I lack a cash-worth of sugar.





* * *





ai.



aiṅkalak kappiyil naḻuviṉa kappi ivaṉ.

He is a broken grain fallen out of five kalams.

One of a large number of insignificant persons.





* * *





aiṅkātam pōṉālum akappē araikkācu kiṭaiyātē.

Though you may go fifty miles, you will not fetch half a cash.





* * *





aiṅkātam pōṉālum, akappai araikkācu.

Although it may go fifty miles, an agappai will fetch but half a cash.





* * *





aiṅkātam pōṉālum aṟimukam vēṇṭum.

Though one goes fifty miles off, an acquaintance is needed.





* * *





aiṅkātam pōṉālum taṉ pāvam taṉṉōṭē.

Though he may go fifty miles, his own sin will still cleave to him.





* * *





aiṅkāyam iṭṭa kāram iṭṭālum taṉ nāṟṟam pōkātām pēyccuraikkāykku.



Though cooked with five ingredients, the wild gourd will not lose its odour.



* * *





aitatu nel, aṭarntatu cuṟṟam.

In rice plants, distance is required, in kindred, closeness.





* * *





aintu ciṭṭukku iraṇṭu kācu vilai.

The price of five chits a small bird is two cash.





* * *





aippaci mātam aḻukait tūṟal kārttikai mātam kaṉa maḻai.

In October drizzling, in November heavy rain.





* * *





aippaci mātattu veyyalil aṉṟu uritta tōl aṉṟu kāyum .

In the sunshine of October a skin dries the day it is stript off.





* * *





aippacikkum kārttikaikkum maḻai illāviṭṭāl aṇṇaṉukkum cari tampikkum cari.

If the rains of October and November fail, the elder and younger brother will be on a par.





* * *





aippaci mātattu erumaikkaṭāvum mārkaḻi mātattu nampiyāṉum cari.

A buffalo in the month of October and a Vaishnava brahman in December are alike.





* * *





aimpatilē aṟivu, aṟupatilē aṭakkam, aṟupatukkumēl oṉṟumillai.

At fifty discrimination, at sixty moderation, after sixty no distinguishing characteristic.





* * *





aimpatu vayatāṉavaṉukku aintu vayatu peṇṇā?

Is a girl of five fit to be the wife to a man of fifty?





* * *





aiyaṅkārum tottuk koṭuppār.

Even the Aiyangar may communicate contagious disease.





* * *





aiyappaṭṭāl paiya naṭa.

If in doubt, advance slowly.





* * *





aiyappaṉ kutiraiyai vaiyāḷi viṭṭa katai.

Like the story of one who rode Aiyanàr's horse.





* * *





aiyamāṉa kāriyattaic ceyyal ākātu.

To do a doubtful thing is bad.





* * *





aiyam ēṟṟum aṟivē ōtu.

Though reduced to beggary, learn to be wise.





* * *





aiyar eṉpavar tuyyar āvar .

Those who are entitled to be called brahmans are holy.





* * *





aiyar varukiṟavaraiyil amāvācai niṟkumā?

Will the new moon await the brahman’s arrival?





* * *





aiyar katirpōla ammāḷ kutirpōla.

The husband is like an ear of corn, the wife is like a rice bin or grain receptacle.





* * *





aiyaṉār paṭaiyiṟ kuyavaṉār paṭṭatupōla.

As the potter perished in the army of Aiyanar.





* * *





aiyaṉār kōvil ceṅkal attaṉaiyum teyvam.

The bricks of Aiyanar’s temple are so many gods.





* * *





aiyaṉār kōvil maṇṇai mitittavar attaṉaiyum piṭāri.

Those who have trodden the ground of Aiyanar’s temple are so many Pidaris.





* * *





aiyaṉārē vārum kaṭāvaik kaikkoḷḷum.

Come, Aiyanar, and accept a goat, a sacrifice.





* * *





aiyaṉ amaippai ārālum taḷḷakkūṭātu.

No one may dispute the pre-ordination of god.





* * *





aiyō eṉṟāl ivaṉukku āṟu mācattup pāvam cuṟṟum.

The man who expresses commiseration for him will incur six months sin.





* * *





o.



okkap piṟanta taṅkai ōlamiṭṭu aḻaccē oppārit taṅkaikkuc ciṟṟāṭaiyām.

When one’s sister is weeping for a cloth, is it to be given to a woman who resembles her?





* * *





occiyam illāta ūrilē peṇ vāṅkiṉa katai.

The story of a man taking a girl to wife in a village inhabited by the shameless.





* * *





oṭinta kōl āṉālum ūṉṟukōl irukkavēṇṭum

Though a broken one, a walking stick is necessary.





* * *





oṭṭikkoṇṭu vantum taṭṭik kaḻikkiṟāṉ.

Though I cling to him, he repels me.





* * *





oṭṭiṉāl toṭṭililum koḷḷum oṭṭāviṭṭāl kaṭṭililum koḷḷātu.

If compressed the crib will hold it, but if not even a bedstead will not contain it.





* * *





oṭṭiṉālum uḻakkuppīrc ceṉkiṟatā?

Is it to say draw one ulak of milk though the animal is lean?





* * *





oṭṭaikkup paḷuvu ēṟṟukiṟatupōla.

As a camel is loaded.





* * *





oti peruttut tūṇāmā?

Though the odina tree grow large, will it do for a pillar?





* * *





oti peruttāl uralāmā?

Though the odina tree grow large, will it serve for a mortar?





* * *





oti peruttu eṉṉa, upakāram illātavaṉ vāḻntu eṉṉa?

What avails the growth of an odina tree? Of what use is the prosperity of the ungenerous?





* * *





otiyamaram tūṇāmō? oṭṭāṅkiḷiñcil kācāmō?

Will an odina tree do for a pillar? will a broken oyster-shell pass as a coin?





* * *





otiyamaramum oru camayattukku utavum.

Even an odina tree may prove useful on an emergency.





* * *





oyyārakkoṇṭaiyām tāḻampūvām uḷḷē mēyumām īrum pēṉum.

Her tresses are graceful and ornamented with the flowers of the screw pine, but nits and lice breed therein.





* * *





oru uṟaiyilē iraṇṭu kattiyā?

Are two swords contained in one sheath?





* * *





oru ūrukku oru vaḻiyā?

Is there but one way to a village?





* * *





oru kaṭṭu vaikkōlait taṇṇīriṟ pōṭṭu eṭṭu āḷ kūṭi iḻuttāṟ pōla.

As a bundle of straw that had been dipped in water was dragged along by eight persons.





* * *





oru kampattil iraṇṭu āṉai kaṭṭukiṟatā?

What, to tie two elephants to one pole?





* * *





oru kācu akappaṭukiṟatu kutiraik kompu

To earn one cash is as difficult as to find a horse with horns.





* * *





oru kācu pēṇiṉ iru kācu tēṟum.

One cash saved is two cash got.





* * *





orukāṟ ceytavaṉ irukāṟ ceyvāṉ.

He who has done a thing once, may do it again.





* * *





oru kācu koṭātavaṉ oru varākaṉ koṭuppāṉā?

Will he who refuses to give a cash, give a pagoda?

A Pagoda is a gold coin worth about seven shillings.





* * *





oru kācu eṉṟa iṭattil aḻukiṟāṉ.

He weeps when the word money is uttered.





* * *





orukuṭam pālukku oru tuḷi purai.

One drop of butter-milk to a pot of milk.





* * *





oru kuṇṭilē kōṭṭai piṭikkalāmā?

Can a fort be taken with one ball?





* * *





oru kulattiṟ piṟanta tāmaraiyum alliyum orē taṉmaiyuṭaiyatalla atupōla oru tāyiṉ vayiṟṟil piṟanta cakōtarar iruppārkaḷ.

The lotus flowers of a tank are not all of the same species, so the children of the same mother are not all alike.





* * *





oru kūṭu muṭaintavaṉ oṉpatu kūṭu muṭaivāṉ.

He who has platted one basket may plat nine.





* * *





oru kūṭai kallum teyvamāṉāl, kumpiṭukiṟatu entakkallai?

If all the stones in a basket be gods, which shall I worship?





* * *





oru kai taṭṭiṉāl ōcai eḻumpumā?

Can clapping be effected by one hand?





* * *





oru kai taṭṭiṉāl ōcai eḻumpumā, irukai taṭṭiṉāl ōcai eḻumpumā?

Can clapping be effected with one, or with both hands?





* * *





oru kai muḻam pōṭumā?

Can cubits be measured with only one arm?





* * *





oru kōpam vantu kiṇaṟṟil viḻuntāl āyiram cantōṣam vantālum eḻumpalāmā?

When one has fallen into a well in a fit of anger, will a thousand joyous considerations help him out?





* * *





oru caṭṭiyilē iraṇṭu tailam.

Two kinds of oil in one chatty.





* * *





oru cantippāṉai nāy aṟiyātu.

A dog does not know the vessels used on fast-days.

Separate vessels are kept for special occasions, and also for different purposes on ordinary days, as for boiling milk &c.





* * *





oru cāṇ kāṭṭilē oru muḻattaṭi veṭṭalāmā?

Can a stick a cubit long be cut in a grove a span high?





* * *





orutaram viḻuntāl teriyātā?

Having fallen once are you not wiser?





* * *





orutalai vaḻakku nūlilum cevvai.

An ex parte statement is straighter than a line.





* * *





oru talaikku iraṇṭu ākkiṉaiyā?

Are there two punishments for one head?





* * *





oru tāy aṟṟa piḷḷaikku ūrellām tāy.

The whole village is mother to the motherless.





* * *





oru naṉṟi ceytavarai uḷḷa aḷavum niṉai.

Think of those who have done you even one favour as long as you live.





* * *





oru nāṭ kūttukkut talaiyaic ciraittatupōl.

Like shaving the head for a single day’s dance.





* * *





oru nāḷaikku ikaḻcci oru nāḷaikkup pukaḻcci.

One day blame, another day praise.





* * *





oru nāḷaikku iṟakkiṟatu kōṭi piṟakkiṟatu kōṭi.

Ten millions are born, and ten millions die, daily.





* * *





oru nāḷum cirikkātavaṉ tirunāḷilē cirittāṉ, tirunāḷum veṟunāḷāyiṟṟu.

A man who had never laughed before, laughed on a festival day, consequently it became a common day.





* * *





oru nāḷākilum tirunāḷ.

Although only one day, it is a festival day.





* * *





oru paṉai iraṇṭu pāḷai oṉṟu nuṅku oṉṟu kaḷḷu.

One palmyra has two spathes, one yields fruit, the other toddy.





* * *





oru pāṉaic cōṟṟukku oṉṟē mātiri.

One grain suffices to test a whole pot of boiled rice.





* * *





oru piḷḷai peṟṟavaḷukku uṟiyilē cōṟu nālu piḷḷai peṟṟavaḷukku naṭutteruvilē cōṟu.

The rice for a woman who has borne one child, is on the swinging tray, that of the woman who has borne four, is in the middle of the street.





* * *





orupiḷḷaiyeṉṟu ūṭṭi vaḷarttāḷām, atu ceriyāk kuṇam piṭittuc cettatām.

It is said that having only one child she fed it well, but that it died of indigestion.





* * *





oru puttiraṉ āṉālum kuru puttiraṉ āvāṉā?

Although he is an only son, will he become an obedient disciple?





* * *





oru poruḷ ākilum eḻuti aṟi.

Though only one item, note it.





* * *





oru marattup paḻamā orumikka?

What! did one tree yield all this fruit?





* * *





oru marattup paṭṭai oru marattilē oṭṭumā?

Will the bark of one tree stick to another?





* * *





oru marattuk kompu oru marattil oṭṭātu.

The branch of one tree will not stick to another.





* * *





oru maṉappaṭu, ōtuvārkku utavu.

Be single-minded, assist those that teach the Vedas.





* * *





oru miḷakukku āṟṟaik kaṭṭi iṟaitta ceṭṭi.

A merchant who dammed up and drained a river to recover a grain of pepper.





* * *





oru miḷakum nālu uppum pōtum.

One grain of pepper and four grains of salt will suffice.





* * *





oru muḻukkilē maṇ eṭukkiṟatā?

What! is it to take up the soil by diving once?





* * *





orumaippāṭu illāta kuṭi orumikkak keṭum.

A family divided against itself will perish together.





* * *





oruvaṉ aṟintāl ulakam aṟiyum.

If known to one, the world may know it.





* * *





oruvaṉ aṟinta rakaciyam ulakattil paravum.

A secret known to one may spread through the world.





* * *





oruvaṉukku iruvar tuṇai oruvaṉai aṟiya iruvar vēṇṭum.

Two men may help one, two are necessary that one may be known.





* * *





oruvarum aṟiyāta uccita nāmaṉ.

One of illustrious name unknown to any.





* * *





oruvaṉaik koṉṟavaṉ uṭaṉē cāvāṉ pala pēraik koṉṟavaṉ paṭṭam āḷuvāṉ.

He who kills one person immediately suffers death, he who kills many is rewarded with a crown.





* * *





oruvaṉāyp piṟappatu oru piṟappāmā oṉṟi maram tōppāmā?

Is it worth being to be an only offspring? Is a single tree a tope a grove?





* * *





oruvaṉ talaiyil māṇikkam irukkiṟateṉṟu veṭṭalāmā?

May you cut off a man’s head because there is a ruby in it?





* * *





oruvaṉāyp piṟantāl taṉimai iruvarāyp piṟantāṟ pakaimai.

When only one is born there is loneliness, when two enmity.





* * *





oruvar aṟintāl irakaciyam iruvar aṟintāl parāciyam.

If known to one only it is a secret, if to two it is public.





* * *





oruvaṉaip paṟṟi ōrakattiru.

Attach yourself to one person, and dwell in one house.





* * *





oru viral noṭi iṭātu.

A single finger cannot snap.





* * *





ovvāk kūṭṭilum taṉimai aḻaku, ovvāp pēccu vacaiyōṭu okkum.

Solitude is preferable to disagreeable society, an unpleasant expression may be felt as a reproach.





* * *





oḻukāta vīṭu uḷḷaṅkai attaṉai pōtum.

If only as large as the palm of the hand a house that does not leak will suffice.





* * *





oḻukkam uyar kulattil naṉṟu.

Virtue is superior to rank.





* * *





oḻukku vīṭṭilē veḷḷam vantatupōla.

As a flood came into a leaky house.





* * *





oḻukkukku vaitta caṭṭi pōla.

As a chatty placed under a leak.





* * *





oḻuṅku oru paṇam caḷukku mukkāṟ paṇam.

Propriety is worth a fanam, pride, three fourths of a fanam.





* * *





oḷikkum cēvakaṉukku mukattil ēṉ mīcai?

Of what use is a mustache to a sneaking soldier?





* * *





oḷikkap pōyum talaiyāri vīṭṭilā?

When trying to conceal one’s self, is it fit to do so in the house of the chief?





* * *





oḷikkap pōyum iṭam iṭaiñcalā?

Is the place too narrow for one who has gone to hide himself?





* * *





oḷḷiyar teḷḷiyar āyiṉum ūḻviṉai paiya nuḻaintu viṭum.

However enlightened and clear-headed one may be, his destiny will quietly creep in.





* * *





oṟṟiyum cītaṉamum paṟṟi āḷavēṇṭum.

Property received under mortgage, or as a dowry, must be taken into immediate possession.





* * *





oṉṟa vanta pēy ūraik keṭuttatupōla.

As the demon that came for shelter destroyed the village.





* * *





oṉṟa vanta kākam uram peṟṟāṟpōla.

As if a crow that came for shelter was strengthened in his position.





* * *





oṉṟa vanta piṭāri ūrp piṭāriyai oṭṭiṉatupōl.

As a demoness that came for shelter drove away the demoness of the village.





* * *





oṉṟa vanta piṭāri ūrp piṭāri āṉatupōla.

As a demoness that came for shelter became the demoness of the village.





* * *





oṉṟāṉa pirapu uṟaṅkik kiṭaikkaiyil piccaikku vantavaṉ tattiyōṉattiṟku aḻukiṟāṉām.

It is said that whilst a peerless nobleman was lying hungry and exhausted, a beggar cries for rice and curds.





* * *





oṉṟāl oṉṟu kuṟai illai, muṉṉālē kaṭṭat tuṇi illai.

Not a single want, yet no cloth to put on.





* * *





oṉṟu niṉaikka oṉṟāyiṟṟu.

One thing meditated, another effected.





* * *





oṉṟum aṟiyāḷām kaṉṉi avaḷaip piṭattatām caṉṉi.

It is said that the virgin is innocent, and is seized with lock-jaw.





* * *





oṉṟu terintavaṉukku ellām teriyātu.

He who knows one thing does not know all things.





* * *





oṉṟum illaiyeṉṟu ūtiṉāṉ atutāṉum illaiyeṉṟu koṭṭiṉāṉ.

He blew a conch to report that there was nothing, and beat a drum to intimate that there was not even that.





* * *





oṉṟum aṟṟavaṉukku oṉpatu peṇṭāṭṭi.

Nine wives to him who has nothing.





* * *





oṉṟum aṟṟa taṅkāḷukku oṉpatu nāḷ caṭaṅkā?

Is a ceremony of nine days to be performed for a destitute woman?





* * *





oṉṟē kutirai oṉṟē rāvuttaṉ.

One horse, one horseman.





* * *





oṉṟē rājā oṉṟē kutirai.

One king, one horse.





* * *





oṉṟaip peṟṟāl naṉṟē peṟavēṇṭum.

If one thing only be obtained, it ought to be good.





* * *





oṉṟai piṭittāṟ cātikka vēṇṭum.

The thing asserted must be maintained.





* * *





oṉṟaip peṟṟālum kaṭukap peṟu.

Though you get only one thing, get it quickly.





* * *





oṉṟaip peṟṟālum kaṉṟaip peṟu.

If you get only one as your hire, take a calf.





* * *





oṉṟait toṭiṉum naṉṟait toṭu.

Though you touch but one thing, touch what is good.

Most likely referring to the choice of a wife.





* * *





ō.



ō koṭuppāṉukku vāḻkkaip paṭṭu ōṭṭam oḻiya, naṭai illai.

Having become the wife of the wretch, I am compelled to run instead of walking.





* * *





ōṅkil aṟiyum uyar kaṭaliṉ āḻam, pāṅki aṟivāḷ taṉ pattāviṉ valimai.

An onkil fish knows the depth of the ocean, a wife knows her husband’s strength.





* * *





ōcai kāṭṭip pūcai cey.

Ring the bell, and perform pujá.





* * *





ōcai peṟum veṇkalam, ōcaipeṟā maṭkalam.

Sounding brass, a soundless earthen vessel.





* * *





ōṭam viṭṭu iṟaṅkiṉāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.

On landing, a cuff for the boatman.





* * *





ōṭam viṭṭa āṟum aṭi cuṭum.

Even a navigable river may burn the feet when dry.





* * *





ōṭam kaṭṭiṉa tūlam.

A block of wood to which a boat is moored.





* * *





ōṭam kaṭantāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.

After crossing, a cuff for the boatman.





* * *





ōṭam vaṇṭiyilum vaṇṭi ōṭattilum kāṇavumpaṭum.

A boat may be sesen in a cart, and a cart in a boat.





* * *





ōṭam viṭṭa iṭam aṭi cuṭum aṭi cuṭṭa iṭam ōṭam viṭappaṭum.

The foot may be burnt in a navigable river, and a boat may float where the foot was burnt.





* * *





ōṭavum māṭṭāṉ piṭikkavum māṭṭāṉ, ōyāmal pēcuvāṉ.

He can neither run nor overtake, but he can talk continually.





* * *





ōṭavum māṭṭēṉ piṭikkavum māṭṭēṉ.

I can neither run nor catch.





* * *





ōṭi oru kōṭi tēṭuvatilum iruntu oru kācu tēṭuvatu nalam.

It is better to acquire a cash in quiet than a lak by running about.





* * *





ōṭi oṉpatu paṇam campātippatilum uṭkāntiruntu oru paṇam campātippatu naṉṟu.

Better earn one fanam where you are, than nine fanams by running hither and thither.





* * *





ōṭi ōṭi uḷḷaṅkālum veḷuttatu.

The sole of the foot has become white by constant running.





* * *





ōṭippōṉa puruṣaṉ vantu kūṭikkoṇṭāṉ, uṭaimaimēl uṭaimai pōṭṭu miṉukkikkoṇṭāḷ.

The husband that ran away has returned and is reconciled, therefore she has adorned herself with jewels to excess.





* * *





ōṭippōṉa ūril ātarittavaṉ kavuṇṭaṉ.

He who entertained the man that fled ftom his own village was a Koundan-a man of that tribe.





* * *





ōṭippōkiṟavaṉ pāṭippōkiṟāṉ.

He who is running away, does so singing.





* * *





ōṭiya muyal periya muyal allavō?

The hare that ran away was a large one, was it not?





* * *





ōṭiyam ākilum ūṭuruvak kēḷ.

Though it is obscene, hear it out.





* * *





ōṭiyum kiḻavikkup piṟakēyā?

Having set out to run will it do for him to be behind an old woman?





* * *





ōṭukiṟa pāmpaip piṭikkiṟa paruvam.

Old enough to seize a running snake.





* * *





ōṭukiṟavaṉaik kaṇṭāl turattukiṟavaṉukku ilēcu.

Seeing him who retreats, makes the efforts of his pursuer easy.





* * *





ōṭukiṟa veḷḷam aṇaiyil niṟkumā?

Will a rolling flood stay at the anicut?





* * *





ōṭṭaic caṭṭi āṉālum koḻukkaṭṭai ventāṟ cari.

The chatty may be cracked, what matters that if it bakes the cakes.





* * *





ōṭṭai nāḻikkup pūṇ kaṭṭiṉatu pōla.

Like adorning a cracked measure with a metal rim.





* * *





ōṭṭaip pāṉaiyilē carkkarai irukkum.

A cracked pot will hold sugar.





* * *





ōṭṭaip pāṉaic carkkarai kacakkumā?

Will sugar, because put in a cracked pot, taste bitter?





* * *





ōṭṭai maṇi āṉālum ōcai nīṅkumā?

Though the bell may be cracked will it be void of sound?





* * *





ōṇāṉ viḻuṅkiya kataipōla.

Like the story relating to the swallowing of a bloodsucker.





* * *





ōtātārkku illai uṇarvoṭu oḻukkam.

The unlettered have neither good sense nor virtue.





* * *





ōtuvaṉ ellām uḻuvāṉ talaikkaṭaiyil.

All poets may be found at the entrance of the ploughman’s house.





* * *





ōtuvāṉukku ūrum uḻuvāṉukku nilamum illaiyā?

Has the poet no country, has the ploughman no land?





* * *





ōtuvārkku utavu.

Assist instructors-the brahmans.





* * *





ōnti vēlikku iḻukkiṉṟatu, tavaḷai taṇṇīrukku iḻukkiṉṟatu.

The blood-sucker draws its prey to a hedge, the frog to water.





* * *





ōmapiṇṭattai nāy iccittāṟpōlē.

As if a dog longed for consecrated food.

Spoken by Síta to Ràvanà.





* * *





ōyā maḻaiyum oḻiyāk kāṟṟum.

Continual rain, and ceaseless wind.





* * *





ōyvilā nēcamē yōlamē caraṇam.

Thou, the object my unceasing love, take me under thy protection.





* * *





ōram coṉṉavaṉ ārukku āvāṉ?

Will any employ a person who is given to one-sided statements?





* * *





ōram coṉṉavaṉ kuṭittaṉampōlē.

Like the family of him who makes partial statements.





* * *





ōr āṇṭi pacittirukka ulakam ellām kiṟukiṟeṉṟu cuṟṟukiṟatā?

Is the whole world so giddy through famine as to allow a religious mendicant to suffer from hunger?





* * *





ōr āṟu tāṇṭa māṭṭātavaṉ oṉpatu āṟu tāṇṭuvāṉā?

Will he who cannot cross one river, cross nine?





* * *





ōr ūrukkup pēccu maṟṟōr ūrukku ēccu.

That which is polite in one country may be abusive in another.





* * *





ōr ūrukku oru pēriṭṭuk koḷḷalāmā?

May one assume a different name in every village?





* * *





au.



auvai collukku accam illai.

No fear about Avvai's sayings.





* * *





auvaiyārmēlum kuṟṟam uṇṭu aṇṇāviyārmēlum paḻutu uṇṭu.

Even Avvai may be charged with blame, a religious teacher also may have his defects.





* * *





ka.



kaṅkaiyilē muḷaittālum pēyccurai nallacurai āmā?

Will a wild gourd become a good gourd by growing on the Ganges?





* * *





kaṅkaiyil mūḻkiṉālum kākkai aṉṉam ākumā?

Will a crow become a swan by bathing in the Ganges?





* * *





kaṅkaiyil āṭiṉālum, kaṇamum viṭāmaṟ ceyta pāvam tīrātu.

Though you wash in the Ganges, sin repeated again and again will not be expiated.





* * *





kaṅkaiyil āṭiṉālum pāvam tīrumā?

Will sin be expiated by bathing in the Ganges?





* * *





kaṅkaiyilē paṭintālum pēyccuraikkāy nalla curaikkāy ākātu.

Though a wild gourd be dipped in the Ganges, its inferiority will remain.





* * *





kaṅkaiyilē piṟanta nattai cālakkirāmam ākātu.

A snail of the Ganges is not a Shalgram.

The Shalgram is a flinty stone, containing the impression of one or more ammonitæ, supposed by the Hindus to represent Vishnu.





* * *





kacakki mōralāmā?

Can the fragrance of a flower be appreciated after bruising it?





* * *





kacaṭarukku illaik kaṟṟōr uṟavu.

The base do not enjoy the friendship of the learned.





* * *





kacaṭaṟak kallārkku icai uṟal illai.

Persons of imperfect learning have no reputation.





* * *





kacaṭāṉa kalviyiṉum kalviyīṉam nalam.

Ignorance is better than imperfect knowledge.





* * *





kacintu vantavaṉ kaṇṇait tuṭai.

Wipe off the tears of him who comes weeping.





* * *





kaccaṟ karuvāṭu mōṭcattiṟkup pōṉālum, piccaikkārar mōṭcattiṟkup pōkamāṭṭārkaḷ.

Though dried fish may attain final emancipation-heaven-beggars cannot.





* * *





kañci varatappā eṉṟāl, eṅkē varatappā eṉkiṟāṉ.

If I say, O Varathappa give me some kanji, he replies, O Varathappa where.





* * *





kaṭalilē ēṟṟam pōṭṭa katai.

The story relating to a picotta on the sea-side.





* * *





kaṭalil iṭṭa peruṅkāyampōla.

Like assafœtida cast into the ocean.





* * *





kaṭalilē turumpu kiṭantālum maṉatilē oru col kiṭavātu.

A rush may remain in the sea, but a secret will not remain in the mind.





* * *





kaṭalil karaitta puḷipōla.

Like tamarind acid dissolved in the sea.





* * *





kaṭalait tūrttum kāriyam muṭikka vēṇṭum.

You must accomplish your undertaking, though you may have to fill up the ocean.





* * *





kaṭalai tūrttālum kāriyam muṭiyātu.

Though the sea be filled up, the thing cannot be effected.





* * *





kaṭal kotittāl viḷāva nīr eṅkē?

Should the sea boil, whence water to cool it?





* * *





kaṭalnīr niṟaintu āvatu eṉṉa, kāñcirai paḻuttu āvatu eṉṉa?

What benefit arises from the water of the ocean, what good comes from the ripening of the kànjirai, strychnos nux vomica, fruit?





* * *





kaṭal tāṇṭa ācai uṇṭu, kālvāy tāṇṭak kāl illai.

He wishes to cross the ocean, but has no feet to cross a small drain.





* * *





kaṭal mīṉukku nuḷaiyaṉ iṭṭatē caṭṭam.

As regards the fish of the sea, whatever name the fisherman gives is final.





* * *





kaṭal vaṟṟik karuvāṭu tiṉṉalāmeṉṟu uṭal vaṟṟic cettatām kokku.

It is said that the stork died while waiting for the ocean to dry, in the hope of getting a supply of dried fish.





* * *





kaṭal perukiṉāl karaiyum perukumā?

Will the shore extend when the sea flows?





* * *





kaṭal perukiṉāl karai ētu?

What avails the shore when the sea flows?





* * *





kaṭaṟ karai tāḻaṅkāy kīḻ toṅki eṉṉa mēl toṅki eṉṉa?

What matters it whether the wild pine fruit on the sea-shore hangs high or low?





* * *





kaṭaṉōṭu kaṭaṉ kantap poṭi kāṟpaṇam.

Though my debt be increased, let me have a quarter of a fanam worth more of fragrant powder.





* * *





kaṭaṉ illāta kañci kāl vayiṟu.

One fourth supply of kanji for the stomach is better than debt.





* * *





kaṭaṉkāraṉukkuk kaṭaṉum, uṭaṉpiṟantāṉukkup paṅkum koṭukkavēṇṭum.

One must pay one’s creditor and give to a relation the portion due to him.





* * *





kaṭaṉ vāṅkik kaṭaṉ koṭuttavaṉumkeṭṭāṉ; maram ēṟik kai viṭṭavaṉum keṭṭāṉ.

He who borrowed to lend was ruined; and he who let go his hold of the tree he had climbed also perished.





* * *





kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyum paṭṭiṉi kalyāṇam paṇṇiyum canniyāci.

Famished though he has borrowed, an ascetic though he is wedded.





* * *





kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyum paṭṭiṉiyā?

What to famish after having borrowed money!





* * *





kaṭaṉkāraṉukkuk kaṭaṉum paḻikāraṉukkup paḻiyum koṭukka vēṇṭum.

Debt to the creditor and retaliation to the wrong-doer are due.





* * *





kaṭaṉkāraṉai vaikkak kaḻu uṇṭā?

Is there a stake for impaling debtors?





* * *





kaṭaṉ paṭṭār neñcam pōṟ kalaṅkiṉāṉ.

He was as disquieted as the mind of a debtor.





* * *





kaṭā kaṭā eṉṟālum maruntukku oru pīr eṉkiṟāṉ.

Though informed that it is a he-goat, he persists in asking a drop of milk for a medicinal purpose.





* * *





kaṭā piṉvāṅkukiṟatu pāyccalukku aṭaiyāḷam.

The drawing back of the he-goat shows that he is about to butt.





* * *





kaṭā mēykkiṟavaṉ aṟivāṉō koḻup pōṉaviṭam?

Does the cowherd know where the ploughshare has passed?





* * *





kaṭāviṉ cantiṟ pullait tiṉkiṟatupōla.

Like eating grass screened behind 2 bull.





* * *





kaṭāvum kaṭāvum caṇṭai pōṭukiṟapōtu uṇṇi nacuṅkiṉāṟpōla.

As the tick was crushed to death when the goats fought.





* * *





kaṭikōlilē kaṭṭiṉa nāy

A dog tied to a stick.





* * *





kaṭikka oru elumpum illai, kātil miṉukka oru ōlaiyum illai.

She has not even a single bone to pick, nor has she an ear-ornament to polish.





* * *





kaṭikkiṟa nākam kalantu uṟavākumā?

Will the cobra be affected by friendly intercourse?





* * *





kaṭikkiṟa nāykkuk kaḻuttil kuṟuṅ kayiṟu.

A short rope round the neck of a biting dog.





* * *





kaṭitāṉa piḷḷai peṟṟārukku utavumā?

Will an obdurate child prove helpful to its parents?





* * *





kaṭitāṉa col aṭiyilum valitu.

A harsh word is more painful than a blow.





* * *





kaṭitta pākkum koṭāta ciṟṟappaṉ, kaṭaitteru varaiyil vaḻi viṭṭāṉām.

It is said that his uncle who would not give even a bite of an areca-nut, bore him company to the bazaar.





* * *





kaṭitta vāy tuṭaittāṟpōla.

Like wiping the mouth after biting.





* * *





kaṭitta pāmpukku pālvārttāl viṣattait tarum atupōla pollātavarkkuc ceyyum upakāram irukkum.

When milk is placed before a biting snake, in return it gives poison, and such is the return for the favours done to the wicked.





* * *





kaṭitta mūṭṭaiyum cari, kaṭiyāta mūṭṭaiyum cari.

Bugs are all the same whether they bite or not.





* * *





kaṭitta nāykkuk kāṭiyaik koṭu.

Give vinegar to the dog that bit you.





* * *





kaṭittālum kaṭikkaṭṭum. nī kollātiru.

No matter if it bite you, do not kill it.





* * *





kaṭitta nāyaip payittiyam koṇṭatu eṉṟu colvārkaḷ.

They will call that a mad dog which has bitten some one.





* * *





kaṭinta collilum kaṉinta collē naṉmai.

A kind word is better than a harsh one.





* * *





kaṭiyāta mūṭṭaiyeṉṟu viṭṭu viṭuvārkaḷā?

Will they let a bug escape because it did not bite?





* * *





kaṭiyum cuṟukkuttāṉ aṭiyum cuṟukkuttāṉ.

Both the bite and the stroke were quickly effected.





* * *





kaṭukiccu muṭukiccu vaṭukacci kaliyāṇam.

The Vaduga woman's wedding is come nigh.





* * *





kaṭuku pōkiṟa iṭattil taṭi eṭuttukkoṇṭu tirivāṉ, pūcaṉikkāy pōkiṟa iṭam teriyātu.

He walks about with a staff in a place that will admit a mustard seed, and yet he is ignorant of the place where a pumpkin might easily pass.





* * *





kaṭuku ciṟuttālum kāram pōkātu.

Though small, the mustard seed is not without pungency.





* * *





kaṭuku pōṉa iṭam ārāyvār pūcaṉikkāy pōṉa iṭam teriyātu.

They search for a grain of mustard seed, but not for a missing pumpkin.





* * *





kaṭuku pōṉa iṭam ārāyvār miḷaku pōṉa iṭam ārāyār.

They search for a grain of mustard seed but not for a grain of pepper.





* * *





kaṭukuk kaḷavum kaḷavutāṉ karppūrak kaḷavum kaḷavutāṉ.

Theft is theft whether as regards a mustard seed or camphor.





* * *





kaṭukkāykku akaṇi nañcu, cukkiṟkup puṟaṇi nañcu.

The kernel of the gall-nut and the skin of ginger are poisonous.





* * *





kaṭuṅkāṟṟu maḻai kāṭṭum, kaṭuñciṉēkam pakai kāṭṭum.

Strong wind foretells rain, excessive friendship foreshows hatred.





* * *





kaṭuñciṉēkam kaṇṇukkup pakai.

Excessive intimacy will prove unfriendly to one’s eyes.





* * *





kaṭuñceṭṭuk kāriyakkēṭām.

It is said that hard dealing fails in its object.





* * *





kaṭuñceṭṭuk kaṇṇaik keṭukkum.

Unfair dealing destroys the eyes.





* * *





kaṭuñcoṟ kēṭṭāl kātukku aruvaruppu.

It is shocking to the ears to hear harsh words.





* * *





kaṭumuṭukkaṭā cēvakā kamparici aṭā campaḷam.

Peon, my fellow, you are too strict, you have kamboo rice only as wages.





* * *





kaṭumpaci kal matil uṭaittum kaḷavuceyyac collum.

Extreme hunger will induce a man to break through a stone wall and steal.





* * *





kaṭai keṭṭa vāḻvu talai kaṭṭa nēram illai.

Labouring in menial offices she has not leisure to put up her tresses.





* * *





kaṭaikkuk kaṭai āḷ iruppārkaḷ.

Each bazaar will have its attendant.





* * *





kaṭaicic cōṟṟukku mōrum, kāl māṭṭiṟkup pāyum vēṇṭum.

Buttermilk for the last distribution of rice, and a mat for the foot of the bed are indispensable.





* * *





kaṭaittēṅkāy eṭuttu vaḻip piḷḷaiyārukku uṭaittāṟpōla.

Like taking up a cocoanut in the bazaar and breaking it to Ganésa.





* * *





kaṭaiyilē koṇṭu maṉaiyilē vaikkiṟāṉ.

He buys in the bazaar, and bestows at home.





* * *





kaṭaiyil arici kañcikku utavumā, apicāri puruṣaṉ āpattukku utavuvāṉā?

Will the rice in the bazaar serve for kanji, will the paramour of an adulteress be of service to her in distress?





* * *





kaṭaiyil vantatum ariciyō naṭaiyil ventatum cātamō?

Is that which comes to the bazaar rice? Is boiled rice cooked by the way desirable?





* * *





kaṭaiyilē kaṭṭit tūkkiṉālum aḻukaṟ pūcaṇikkāy aḻukalē.

Although rotten pumpkins are tied up in the bazaar, they are still rotten.





* * *





kaṭṭappāraiyai viḻuṅkic cukkuk kaṣāyam kuṭittatupōla.

Like drinking a decoction of dry ginger after swallowing a crowbar.





* * *





kaṭṭāntarai aṭṭaipōla.

Like a wood-louse on dry ground.





* * *





kaṭṭikkoṇṭu varaccoṉṉāl veṭṭikkoṇṭu varuvāṉ.

When told to tie and bring, he cuts and brings.





* * *





kaṭṭi aḻukiṟapōtu kaiyum tuḻāvukiṟatē.

When she embraces and weeps, her hand is groping to steal.





* * *





kaṭṭil uḷḷa iṭattil piḷḷai peṟṟuc cukkuk kaṇṭa iṭattil kāyam tiṉpāḷ.

She brings forth her child where she sees a bedstead, and takes the prescribed tonic where she sees dry ginger.





* * *





kaṭṭi vaḻi viṭṭāl veṭṭi aracu āḷalām.

If he organize an army and lead it, he may cut down the enemy and reign as a king.





* * *





kaṭṭi aḻukaiyilē eṉ makaḷē uṉakkup peṭṭiyilē kai eṉṉa?

My daughter, why is your hand in the basket while you embrace and weep?





* * *





kaṭṭi aṭittāl eṉṉa viṭṭu aṭittāl eṉṉa?

It makes no difference whether you flog one bound or unbound.





* * *





kaṭṭiṉa vīṭṭukkup paḻutu colluvār palar.

Many will find fault with a house newly built.





* * *





kaṭṭiṉa vīṭṭukkuk karuttuc colluvār palar.

Many will express an opinion respecting a house just built.





* * *





kaṭṭiṉavaṉukku oru vīṭāṉāl, kaṭṭātavaṉukkup pala vīṭu.

He who has built a house has only one, whereas he who has not built makes use of many.





* * *





kaṭṭivaitta pūṉaiyai aviḻttu viṭṭu vāpūs vāpūs eṉṟāl varumā?

On untying a cat and calling puss, puss, will it come?





* * *





kaṭṭilait tiruppippōṭṭāl talaivali pōmā?

Will head-ache go by turning the bedstead?





* * *





kaṭṭivaitta paṇattait taṭṭi paṟittāṟpōla.

As if one displaced and carried away money carefully tied up.





* * *





kaṭṭi vitai veṭṭi vitai.

Provide seed-corn, and sow having tilled the ground.





* * *





kaṭṭu aṟinta nāyum alla, kaṉam aṟinta kapparaiyum alla.

He is not a dog accustomed to restraint, nor a mendicant’s dish that knows what self-respect is.





* * *





kaṭṭukkuk kaṭṭu māṟik kaṭṭavēṇṭum.

Every time you have to tie, you must tie it in a different way.





* * *





kaṭṭuppaṭṭālum kavarimāṉ mayirāl kaṭṭup paṭavēṇṭum, kuṭṭuppaṭṭālum mōtirak kaiyāl kuṭṭuppaṭa vēṇṭum.

When bound, it should be by the hair of the roe, and if cuffed, it should be by a hand ornamented with jewels.





* * *





kaṭṭuccōṟṟu mūṭṭaiyum iḷam piḷḷaiyum eṭuppatu varuttam.

It is difficult to carry, besides a child, boiled rice tied up for a journey.





* * *





kaṭṭaikkup pōkumpōtu kālāḻi pīliyā?

Are toe-rings necessary when a woman goes out to g the firewood?





* * *





kaṭṭōṭē pōṉāl kaṉattōṭa varalām.

If one sets out auspiciously, he may return with honour.





* * *





kaṇakkatikārattaip piṟakkum kōṭāvi.

He is an axe splitting the tree of arithmetic.





* * *





kaṇakkaṉ kaṇakku aṟivāṉ, taṉ kaṇakkait tāṉ aṟiyāṉ.

The accountant is clever at numbers, but he is ignorant of his own accounts.





* * *





kaṇakkuk kuñcaiyum kākkaik kuñcaiyum kaṇṭa iṭattilē kaṇṇaik kuttu.

Bore through the eyes of a young kurnum and a young crow wherever you find them.





* * *





kaṇakkaip pārttāl piṇakku varum.

When accounts are examined, difficulties arise.





* * *





kaṇakkaṉukkup paṭṭiṉi uṭaṉ piṟappu.

To suffer hunger is natural to an accountant.





* * *





kaṇakku aṟinta piḷḷai vīṭṭil iruntāl vaḻakku aṟātu.

There will be constant disputes in a house should one of its inmates be a skilful accountant.





* * *





kaṇakkaṉ vīṭṭuk kaliyāṇam viḷakkeṇṇeykkuk kēṭu.

A marriage ceremony in the house of an accountant is a waste of oil.





* * *





kaṇakkappiḷḷai ellām eḻuttuppiḷḷai alla.

All accountants are not learned.





* * *





kaṇapati pūcai kai mēlē payaṉ.

The pooja of Ganèsa has immediate effect.





* * *





kaṇavaṉaip pirintum ayal vīṭṭil irukkiṟatā?

What! in a neighbour's house, separated from your husband?





* * *





kaṇukkāl peruttāl kaṇavaṉait tiṉpāḷ.

If her ankle grow big, she will be deprived of her husband.





* * *





kaṇ kaṇṭatu kai ceyyum.

What the eye has seen the hand may do.





* * *





kaṇkuttippāmpupōlap pārttiruntēṉ.

I was waiting like an eye-snake.





* * *





kaṇ kuruṭṭukku maruntu iṭṭāl teḷiyumā?

Is blindness curable by an external application?





* * *





kaṇ kuruṭu āṉālum nittiraiyil kuṟaiyā?

Though blind, does he sleep the less?





* * *





kaṇ keṭṭa piṉpu cūriya namaskāram ceytu eṉṉa palaṉ?

What benefit can you expect from the worship of the sun if you have lost your eyesight?





* * *





kaṇ pāvaṉaiyāyk koṇṭai muṭikkiṟatu.

Tying one’s lock of hair in imitation of others’.





* * *





kaṇṭa kaṇṭa kōyil ellām kai eṭuttuk kumpiṭṭēṉ.

I worshipped with raised hands in every temple I visited.





* * *





kaṇṭatu pāmpu kaṭittatu māṅkoṭṭai.

That which was seen was a snake, that which bit was the stone of a mango fruit.





* * *





kaṇṭatu kāṭci peṟṟutu pēṟu.

Whatever is seen is a sight, whatever is received is a gift.





* * *





kaṇṭataik kēḷāviṭṭāl uṇṭu uṟaṅka māṭṭāṉ.

He will neither eat nor sleep without asking whatever he sees.





* * *





kaṇṭataik kēḷāviṭṭāl koṇṭavaṉ aṭippāṉ.

If she does not attend to the affairs of her family, her husband will beat her.





* * *





kaṇṭaṟiyātavaṉ peṇṭu paṭaittāl kāṭu mēṭu ellām iḻuttut tirivāṉām.

It is said that if an inexperienced man marries, he will wander about dragging his wife, through jungles and over hills.





* * *





kaṇṭataik kaṟkap paṇṭitaṉ āvāṉ.

By learning what he sees, a person becomes a pundit.





* * *





kaṇṭatu kēṭṭatu collātē kāṭṭumarattilē nillātē.

Never utter what you have seen and heard, nor stand under a wild tree.





* * *





kaṇṭatu pāmpu kaṭittatu karukkumaṭṭai.

That which he saw was a snake, but he was bit by the rough points of the palmyra stem.





* * *





kaṇṭataik kaṟṟukkoṇṭu karaiyēṟu

Acquire what you can, and go ashore.





* * *





kaṇṭavaṉ eṭāṉā?

Will he who finds, not take up?





* * *





kaṇṭāl teriyātā kampaḷiyāṭṭu mayir?

May not sheep-hair be known as soon as seen?





* * *





kaṇṭāl muṟai collukiṟatu kāṇāviṭṭāl peyar collukiṟatā?

If present, do you call mentioning the relationship of the party, and when absent, his name only?





* * *





kaṇṭāl āyam kāṇā viṭṭāl māyam.

If seen, duty, if not seen, fraud.





* * *





kaṇṭu cetta piṇam āṉāl cuṭu kāṭṭiṟku vaḻi teriyum.

If one die under human observation, his corpse will find its way to the place of cremation.





* * *





kaṇṭu pēcak kāriyam irukkiṟatu mukattil viḻakka veṭkamāy irukkiṟatu.

I have something to speak of in person, but I am ashamed to look at his face.





* * *





kaṇṭum kāṇavillai, kēṭṭum kēṭkavillaiyeṉṟu irukka vēṇṭum.

Behave as though seeing, you see not, and hearing, you hear not.





* * *





kaṇṭu eṭuttavaṉ koṭuppāṉā?

Will he who finds and takes a thing give it up?





* * *





kaṇṇāl kaṇṭataṟkuc cāṭciyum vēṇṭumā?

Is a witness needed to prove that which one has seen with his own eye?





* * *





kaṇṇāṟ kaṇṭatu poy akappaikkuṟi mey

That which one has seen is false, the sign of agappai is true.





* * *





kaṇṇāṟ kaṇṭataṟku ēṉ akappaikkuṟi?

Why the sign of an agappai in proof of that which one has seen?





* * *





kaṇṇārak kāṇātatu mūṉṟil oru paṅku.

That one has not seen with his own eye amounts to one third.





* * *





kaṇṇālē cīvaṉ kaṭakaṭa eṉṟu pōṉālum vaṇṇāṉ kaḻutai cumantē tīravēṇṭum.

The ass of a washerman must carry the pack through, though its life may depart with a rattling noise through the eyes.





* * *





kaṇṇāṉa pērkaḷai maṇṇākkukiṟāṉ.

He treats as common earth those who are precious as the eyes.





* * *





kaṇṇāṉa maṉataip puṇṇākkukiṟāṉ.

He wounds the heart which is as tender as the eye.





* * *





kaṇṇālē cīvaṉ kaṭakaṭaveṉṟu pōṉālum vaṇṇāṉukku maḻai nañcu.

Though the life of the washerman is forced through his eyes by reason of drought, rain to him would be poison.





* * *





kaṇṇāṭi niḻaliṟ kaṇṭa paṇam kaṭaṉukku utavumā?

Will the money reflected in a mirror avail for the payment of debt?





* * *





kaṇṇāṉa pērkaḷaip puṇṇākkiṉāy.

You have wounded your dearest friends.





* * *





kaṇṇimai kainoṭi aḷavē māttirai.

The twinkling of the eye, or the snapping of the fingers, is the measure of a moment of time.





* * *





kaṇṇiyil akappaṭṭa māṉpōl kalaṅkukiṟāṉ.

He is dismayed like a deer caught in a snare.





* * *





kaṇṇiyil akappaṭṭa karikkuruvipōl kalaṅkukiṟāṉ.

He is agitated as an ensnared blackbird.





* * *





kaṇṇiṟ paṭṭāṟ karikkumā, puruvattiṟ paṭṭāṟ karikkumā ?

When will it give pain, when it hits the eye or the eye-brow?





* * *





kaṇṇiṉāl eṇṇuvāṉ.

He can count with his eyes—at sight.





* * *





kaṇṇiṟ puṇ vantāl kaṇṇāṭi pārkkal ākātu.

When the eyes become sore, one cannot see them reflected in a mirror.





* * *





kaṇṇiṉmuṉṉē oṉṟu collik kāṇāta iṭattilē iraṇṭu collukiṟatu.

To assert a thing when in sight and to contradict it when out of sight.





* * *





kaṇṇilē eṇṇey paṭṭāṟ karikkumō piṭariyil paṭṭāl karikkumō ?

Will oil give pain when it falls into the eye, or on the back of the neck?





* * *





kaṇṇiṟ paṭṭa kaiyait taṟippār illai.

No one cuts off the hand because it has struck the eye.





* * *





kaṇṇilē paṭṭāl viralait taṟikkiṟatā ?

Should it strike the eye is the finger to be cut off.





* * *





kaṇṇilē varukiṟatu puruvattilē.

The eye brow has received that which threatened the eye.





* * *





kaṇṇil kaṇṭatu kōṭi kāṇātatu aṉantam kōṭi.

That which was seen was a crore, and that not seen many crores.





* * *





kaṇ uḷḷapōtē kāṭci.

One has the pleasure of seeing as long as the eyes are unimpaired.





* * *





kaṇ ūṉaṉ kaipporuḷ iḻappāṉ.

The blind will lose his wealth.





* * *





kaṇṇukkup puruvam kātāmā ?

Is the brow ten miles in advance of the eye?





* * *





kaṇṇukkup puṇṇum alla, kāṇpārkku nōyum alla.

It is neither an eye-sore, nor painful to beholders.





* * *





kaṇṇum karuttum uḷḷapōtē kāṇōm ataṉ piṉpu eṉṉa kiṭaikkum ?

We do not realize it when the eye and mind are unimpaired; what may we expect to gain afterwards?





* * *





kaṇṇukku imai kātāmā ?

Is the eye-lid far apart from the eye?





* * *





kaṇṇukkuṭ cammaṇam koṭṭuvāḷ kampattil aintāṉai kaṭṭuvāḷ.

She will sit in one's eye cross-legged, and tether five elephants to the pole of a dancer.





* * *





kaṇṇai imai kāttatupōl.

As the eye-lash preserved the eye.





* * *





kaṇṇaik keṭutta teyvam matiyaik koṭuttatu.

The deity that deprived him of sight gave him superior mental endowments.





* * *





kaṇṇaik koṇṭu naṭantatupōla uṉṉaik koṇṭu naṭantēṉ.

I guarded you as I did my own eyes.





* * *





kaṇṇaik kaṭṭik kāṭṭil viṭṭatupōla.

As one was left in the jungle blindfolded.





* * *





kaṇṇaik kāṭṭi aḻaittāl varātavaḷ kaiyaip piṭittu iḻuttāl varuvāḷā ?

Will she who would not come when called by the expression of your eyes, obey when taken by the hand?





* * *





kaṇṇaik keṭutta teyvam kōlaik koṭuttatu.

The deity that destroyed the eyes gave a supporting staff.





* * *





kaṇṇaimūṭik kuṭṭukiṟatā?

Is it to strike the head after covering the eyes?





* * *





kaṇṇō puṇṇō.

Is it an eye, or a sore?





* * *





kaṇ terintu naṭappavarkaḷ paḷḷattil viḻamāṭṭārkaḷ.

They who walk with their eyes open will not fall into the pit.





* * *





kaṇterintu vaḻi naṭakkumpaṭi niṉai.

Take care that you walk with your eyes open.





* * *





kaṇ pārttāl kai ceyyum.

If the eye have seen it, the hand may perform it.





* * *





katavaic cāttiṉāl nilai puṟampu.

When the door is shut, the door-frame is outside.





* * *





katiravaṉ cilaraik kāyēṉ eṉkumā ?

Will the sun say, I will not burn certain persons?





* * *





katiraik kaḷaintum kaḷaiyaip piṭuṅku.

Weed the corn even at the risk of destroying the ears.





* * *





katir nūl kuṟaintālum kaḷḷacci kaḻuttu nūl kuṟaiyātu.

Though the thread on the spindle diminishes, that on the neck of a kalla woman does not.





* * *





kataikkuk kālum illai talaiyum illai.

Mere rumours have neither head nor foot.





* * *





kataikkuk kāl illai pēykkup pātam illai.

Rumours have no legs, nor have demons feet.





* * *





kataiyō pirāmaṇā kantaiyō pottukiṟāy, allaṭī pēy muṇṭāy cīlaippēṉ kuttukiṟēṉ.

O brahman, do you repeat a story or mend old clothes? No, you foolish widow, I crush the lice in my clothes.





* * *





kattāikkāy cottai eṉṟāl aāivāḷ maṇaik kuṟṟam eṉkiṟāḷ.

When it is said that the brinjal is worm eaten, she imputes it to a defect in the knife.





* * *





kattarikkāyilē kālum kaiyum muḷaittāṟpōla.

As if feet and hands shot forth in the brinjal.





* * *





kattarikkāy virai curaikkāy kāykkātu.

The seed of a brinjal will not produce gourds.





* * *





kattarikkāy vāṅkap pūcaṇikkāy kocuṟā?

When you buy a brinjal, will the bazaar people give you a pumpkin in at the bargain?





* * *





kattiyum kaṭāvum pōlē.

Like a knife and a he-goat.





* * *





kattiyaip pārkkilum kaṉa kōpam kolai ceyyum.

Great anger is more destructive than the sword.





* * *





kattikaṭṭi peṇcāti eppōtum kaimpeṇcāti.

The wife of a swordsman is at any moment liable to becomes widow.





* * *





katti irukkum iṭattukku marai kāvukiṟatā?

Is the elk to be carried to the knife?





* * *





kattumaṭṭum kattippōṭṭuk katavaic cāttiviṭṭup pō

Bawl out as long as you like, and shut the door when you go.





* * *





kattu kattu eṉṟāl kaḻutaiyum kattātu collu collu eṉṟāl pulavaṉum collāṉ



When repeatedly urged to bray, even an ass will not do so, when asked to sing, even a poet will refuse.





* * *





kantapoṭik kaṭaikkāraṉukku vācaṉai teriyumā?

Does the perfumer appreciate the distinction of scents?





* * *





kantaikkut takka pontai.

The hole is in proportion to the rag.





* * *





kantaikkuc caraṭu ēṟukiṟatu ellām palam.

The more it is stitched and the stronger does the rag become.





* * *





kantai āṉālum kacakki uṭu, kūḻ āṉālum kuḷittuk kuṭi.

Though only a rag; wash it and put it on, and if but kanji, drink it after ablution.





* * *





kapaṭa iṉ colliṉum kaṭiya collē nalam.

Better is a harsh word than one smooth and feigned.





* * *





kapālak kuttu kaṇṇaic cuḻittatu.

Severe head-ache caused the eyes to sink.





* * *





kappalilē pātippākkup pōṭṭatupōlē.

Like dropping a bit of areca-nut in a ship.





* * *





kappal ēṟip paṭṭa kaṭaṉ koṭṭai nūṟṟuk tīrkkavēṇṭum.

Debt incurred by the sea-faring merchant must be discharged by spinning cotton.





* * *





kappal ōṭip paṭṭa kaṭaṉ koṭṭai nūṟṟut tīrumō?

Can a debt incurred on account of a sea-faring life be discharged by spinning?





* * *





kappaṟkāraṉ peṇṭāṭṭi toppaikkāri, kappal uṭaintāl piccaikkāri.

The ship owner's wife is in good condition as long as the ship is safe, but if that be lost she is a beggar.





* * *





kappaṟkāraṉ vāḻvu kāṟṟu aṭittāl pōccu.

The prosperity of the ship-owner is at the mercy of the wind.





* * *





kamariṟ kaviḻnta pāl amararkku iṭṭa aṉṉam.

Milk poured into a crevice, rice offered to enemies.





* * *





kampap piccaiyō kaṭaip piccaiyō?

Are not alms obtained by pole-dancing the lowest species of alms?





* * *





kampaḷiyilē ēṟṟacōṟṟai mayir mayir eṉkiṟatā?

Is it to say that the boiled rice received in a cumbly is full of hair?





* * *





kampaḷi viṟṟa paṇattukku mayir muḷaittu irukkiṟatā?

Does hair grow in the money for which cumblies have been sold?





* * *





kampaḷiyil oṭṭiṉa kūḻaippōla.

Like food sticking to a cumbly.





* * *





kampaḷimēl piciṉ.

Gum on a cumbly.





* * *





kampaṉ vīṭṭuk kaṭṭut taṟiyum kavi pāṭum.

Even the peg to which a cow is tied at Kamban’s house will sing.





* * *





kampukkuk kaḷai veṭṭiṉāṟpōlum irukkavēṇṭum, tampikkup peṇkoṇṭāṟpōlum irukkavēṇṭum.

Arrange it so that when he goes to weed the rye, he may also engage a wife for his younger brother.





* * *





kammāḷaṉ pacuvaik kātu aṟuttuk koḷḷavēṇṭum.

One must buy a smith’s cow after cropping her ears.





* * *





kammāḷaṉ vīṭṭil piḷḷai piṟantāl tēvaṭiyāḷ teruvil carkkarai vaḻaṅka vēṇṭum.

When a child is born in a smith’s family, sugar must be dealt out in the street of the dancing girls.





* * *





kammāḷaṉ nāy cammaṭṭit toṉikku añcumā?

Will the smith’s dog be alarmed at the sound of a hammer?





* * *





kayiṟu illā pamparampōla.

Like a top without a string.





* * *





kayiṟṟaip pāmpeṉṟu eṇṇik kalaṅkukiṟatu.

Trembling at a bit of rope thinking it to be a snake.





* * *





karakattu nīr kātaṅ kākkum.

Water in a hand-pot will avail for a katham.





* * *





karaṭi piṟai kaṇṭatu pōla.

As a bear saw the new moon.





* * *





karaṭi turattiṉālum kaikkōḷat teruvil pōka vaḻi irātu.

Though one be pursued by a bear, he may not escape through weavers’ street.





* * *





karaṭiyāl turattappaṭṭavaṉukkuk kampaḷikkāraṉaik kaṇṭāṟ payam.

He who has been kicked by a bear, fears the sight of one who sells cumblies.





* * *





karaṭi puṟṟilē vāy vaittāṟ pōl, karuṭaṉ kāliṟ cataṅkai kaṭṭiyatupōl.

As the bear puts its nose into the anthill, as small bells were tied to the legs of a kite.





* * *





karaṇam tappiṉāl maraṇam.

To fail in a somersault may be fatal.





* * *





karammāṟik kaṭṭiṉāl kaṉam kuṟaiyātu.

Excessive cording will not diminish the weight of a package.





* * *





karippukku iruntu piḻai, kalakattukku ōṭip piḻai.

In famine, save your life where you are, in war, by flight.





* * *





karippilē piḷḷai viṟṟāṟpōlē.

Like selling a child in famine.





* * *





karu illāta vittaiyum kuru illā cīṣaṉumpōl.

Like an art without ingenuity to comprehend it, and a disciple without a teacher.





* * *





karukki urukki neyvārttālum kaṇṭa niyāyantāṉ colluvāṉ.

Though you give him fried meat and pour melted ghee on his rice, he will say nothing but what he saw.





* * *





karuṅkaṇṇi paṭṭāl karaiyāṉ kaṇṇālum tirumpip pārāṉ.

When the karunkanni fish is caught, the fisherman does not even turn to look at it.





* * *





karuṅkallilē nār urippāṉ.

He will strip fibre from a piece of granite.





* * *





karuṅkāli ulakkaikku veḷḷip pūṇ kaṭṭiṉatupōla.

As a silver ferrule fastened to an ebony pestle.





* * *





karuṅkālik kaṭṭaikku vāy nāṇāk kōṭāli iṉa vāḻait taṇṭukku vāy nāṇumā?

Will an axe whose edge is not blunted by cutting a piece of ebony, be made dull by cutting a stem of a plantian tree?





* * *





karuṭaṉ kālil keccai kaṭṭiṉatupōla.

As a string of small bells was tied to the leg of a kite.





* * *





karuṭaṉaik kaṇṭa pāmpupōla.

Like a snake in sight of a kite.





* * *





karuppaṅkaṭṭiyilum kallu irukkum.

Stones may be found even in sugar candy.





* * *





karuma oḻuṅku perumaikku aḷavu.

Consistency of action is the measure of greatness.





* * *





karumattai muṭikkiṟavaṉ arumai oṉṟum pārāṉ.

A resolute man cares nothing about difficulties.





* * *





karumāṉ irunta iṭamum kaḻutai puraṇṭa kaḷamum cari.

A blacksmith’s shop, and the place in which asses roll themselves are alike.





* * *





karumpilum tēṉ irukkum kaḷḷiyilum pāl irukkum.

There is honey in sugarcane, there is sap in milk-hedge.





* * *





karumpukkaṭṭikku eṟumpu tāṉē varum.

Ants will of course be attracted by a lump of sugar-candy.





* * *





karumpu muṟittu kaḻutaiyai aṭitta katai.

The story of one striking an ass with a sugar-cane.





* * *





karumpaik kaḻutaikkumuṉ pōṭṭāl kaḻutaikku teriyumō karumpu ruci.

Does the ass enjoy the flavor of the sugar-cane that is placed before it?





* * *





karumpu tiṉṉak kūliyā?

What, a reward for eating sugar-cane?





* * *





karumpum vēmpu āyiṟṟē.

Even sugar cane has become a margosa tree.





* * *





karumpukku uḻuta puḻuti kāycciya pālukkuc carukkarai āmā?

Will earth pulverised by the plough for sugar-cane, answer for sugar to be mixed with boiled milk?





* * *





karumpu kacakkiṟatu vāykkuṟṟam.

If sugar-cane prove bitter to the palate, it is the fault of the mouth.





* * *





karumpu ruciyeṉṟu vērōṭu piṭuṅkalāmā?

May sugar-cane be plucked up by the root because it is of fine flavor?





* * *





karumpai virumpa, atu vēmpu āyiṟṟu.

If taken to excess sugar-cane becomes bitter as margosa.





* * *





karumpu kaṭṭōṭē irukka eṟumpu tāṉē varum.

Where bundles of sugar-cane are, there ants will come of themselves.





* * *





karuvu illāta muṭṭaiyum kuru illāta vittaiyum.

An egg without yolk, an art without a teacher.





* * *





karuvēlamarattiṟku niḻal illai, kaṉṉāṉukku muṟai illai.

The karuvél Acasia casts no shade, smiths observe no relationship in marrying.





* * *





karai kāṇāta tōṇipōla.

As a doney out of sight of land.

The common name of a country vessel, some of which are of considerable size. They have no deck and are thatched with palm leaves.





* * *





karai taṭṭi kappalpōla.

Like a stranded vessel.





* * *





karaippakkam pātai irukkak kappal ēṟiṉavaṉum, collātatai maṉaiyāḷukkuc coṉṉavaṉum paṭṭa pāṭupōla.

Like the misery endured by him who embarked when there was a way by land, and by the husband who revealed to his wife that which he ought not.





* * *





karaippār karaittāṟ kallum karaiyum.

Under the management of the skilful, even a stone may be dissolved.





* * *





kalakattilē puḷukātavaṉ narakattilē pōvāṉām.

It is said that he who does not exaggerate when a tumult occurs will go to hell.





* * *





kalakattilē pōyum kālmāṭu talaimāṭā?

In regard to a bed is the distinction of head and foot observed in a battle-field?





* * *





kalakam kalantāl ulakam kalaṅkum.

In a general insurrection universal consternation prevails.





* * *





kalakam piṟantāl niyāyam piṟakkum.

Popular agitation leads to justice.





* * *





kalakamē meyyāṉāṟ puḷukātavaṉ pāvam .

If an insurrection exists, it is wrong not to make it known.





* * *





kalakkantaik kaṭṭik kāṇap pōṉāl irukkalak kantai kaṭṭi etirē vantāḷ.

When I went in rags to see her, she appeared enveloped in double the quantity.





* * *





kalak kampu tiṉṟālum kāṭai kāṭṭilē.

Though it may consume a kalam of gtain, the quail lives in the jungle.





* * *





kalakkiṉum kaṭal cēṟākātu.

Though never so troubled, the sea does not become muddy.





* * *





kalattilē cōṟṟai iṭṭuk kaiyaip piṭittāṟpōla.

Like holding the hand after putting rice on the plate.





* * *





kalak nellukkuk kala umi irukkumā?

Will a kalam of paddy yield a kalam of husks?





* * *





kalap paṇattaip pārkkilum oru kiḻap piṇam nallatu.

The corpse of an old person is better than immense wealth.





* * *





kalap pāl kaṟakkalām tuḷip pāl mulaikku ēṟṟalāmā?

A kalam of milk may be drawn; can a drop be infused into the teat?





* * *





kalap pālukkut tuḷip purai.

A drop of butter-milk or curds to a kalam of milk.





* * *





kalappāṉālum pūcapūcap poṉṉiṟam.

Though not pure, repeated gilding will give it the colour of gold.





* * *





kalap pālil oru tuḷi viṣam kalantāṟpōlē

Like putting a drop of poison in a kalam of milk.





* * *





kalap pāl kuṭitta pūṉai āḻākkup pāl kuṭiyātā?

Will not a cat that has drunk a kalam of milk drink an álak?





* * *





kala mā iṭittavaḷ pāviyum, kappi iṭittavaḷ puṇṇiyavatiyumā?

Is she who pounded a kalam of flour vicious, and she who pounded rolong virtuous?





* * *





kalam kalantāl kulam kalakkum.

When plates are interchanged, tribal marriages interchange.





* * *





kalam kuttiṉālum patar arici ākātu.

Though a kalam of chaff be pounded, it will not become rice.





* * *





kala umi tiṉṟāl oru arici taṭṭātā?

If a kalam of chaff be eaten may hot a grain of rice turn up?





* * *





kaliyāṇac cantaṭiyil tāli kaṭṭa maṟantāṉām.

It is said that he forgot to tie the táli owing to the bustle at the wedding.





* * *





kaliyāṇattilum pañcam illai, kaḷattilum pañcam illai.

No scarcity at a wedding, nor in the threshing floor.





* * *





kaliyāṇa vīṭṭil pantaṟkālaik kaṭṭi aḻukiṟavaḷ cetta vīṭṭil cummā iruppāḷā?

Will the woman who wept as she clung to the post of a marriage pandal at be silent when she attends a funeral?





* * *





kaliyāṇa vīṭṭiṟkup pōy aṟiyāṉ, mēḷac cattamum kēṭṭum aṟiyāṉ.

He has never been at a wedding, nor has he heard the sound of a tom-tom.





* * *





kaliyāṇam ceytum caṉṉiyāciyā?

What, though married yet a Sanyási-ascetic?





* * *





kal ākilum kaṇavaṉ, pul ākilum puruṣaṉ.

Though in heart hard as a stone, and worthless as a blade of grass, he is your husband.





* * *





kal eṟikkut tappiṉālum kaṇ eṟikkut tappakkūṭātu.

Though one may escape the cast of a stone, he cannot escape the glance of an evil eye.

The evil eye kaṇṇūṟu and the evil tongue nāvūṟu are feared very generally. To avert their influence as regards fruits &c, in a garden, it is common to put up some object of attraction as a water-pot whitewashed, inverted on a stump, and dotted with black spots &c. In some enclosures grotesque images are seen.





* * *





kallaṭiccittaṉ pōkiṟa vaḻi, kāṭu mēṭu ellām taviṭu poṭi.

All the rough and rugged ground before the stone-cutter will be reduced to powder.





* * *





kallāṭam paṭittavaṉōṭu mal āṭātē.

Do not contend with him who has studied kalladam.

Kalladam a work on the third part of grammar is said to be extremely dry.





* * *





kallātār celvattilum kaṟṟār vaṟumai nalam.

The poverty of the learned is better than the wealth of the unlearned.





* * *





kallāta oruvaṉ kulanalam pēcutal nelluṭaṉ piṟanta patarākummē.

The illiterate who boast of high rank may be compared to the dust mixed with paddy-unhusked rice.





* * *





kallār uṟavilum kaṟṟōr pakai nalam.

The enmity of the learned is better than the friendship of the illiterate.





* * *





kallār uṟavakal kāmak kaṭal kaṭa.

Keep at a distance from the unlettered; cross the sea of passion.





* * *





kallilum vaṉmai kaṉamūṭar neñcam.

The heart of a great fool is harder than a stone.





* * *





kallilē nār urikkiṟatu pōla.

Like stripping fibre from a stone.





* * *





kallum tēṅkāyumpōlap pēcukiṟāṉ

He speaks as effectively as the hitting of a stone on a cocoanut.





* * *





kallum kāvēriyum uḷḷamaṭṭum vāḻka.

May you live as long as the rocks and the Cauveri exist.

The river Cauveri rises in the western ghauts and flows into the bay of Bengal. It is the source of the great fertility of Tanjore and the adjacent districts. According to the legend it originated in the following manner. In ancient times when the people of southern India suffered from drought, Ganésa became incarnate in the form of a crow. As such he visited the abode of an ascetic on one of the western hills. He there alighted on the waterpot of a hermit and upset it. The water thus spilt, by reason of the hermit’s merit, became a river: the current spelling would make the name the lake of the crow: the legend requires the word to be spelt kāvēri crow mounting, whereas kāvēri lake of the crow is common.





* * *





kallu varukiṟa vicaiyaik kaṇṭāl pallaic cikkeṉa mūṭavēṇṭum.

On seeing the rapid approach of a stone press your teeth and shut your mouth.





* * *





kallai ākilum karaikkalām, mūrkkaṉ maṉataik karaikkal ākātu.

Even stones may be dissolved, but the mind of a fool cannot.





* * *





kallaik kaṇṭāl nāyaik kāṇōm nāyaik kaṇṭāl kallaik kāṇōm .

On finding a stone we see no dog, on seeing a dog we find no stone.





* * *





kallaik kuttuvāṉēṉ kai nōkiṟatu eṉṟu aḻuvāṉēṉ?

Why beat a stone and then weep, exclaiming it has pained my hand?





* * *





kallaippōlak kaṇavaṉ irukkak kañcikku aḻuvāṉēṉ?

Why weep for kanji while you have a husband as hard as a stone?





* * *





kallaik kiḷḷi kai iḻantatu pōlām.

Like losing the hand by pinching a stone.





* * *





kalvi illāṉ celvam kaṟpillāḷ aḻaku.

Wealth without learning is like beauty without chastity.





* * *





kalvi eṉṟa payirukkuk kaṇṇīr eṉṟa maḻaivēṇṭum.

Plants of learning flourish in the rain of tears.





* * *





kalviyum kulamum velvatu viṉavil.

When inquiring what will prevail, one’s learning and rank naturally occur.





* * *





kalvi uḷḷa iḷaiñaṉ kaṉa kiḻavaṉ.

A well instructed youth is an old man.





* * *





kalviyaip paṅku pōṭa ēlātu.

Learning cannot be allotted in portions-to persons:





* * *





kavaṇeṟi neṟiyil nillātē,kaṇṭavaṉ talaiyai uṭaikkum.

Do not stand in the way of a stone from a sling, it may break the head of the beholder.





* * *





kavi koṇṭārkkuk kīrtti, ataic cevikoḷḷārkku avakīrtti.

Praise to those who approve of a poem, and blame to those who will not listen to it.





* * *





kavikkoṇṭārkkum kīrtti kalaippārkkum kīrttiyā?

Are those who accept a poem and those who reject it alike praiseworthy?





* * *





kavintirāṇam kajēntirāṇam rājāvē niṟpayē vaki.

O king, manage a poet laureate and a superior elephant without fear.





* * *





kavaiyaippaṟṟik kaḻutaiyiṉ kālaip piṭi.

To effect your object, if necessary, cling to the legs of an ass.





* * *





kaḻutaiyaik kāṇavēṇṭiṉāl kuṭṭic cuvarilē kāṇalām.

If an ass be wanted, it may be found feeding near a ruinous wall.





* * *





kaḻutaik keṭṭāl kuṭṭiccuvar.

If an ass be out of condition, it will be as useless as a ruinous wall.





* * *





kaḻutaikku ēṉ kaṭivāḷam?

Why a bridle for an ass?





* * *





kaḻutaip puṇṇukku puḻuti maruntu.

Dust is medicine for the sores of an ass.





* * *





kaḻutaiyum kutiraiyum cari āmā?

Are an ass and a horse equal?





* * *





kaḻutaiyum kutiraiyum piṇaittāṟpōla .

Like yoking an ass and a horse together.





* * *





kaḻutai aṟiyumā kantappoṭi vācaṉai?

Can an ass appreciate fragrant powder?





* * *





kaḻutaikkuc cēṇam kaṭṭiṉālum kutirai āmā?

Does an ass become a horse by being saddled?





* * *





kaḻutai tēyntu kaṭṭeṟumpu āyiṟṟu.

The ass by wearing away has become an ant.





* * *





kaḻutaikku ēṉ kaviṉam, allatu kaṭivāḷam?

Why a bit or a bridle for an ass?





* * *





kaḻutaiyaik kaṭṭi ōmam vaḷarttatu pōla.

Like tying an ass and feeding a sacrificial fire.

This proverb relates to the following story. A certain Maharàja had a barber who had evinced great skill in shaving hie royal master when he was asleep. To reward him the King resolved to elevate him in the social scale. The brahmans were called and ordered to make a brahman of the barber. They set to work, kindled a sacred fire and the barber was led round it, whilst the priests uttered the required incantations for accomplishing their purpose.

The prime minister being of a somewhat facetious temperament determined to metamorphose an ass. As the proverb intimates he kindled a fire and led the animal round it. When the King saw him thus engaged he asked him what he was doing. The prime minister replied by stating that if the brahmans could make a brahman of a barber he thought an ass might be made into a horse, and he was therefore engaged in the ceremony.





* * *





kaḻutaikku upatēcam kātilē coṉṉālum apayak kural aṉṟi aṅku oṉṟum illai .

Though religious instruction be whispered in the ears of an ass nothing will come of it but the accustomed braying.





* * *





kaḻutaikku vāḻkkaippaṭṭu utaikku añcalāmā?

Having married an ass, do you fear his kicking?





* * *





kaḻutai mēl ēṟi eṉṉa, iṟaṅki eṉṉa?

What matters it whether you mount an ass, or alight from one?





* * *





}kaḻutaimēl ēṟiyum perumai illai, iṟaṅkiyum ciṟumai illai.

Though you may ride on an ass, you gain no honour, nor are you disgraced by dismounting.





* * *





kaḻutai uḻutu vaṇṇāṉ kuṭi āṉāṉā?

Did the washerman become a farmer by ploughing with an ass?





* * *





kaḻutai uḻutu kuṟavaṉ kuṭi āṉāṉā?

Has the forester become a farmer by ploughing with an ass?





* * *





kaḻutai mayir piṭuṅkiṉāl ilāpam eṉṉa naṣṭam eṉṉa?

What profit or loss will arise from plucking off the hair of an ass?





* * *





kaḻutai mayir piṭuṅkit tēcam kaṭṭi āḷvāṉā?

Can one reign as a king by selling ass-hair?





* * *





kaḻutaikkut teriyumā kastūri vācaṉai?

Does an ass appreciate the odour of musk?





* * *





kaḻutaikkup paratēcam kuṭṭiccuvar

The foreign country of an ass is a ruinous wall.





* * *





kaḻutai tappiṉāl kuṭṭiccuvariṭattil irukkum

If an ass goes astray, it may be found near a ruinous wall.





* * *





kaḻutaippotiyil aiṅkala māṟāṭṭamā?

Are there five kalams of fraud in the pack on an ass?





* * *





kaḻutai viṭṭai āṉālum kai nirampa vēṇṭum eṉkiṟāy.

You say get a handful, although it be but ass dung.





* * *





kaḻuttilē karimaṇi illai peyar muttumālai.

Her name is pearl necklace, yet on her neck she has not a black bead.





* * *





kaḻuvik kaḻuvip piṉṉum cēṟṟai mitikkiṟatā?

What! is it to tread in the mud every time you wash your feet?





* * *





kaḻuvukku ēṟṟa kōmuṭṭi.

A kómutti exactly fitted to the stake.





* * *





kaḷam cāṟukiṟavaḷai miraṭṭuvāṉām pōr piṭuṅkukiṟavaṉ.

It is said that he who steals grain from the stack, will frighten away the women who may sweep the threshing floor.





* * *





kaḷaiyak kūṭātataik kaṇṭāl aṭi peyarntu appuṟam pō.

If you cannot pluck up, pass by on the other side.





* * *





kaḷaiyak kūṭātataik kaṇṭiyāmal cakittukkoḷ.

Do not fret yourself about that which cannot be avoided.





* * *





kal viṟṟuk kalappaṇam campātippatai viṭa kaṟpūram viṟṟuk kāṟpaṇam campātippatu mēl.

It is better to gain a quarter of a fanam by selling camphor than a kalam by selling toddy.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉum āki viḷakkum piṭikkiṟāṉ.

He plays the thief and also holds the lamp.





* * *





kaḷḷa vicuvācam kaḻuttellām cepamālai.

The neck of the hypocrite is covered with rosaries.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉaiyum veḷḷa aṇaiyum kaṭṭiviṭu.

Secure a thief by tying, and a flood by damming it up.





* * *





kaḷḷap piḷḷaiyilum cellappiḷḷai uṇṭā?

Are there any indulged ones among the children of kallars?





* * *





kaḷḷaṉai āru naḷḷār eṉṟum.

No one will ever approve of a thief.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉukku ūrellām viḷḷāp pakai.

The whole country owes implacable hatred to a thief.





* * *





kaḷḷa māṭu cantai ēṟātu.

A vicious bullock will not be brought to market.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉai nampiṉālum kuḷḷaṉai nampalākātu.

A rogue may be trusted, but not a dwarf.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ piṉ pōṉālum kuḷḷaṉ piṉpōkappaṭātu.

One may follow a rogue without risk, but not a dwarf.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ accam kāṭu koḷḷātu.

The jungle is not large enough to contain the fear of rubbers.





* * *





kaḷḷa māṭu tuḷḷum.

An unwilling bullock is restless.





* * *





kaḷḷa maṉam tuḷḷum.

A guilty mind is agitated.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ uṟavu uṟavum alla, kācā viṟaku viṟakum alla.

The friendship of a rogue is not friendship, nor is kásáwood fuel.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ putti tiruṭṭumēlē

The mind of a rogue is set on thieving.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ piḷḷaikkum kaḷḷap putti.

Even the child of a thief is characterized by thievish propensites.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ maṉaivi kaimpeṇ eṉṟum.

The wife of a thief is at any moment liable to become a widow.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ maṉaiyāḷaik kaḷavup poruḷaik kāṇum kuṟi kēṭkalāmā?

Can the wife of a thief be consulted as an augur for the recovery of stolen property?





* * *





kaḷḷaṉum veḷḷamum oṉṟu.

A thief and a flood are alike.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉukkup pāti veḷḷaṉukkup pāti.

One half to the rogue, and one half to the honest.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉum tōṭṭakkāraṉum oṉṟu.

A gardener is another term for a thief.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉum tōṭṭakkāraṉum oṉṟu kūṭiṉāl viṭiyumaṭṭum tiruṭalām.

If the thief and a gardener are united, one may steal till day-break.





* * *





kaḷḷaṉ koṇṭa māṭu ettuṟai pōyeṉṉa?

What matters it to what ferry the bull may go that has been bought by a thief?





* * *





kaḷḷaṉukkuk kāṇpittavaṉ pakai.

He who points out a thief is hated by him.





* * *





kaḷḷi peruttu eṉṉa? kāy uṇṭā niḻal uṇṭā?

What if the kalli grow large? Has it any fruit or any shade?





* * *





kaḷḷikku nāṭu ellām kāṭu.

The whole country is a wild fit for the growth of the kalli.





* * *





kaḷḷik kompukku veḷḷippūṇ kaṭṭiṉatupōla.

Like attaching a silver ferrule to a kalli stick.





* * *





kaḷḷi eṉṉaṭi kal iḻaippatu? kātil iruppatu pittaḷai.

Why you hypocrite, inlay with gems? that which is in the ear is made of brass.





* * *





kaḷḷikkum veṭṭik kaṟṟāḻaikkum veṭṭukiṟatu.

After cutting the kalli to cut the kattalai.





* * *





kaḷḷikkut taṇṇīr kaṇṇilē, nīlikkut taṇṇīr nimaiyilē.

The tears of a thievish woman are in her eyes, those of an abandoned woman are in her eyebrows.





* * *





kaḷḷikku kalanīr kaṇṇilē.

A thievish woman has a kalam of tears in her eyes.





* * *





kaḷḷikku vēli ēṉ, cuḷḷikku kōṭāli ēṉ?

What avails a fence against a thievish woman, or an axe for cutting twigs?





* * *





kaḷḷum cūtum irukkiṟa iṭattil kūttik kaḷḷaṉum iruppāṉ.

Where there is theft and gambling, there will also be a whore-monger.





* * *





kaḷḷaik kuṭittāl uḷḷataic colvāṉ.

Should he drink toddy, he will tell the truth.





* * *





kaṟakkiṟatu uḻakkup pāl, utaikkiṟatu pallup paṟakka.

Yielding an ulak of milk, and kicking out the teeth.





* * *





kaṟanta pālum eccil piṟanta piḷḷaiyum eccil.

First drawn milk is unclean, and so is a first-born child.





* * *





kaṟanta pāl mulaikku ēṟumā?

Will the milk that has been drawn, again enter the udder?





* * *





kaṟanta pālaik kāyccāmaṟ kuṭittāl kāca viyāti tāṉē eṭupaṭum.

If fresh drawn milk unboiled be drunk, consumption may be cured.





* * *





kaṟuttatu ellām taṇṇīr veḷuttatu ellām pāl eṉkiṟāṉ.

Whatever is black he calls water, and whatever is white he calls milk.





* * *





kaṟuppu veḷuppu ākātu, kacappu iṉippu ākātu.

Black will not become white, nor bitter sweet.





* * *





kaṟuppu nāy veḷḷai nāy ākumā?

Will a black dog become white?





* * *





kaṟumuṟukāntap paṭalam vācikkiṟār kavirāyar.

The poet is reading the section on grumbling.





* * *





kaṟaiyāṉ puṟṟil aravam kuṭikoṇṭatupōla.

Like a snake that occupied an ant-hill.





* * *





kaṟaiyāṉ puṟṟup pāmpukku utavukiṟatu.

An ant-hill is useful to a snake.





* * *





kaṟkaṇṭāṟ ceyta eṭṭikkaṉi kacakkumā?

Will etti fruit made of sugar-candy taste bitter?





* * *





kaṟkaiyil kalvi kacappu, kaṟṟapiṉpu atuvē iṉippu.

Learning when being acquired is bitter, but when possessed it is sweet.





* * *





kaṟpakataruvaic cārnta kākamum amutam uṇṇum.

Even a crow that lives in the kalpaka tree feeds on ambrosia.





* * *





kaṟpittavaṉ kaṇṇaik koṭuttavaṉ.

He who gave the eye is he who taught.





* * *





kaṟpittavaṉ kāppāṟṟuvāṉ.

He who preordained will protect or guide.





* * *





kaṟpillā aḻaku vācaṉai illāp pū.

Beauty without chastity, a flower without fragrance.





* * *





kaṟṟa iṭattilā vittaiyaik kāṭṭukiṟatu?

To practise a trick on him who taught it?





* * *





kaṟṟavaratu kōpam nīriṉ piḷavupōl māṟum.

The anger of the learned passes away as water recovers its surface after being divided.





* * *





kaṟṟatu kaiyaḷavu kallātu ulakaḷavu.

What he has learnt is a handful, and what he has still to acquire is wide as the world.





* * *





kaṟṟatu collāṉ maṟṟu eṉṉa ceyvāṉ?

What other service will he do for you who would not tell you what he has learnt?





* * *





kaṟṟavaṉukku vittai kāl nāḻi.

To the artist his art is but a quarter of a náli.





* * *





kaṟṟa vittaiyaik kāyccik kuṭikkiṟavaṉpōlē.

Like one who boils and drinks his learning.





* * *





kaṟṟaṟi mōḻaiyāy irātē.

Be not a learned fool.





* * *





kaṟṟavaṉum uṇpāṉ, peṟṟavaḷum uṇpāḷ.

A learned man and a fruitful woman will not suffer from want of food.





* * *





kaṟṟāḻai nāṟṟamum pittaḷai vīccamum pōkātu.

The ill odour of the karráli and the bad smell emitted from brass will not leave them.





* * *





kaṟṟāḻai ciṟuttālum āṉai aṭi vaikkātu.

Though the karràlai be small, the elephant will not tread upon it.





* * *





kaṟṟuk koṭutta pēccum kaṭṭik koṭutta cōṟum ettaṉai nāḷ niṟkum.

How long will words put into one’s mouth, and rice prepared for a journey, last?





* * *





kaṟṟuk kaṟṟup pēcātē.

Do not speak in a studied way.





* * *





kaṟṟum kaṟṟaṟimōḻai.

Though he has learnt, he continues a learned fool.





* * *





kaṟṟum kaṟṟaṟimōḻaiyā?

What, to be a learned fool?





* * *





kaṟṟōr arumai kaṟṟōr aṟivar.

The learned know the worth of the learned.





* * *





kaṉa ācai kaṉa naṣṭam.

Excessive desire entails great loss.





* * *





kaṉa eli vaḷai eṭātu.

When rats are numerous, they do not burrow.





* * *





kaṉaka māri poḻintatupōlē.

As it rained gold.





* * *





kaṉañ ceytāl iṣṭam, kaṉavīṉattāl naṣṭam.

Dignity gains approval, meanness entails loss.





* * *
