kaṉatta uṭaimaikku aṉarttam illai.

Solid jewels are invaluable.





* * *





kaṉattāl iṉamākum paṇattāl caṉamākum.

Dignity or worth increases relatives, wealth secures society.





* * *





kaṉattiṟku naṟkuṇam cumaitāṅki.

Virtue is the support of dignity.





* * *





kaṉattaik kaṉam aṟiyum karuvāṭṭup pāṉaiyai nāy aṟiyum.

Dignity is appreciated by dignity, the pot of dried fish is known to the dog.





* * *





kaṉa mūṭaṉ kaipporuḷ iḻappāṉ.

A great fool loses his substance.





* * *





kaṉavilum kākkaikku malam tiṉkiṟatē niṉaivu.

Even in its dreams the crow’s thoughts turn on eating filth.





* * *





kaṉavil kaṇṭa paṇam celaviṟku utavumā?

Will the money seen in a dream meet one’s expenses?





* * *





kaṉavil kaṇṭa poruḷ kāṉalil kaṇṭa puṉal.

The wealth seen in a dream, the water observed in a mirage.





* * *





kaṉavil kaṇṭa cōṟu paci tīrkkumā?

Will the rice consumed in a dream appease hunger?





* * *





kaṉavilē kaṇṭavaṉukkup peṇ koṭutta katai.

The story of a woman given in marriage to a man seen in a dream.





* * *





kaṉinta paḻam tāṉē viḻum.

Ripe fruit falls of itself.





* * *





kaṉinta paḻam nīr tiṉṟīr kāyai ulukkiviṭṭīr.

You have eaten the ripe fruit, and you have shaken off the unripe.





* * *





kaṉiyai viṭṭuk kāyait tiṉkiṟatā?

What, to leave ripe fruit and to eat unripe?





* * *





kaṉmattiṉāṟ cātiyaṉṟic ceṉmattiṉāl illai.

Caste arises from action, it is not from birth.





* * *





kaṉamaḻai peytālum karuṅkal karaiyumā?

Will granite be dissolved by any amount of hail?





* * *





kaṉmattiṉāl vantatu taṉmantiṉāl pōkavēṇṭum.

That which has come of action-sin-must go by alms giving religious merit.





* * *





kaṉṟu irukkak kācattaṉai pāl kaṟavāp pacu, kaṉṟu cetta piṟaku kalappāl kaṟakkumā?



Will a cow that gave milk worth a small coin while its calf lived, give a kalam after her calf is dead?





* * *





kaṉṟukkuṭṭiyai aviḻkkac coṉṉārkaḷā kaṭṭuttaṟiyaip piṭuṅkac coṉṉārkaḷā?



Did they tell you to loose the calf or to pull up the peg?





* * *





kaṉṟum pacuvum kāṭēṟi mēyntāl kaṉṟu kaṉṟu kaṉṟuvaḻiyē pacu pacu vaḻiyē

When the calf and cow go out to graze, each takes its own way.





* * *





kaṉṟukaḷāyk kūṭik kaḷai paṟikkap pōṉāl vaikkōl ākumā cettai ākumā?

If a herd of calves weed the corn, will there be any straw, or even dry stubble?





* * *





kaṉṟaik kaṇṭu ōṭivarum pacuvaippōlē.

Like a cow that comes running on seeing her calf.





* * *





kaṉṉāṉ naṭamāṭak kuyavaṉ kuṭipōvāṉ.

When the brazier begins to move, the potter will abandon his dwelling-place.





* * *





kaṉṉāṉukku muṉṉē kaḻutai paratēcam pōṉāṟpōlē.

As an ass went on a pilgrimage before a copper-smith.





* * *





kaṉṉi irukkak kāḷai maṇam pōkalāmā?

When a virgin is yet unmarried, may the youth-her brother-marry?





* * *





kā.



kākattilē veḷḷai uṇṭā?

Are there any white crows?





* * *





kākattiṉ kaṇṇukkup pīrkkampū poṉ niṟam.

To the eye of a crow the flower of the gourd is tinged with gold.





* * *





kākam kar eṉṟāl kaṇavaṉai appā eṉṟu kaṭṭikkoḷḷuvāḷām.

It is said that when a crow caws she will exclaim, alas, and cling to her husband.





* * *





kākam ēṟip paṉaṅkāy utirumā?

Will palmyra fruit fall because a crow alights on the tree?





* * *





kākam illāta ūr pāvi illāta ūr.

Vicious persons are not found in a village which has no crows.





* * *





kākam irukkap paḻam viḻuntatupōla.

Like the dropping of the fruit when a crow alighted on the tree.





* * *





kākam illāta ūr cōṉakaṉ illāta ūr.

The country in which crows do not exist is without mussalmans.





* * *





kākam kālamē kā kā eṉṟu kattum.

Crows will caw at day-break.





* * *





kākkaṉukkum pūkkaṉukkum pūttāyō puṉṉai kaṇṇāḷar varuntaṉaiyum poṟukkalaiyō puṉṉai?

O punnai tree, Alexandrian laurel, hast thou blossomed for Kakan and Pookan, couldst not thou have waited till the arrival of my husband?





* * *





kākkāyk kuñcu āṉālum taṉ kuñcu poṉ kuñcu.

Though but a young crow, it is a golden one to its mother.





* * *





kākkaiyiṟ karitu kaḷāmpaḻam.

Kalà fruit, carissa, is blacker than a crow.





* * *





kākkaiyum kuyiṟ kuñcait taṉ kuñcupōl vaḷarkkum.

Even a crow will bring up a young kuyil-Indian cuckoo-as her own.





* * *





kākkaikku añcu kuṇam.

The crow has five characteristics.





* * *





kākkai ēṟiṉatum paṉampaḻam viḻuntatum.

The alighting of the crow, and the falling of the palmyra fruit.





* * *





kākkaiyum kattik kattip pōkiṟatu karuvāṭum ularntu pōkiṟatu.

The crow continues to caw whilst the karuvadu, (salted fish) dries.





* * *





kākkaiyum kāṟṟum pōkku uṇṭāṉāl varum.

Crows and winds go but to return.





* * *





kākkaiyaik kaṇṭu añcuvāḷ karaṭiyaip piṭittuk kaṭṭuvāḷ.

She is afraid on seeing a crow, and yet she will capture a bear.





* * *





kākkai ēṟiṉa kompu acaiyātā?

Will not the branch shake on which a crow alights?





* * *





kāci iraṇṭu eḻuttuttāṉ kāṇa ettaṉai nāṭcellum?

Kàsi is formed of two letters only, how many days will it take to reach it?





* * *





kācikkup pōyum karumam tolaiyavillai.

Though one has gone to Benares his sins are not removed.





* * *





kācikkup pōṉālum akappai araikkācu.

Though taken to Benares, a wooden ladle will fetch but half a cash.





* * *





kācikkup pōṉālum kati peṟa vaḻi illai.

Although he may go to Kàsi, he will not find the way to heaven.





* * *





kācikkuppōyum muṭattavaci kālil viḻukiṟatā?

What! having gone to Kàsi is it to fall at the feet of a lame ascetic?





* * *





kāciyil irukkiṟavaṉ kaṇṇaik kuttak kāñcipurattiliruntu kaiyai nīṭṭik koṇṭu pōkiṟatā?

If you wish to strike the eyes of one at Kási, do you go, for that purpose, with outstretched hand from Conjevaram?





* * *





kācu illātavaṉukku varākaṉ pēccu eṉṉa?

Why should a penniless man talk about pagodas?





* * *





kācu koṭuttāl vēci varuvāḷ, kala nellaik koṭuttāl avaḷ akkāḷum āttāḷum kūṭa varuvārkaḷ.

If you are free with your money a harlot will come, if you give also a kalam of rice, her sister and her mother will come.





* * *





kācukku oru kuñcu viṟṟālum kaṇakkak kuñcu ākātu.

Though young birds are sold a cash each, an accountant's young bird is not good.





* * *





kācukku oru kuñcu viṟṟālum kammāḷak kuñcu ākātu.

Though sold for a single cash, never buy a smith's bird.





* * *





kācukku oru puṭaivai viṟṟālum nāyiṉ cūttu ammaṇam.

Though cloth may be cheap, dogs go naked.





* * *





kācukkūṭu karikkūṭāyp pōyiṟṟu.

The money basket has become a charcoal basket.





* * *





kācukkup pōṉa māṉam kōṭi koṭuttālum varātu.

Chastity lost for a cash will not be recovered by a crore.





* * *





kācaik koṭuttuk kuttu māṭu tēṭukiṟatā?

Will you buy a vicious cow at a high price?





* * *





kāñcipurattiṟkup pōṉāl kāl āṭṭi uṇṇalām.

If you go to Conjeveram, you may eat without labour.





* * *





kāṭikkup pōyt tayir koṇṭuvantatupōla.

Like going for vinegar and bringing curds.





* * *





kāṭu kāttavaṉum kaṟcēri kāttavaṉum palaṉ aṭaivāṉ.

Both will be benefited, he who watches the jungle and he who waits at the cutchery.





* * *





kāṭu vā eṉkiṟatu vīṭu pō eṉkiṟatu.

Home bids me go, and the place of incremation says come.





* * *





kāṭu viḷaintālum oru mēṭu viḷaintālum kaṭaṉ kaḻintu pōm.

The debt will be discharged in either case, whether the jungle or highland yield a good crop.





* * *





kāṭu viḷaiyāviṭṭālum kaṭamai pōkumā?

Though the waste land has yielded nothing, will the tax be remitted?





* * *





kāṭu ventāṟ cantaṉa maramum vēkātō?

Should the jungle be consumed, would the sandal wood tree escape?





* * *





kāṭai iṭam āṉāl nāṭṭai āḷalām.

If a quail crosses one's path to the left, he will govern a province.





* * *





kāṭṭik koṭuttuk kaṭakkap pōy niṟkalāmā?

Is it proper to betray one, and then stand aloof?





* * *





kāṭṭil āṉaiyaik kāṭṭi vīṭṭil peṇṇaik koṭukkiṟatā?

Is it to give a girl in marriage when an elephant in the jungle is shown as dower?





* * *





kāṭṭilē cettālum vīṭṭilētāṉ tīṭṭu.

Though a man dies in the jungle, ceremonial uncleanness will attach to his house.





* * *





kāṭṭup pūṉaikkuc civarāttiri viratamā ?

Does the wild cat observe the fast of Sivaratri?





* * *





kāṭṭukku eṟitta nilāvum kāṉalukkup peyta maḻaiyum.

Moonlight in the jungle and rain in the plains.





* * *





kāṭṭu vāḻai vantāl vīṭṭu vāḻvu pōkum.

When wild plantains come, domestic prosperity will vanish.





* * *





kāṭṭup pēyccuraikkāy kaṟikku ākumā?

Is a wild gourd fit for curry?





* * *





kāṭṭuk kōḻikku uraṟ kuḻiyē kailācam.

The hole in a mortar is paradise to a jungle fowl.





* * *





kāṭṭup pūccikaḷ etirtta katai āccu.

It has become the story of wild insects offering resistance.





* * *





kāṇak kiṭaittatu kārttikaip piṟaipōla.

That which was seen was like the new moon of November.





* * *





kāṇappaṭṭaṉa ellām aḻiyappaṭṭaṉa.

All that is seen is temporary.





* * *





kāṇāmal kaṇṭēṉō kampaṅkatirē cintāmal kuttaṭi cilli mūkkē?

Have I seen kambu-millet for the first time, pound it without scattering thou nose bleeding woman?





* * *





kāṇi āḷaṉ vīṭu vēkaccē kālaip piṭittu iḻutta katai.

The story of one pulling by the feet the landlord whose house was on fire.





* * *





kāṇi ēṟak kōṭi aḻiyum.

While ⁠1

/80

⁠ is added a crore is destroyed.





* * *





kāṇi kaviḻntu pōkiṟatā?

Does land turn upside down?





* * *





kāṇi kāṇiyāyc campātittuk kōṭi kōṭiyāyc celavaḻikka vēṇṭum.

We must acquire by eightieths and spend by crores.





* * *





kāṇikku ottatu kōṭikku.

The profit or loss on an eightieth part of a unit, will determine that on a crore.





* * *





kāṇi cōmpal kōṭi varuttam.

A little indolence creates great trouble.





* * *





kāṇi mantam kōṭi tukkam.

A little indolence will bring great sorrow.





* * *





kātam viṭṭu irukātam cuṟṟukiṟatupōla.

Like ranging over the distance of two kathams instead of one.





* * *





kātam koṭuttu irukātam vāṅkukiṟatupōla.

Like giving a katham and getting two.





* * *





kātavaḻi pōy aṟiyātāṉ mātam ellām naṭantāṉām.

He who never walked a katham is said to have walked a whole month.





* * *





kātavaḻi pōy aṟiyāṉ kaḻutaip piṟappu.

He who never walked a katham is an ass.





* * *





kātavaḻi peyar illāṉ kaḻutaiyōṭu okkum.

He whose reputation does not reach a kathan is like an ass.





* * *





kātilē nārācam ēṟṟiṉāṟ pōla.

Like an iron wire passed into the ear.





* * *





kātil cilanti ōtaṭi āṉanti.

O Avanti, I have a boil in my ear, utter an incantation.





* * *





kātu aṟutta kūli kaimēlē.

The hire for tearing the ear is readily paid.





* * *





kātu aṟuttālum aṟukkum pēṉaip pārttālum pārkkum kuraṅku.

A monkey may tear the ears or hunt lice if he choose.





* * *





kātu kātu eṉṟāl nāti nāti eṉkiṟāṉ.

If one says, my ear, my ear, he replies it is mine, it is mine.





* * *





kātu kātu eṉṟāl ceviṭu ceviṭu eṉkiṟāṉ.

If one cry an ear, an ear, he cries deafness deafness.





* * *





kātu kuttap poṟukkātā?

Can you not bear the boring of your ears?





* * *





kātukku iṭṭāl mukattukku aḻaku.

If worn in the ears, they will add beauty to the face.





* * *





kātukkuk kaṭukkaṉ iṭṭu āṭṭikkoṇṭu tirikiṟāṉ.

Having put on ear-rings he walks about shaking them.





* * *





kātum kātum vaittāṟpōl irukkavēṇṭum.

It should be as if an ear, and an ear, were brought in contact.





* * *





kātai aṟuttavaṉ kaṇṇaiyum kuttuvāṉā?

Will he who cut off the ear strike the eye also?





* * *





kāttiruntavaṉ peṇṭāṭṭiyai vēṟṟu maṉitaṉ piṭittukkoṇṭāṉ.

A stranger took possession of the wife of him who was watching her.





* * *





kāntamum ūciyum pōl.

Like a magnet and needle.





* * *





kāppāṉukkuk kaḷḷaṉ illai.

He who takes care of his property will not be robbed.





* * *





kāyam paṭṭa kuraṅkupōl.

Like a wounded monkey.





* * *





kāyum puḻuviṟkuc cāyum niḻal pōla.

Like a falling shadow on a sun-striken worm.





* * *





kāytta marattil kal eṟipaṭum kāyāta marattil kal eṟipaṭumā?

Stones are thrown at a fruit bearing tree; are they thrown at that which does not bear?





* * *





kāytta marattilē kal eṟiyum cil eṟiyum.

Stones and other missiles are thrown at a fruit-bearing tree.





* * *





kāytta maram vaḷaintu niṟkum naṟkuṇam uṭaiyavar taṇintu niṟpār.

A fruit-bearing tree bends; the virtuous are lowly.





* * *





kāynta maram taḷirkkumā?

Will a dry tree bud?





* * *





kāynta māṭu kampilē viḻuntāṟpōla.

Like a starving cow falling in the kambu-millet.





* * *





kāynta puli āṭṭu mantaiyilē viḻukiṟatupōla.

Like a hungry tiger falling on a flock of sheep.





* * *





kāynta puli āvilē viḻukiṟatu.

A lean tiger falls on a cow.





* * *





kāyntālum vennīr avam pōmō?

Is water useless because it is boiled?





* * *





kāynta vāṉam pēyntāl viṭātu?

Rain after drought will not soon cease?





* * *





kāyntum keṭuttatu pēyntum keṭuttatu.

It was destroyed by the sun and by the rain.





* * *





kārāmpacuvukkup pullāṉāl nantavaṉattukkuk kaḷaiyumām.

Grass serves as food for kine, and as an ornament to a flower garden.





* * *





kārikai kaṟṟuk kavi colluvatiṟ pērikai koṭṭip piḻaippatu naṉṟu.

It is better to live by beating a drum than by composing verses according to the rules of prosody.





* * *





kāriyakkāraṉ kollaiyilē kaḻutai mēykutu.

The ass grazes in the grounds of the industrious.





* * *





kāriyattilē kaṇ allāmal vīriyattilē illai.

His eye is on the object, not on the attendant applause.





* * *





kāriyattukkuk kaḻutaiyiṉ kālaip piṭi.

If necessary, secure your purpose by clinging to the feet of an ass.





* * *





kāriyam peritō vīriyam peritō?

Which is greater, success or boasting?





* * *





kār aṟukkaṭṭum kattari pūkkaṭṭum.

Wait till the kár paddy is reaped, and the brinjal blossoms.





* * *





kārttikaikku mēl maḻaiyum illai, karṇaṉukku mēl koṭaiyum illai.

No rain like that of November, no liberality superior to that of Karnan.

Karnan, one of the seven princes distinguished for great generosity.





* * *





kārttikaip piṟaipōlak kaṇṭēṉ.

I saw it like the third-day moon of November.





* * *





kālañ ceykiṟatu kōlam ceyyātē.

Time will effect that which mere parade cannot.





* * *





kālañ ceykiṟatu ñālam ceyyātu.

The world cannot accomplish that which time effects.





* * *





kālattiṉāl ceyta naṉṟi.

A benefit conferred opportunely.





* * *





kālattiṟ peyta maḻaipōla.

Like seasonable rain.





* * *





kālattukku ēṟṟa kōlam.

Equipage suited to the occasion.





* * *





kālamē eḻuntiruntu kākkai viḻikkal ākātu.

The sight of a crow on rising of a morning is ominous of evil.

The crow being regarded as the vehicle of Saturn in the sight of a single crow is inauspicious; two are not so regarded. The cawing of a crow may excite apprehension. When this is the case the following formula may be used.

Measure the length of your shadow by the foot and add 12 to the number of feet ascertained. Then divide by 7 and the remainder will determine the import of the bird's note (1) Good luck, (2) Gain, (3) Rain, (4) Conflict, (5) Feasting, (6) Victory. If on dividing by 7 there is no remainder the cawing of the crow is ominous of death.





* * *





kālam aṟintu ñālam oḻuku.

Regulate your affairs with due reference to the times in which you live.





* * *





kālam keṭṭuk kaippiccai eṭuttāṟpōla.

Like receiving alms when in distress.





* * *





kālam aṟintu piḻaiyātavaṉ vāl aṟunta kuraṅku āvāṉ.

He who does not live according to the times will become an ape.





* * *





kālam pōm vārttai niṟkum, kappal pōm tuṟai niṟkum.

Time goes, words remain, a ship goes, the shore remains.





* * *





kālam pōṉa kālattil mūlam vantu kuṟukkiṭṭatupōla.

As one is subject to piles in his riper years.





* * *





kālam allāta kālattilē kappal ōṭṭi.

A mariner in unfavourable weather.





* * *





kālālē naṭantāl kātavaḻi, talaiyālē naṭantāl evvaḷavu tūram?

If on foot it is a katham; how much more distant if one walk on the head?





* * *





kālāl muṭintataik kaiyāl aviḻkkakkūṭātu.

What he tied with his feet others cannot untie with the hands.





* * *





kālilē paṭṭapiṟakā kirakacāram pōtātu?

After having hurt the foot is it to be attributed to planetary influence?





* * *





kālil viḻukiṟatu nallatu mēlil viḻukiṟatu keṭṭatu.

To fall at the feet is good, to fall on another is bad.





* * *





kālil paṭṭatu kaṇṇil paṭṭatupōla.

That which struck the feet was felt as if it had struck the eye.





* * *





kālukkup pōṭṭāl talaikkup pōṭukiṟāṉ.

If he puts ornaments on his legs, he will put ornaments on his head.





* * *





kālukkuk kaṭuppē tavira, kaṇṭa palaṉ oṉṟum illai.

No benefit accrued but the trouble of walking.





* * *





kālukkuk kai utavi kaikkuk kāl utavi.

The hand helps the legs and the legs help the hands.





* * *





kālukkut takka ceruppum kūlikkut takka uḻaippum.

A slipper suited to the foot, and labour suited to the hire.





* * *





kālukku ākiṟa ceruppu talaikku ākumā?

Will a slipper that fits the foot do for the head?





* * *





kālaic cuṟṟiṉa pāmpu kaṭittāl oḻiya viṭātu.

The snake that has coiled round the leg will not leave without biting.





* * *





kālaic cel pūttāl aṭutta maḻai aṭaṅkum.

Should winged white-ants come out in the morning the heavy rain will cease.





* * *





kālai tuyilvāṉum mālai iruppāṉum patar.

He who sleeps after day-break, and he who keeps awake in the fore part of the night are worthless.





* * *





kālaip piṭitta caṉiyaṉ ūraic cuṟṟi aṭikkum.

If Saturn seize the feet, he will drive one round the village.





* * *





kālaip paṉikkum kaṇviḻikkum ottatu celvam.

Wealth is comparable to the morning dew, and the twinkling of an eye.





* * *





kālai mōṭcamum vālai ñāṉamum nilaikkātu.

Dreams of bliss and premature wisdom are not lasting.





* * *





kāl aḷavē ākumām kappaliṉ ōṭṭam nūl aḷavē ākumām nuṇcīlai.

The speed of a vessel is proportioned to the force of the wind, the quality of cloth will be as the yarn used in its manufacture.





* * *





kāl āṭak kōl āṭum kōl āṭak kuraṅku āṭum.

The foot puts the stick in motion, as that moves, the monkey dances.





* * *





kāl ciṟitākil kaṉal ūrum kaṉṉiyar mēl māl ciṟitākil maṉam ūrum.

Where there is a little wind fire will spread, where there is lust the affections will be fastened on women.





* * *





kāl naṭaikku iraṇṭu kācu kai vīccukku aintu kācu.

Two cash for walking on foot, five cash for the swing of the arms.





* * *





kāl māṟik kaṭṭiṉāl kaṉam kuṟaiyumā?

If one be tied cross-legged, will his weight diminish?





* * *





kālvāyait tāṇṭātavaṉ kaṭalait tāṇṭuvāṉā?

Can he who cannot leap over a channel leap over the sea?





* * *





kāvaṭip pāram cumakkiṟavaṉukkut teriyum.

The weight of a burden is felt by the bearer.





* * *





kāval tāṉē pāvaiyarkku aḻaku.

Chastity is a feminine grace.





* * *





kāvēri āṟṟai maṟippāy kārttikai mātattuk kaṟkaṭaka cantiraṉaiyum maṟippāyā?

You may stay the Cauvery, but can you hinder the full moon of November if he be in Cancer?





* * *





kāvēri kañciyāyp pōṉālum nāy nakkittāṉ kuṭikka vēṇṭum.

Should the Cauvery become kanji, the dog would partake thereof by lapping.





* * *





kāvōlai viḻuntateṉṟu kuruttōlai cirittatām.

The green leaves of the palm laughed because the dry ones fell off.





* * *





kāḷitōṭṭattuk kaṟpaka virukṣam ārukkum utavātu.

Even the katpaka tree in the garden of Durga is of no use to mankind.

The katpaka tree is said to yield whatever a suppliant may require.





* * *





kāḷippaṭṭam pōṉālum mūḷippaṭṭam pōkātu.

The name Kali may become obsolete, but not the (nicknane) Mooli, the earless.





* * *





kāṟ kācup pūṉai mukkāṟ kācut tayiraik kuṭittatu.

A cat worth a quarter of a cash consumed curds worth three quarters of a cash.





* * *





kāṟṟil akappaṭṭa kappalpōl alaikiṉṟatu maṉam.

The mind is agitated like a ship in a storm.





* * *





kāṟṟukkā maḻaikkā pōrttukkoḷḷa tuṇikkā?

Is it as a security against the wind and rain that you are thus clad?





* * *





kāṟṟukku etirilē tuppiṉāl mukattilē viḻum.

If one spit against the wind-the spittle-will strike his own face:





* * *





kāṟṟukkut tōṇi etirttu ōṭātu.

A dhony can not sail against the wind.





* * *





kāṟṟum cilarai nīkki vīcumō?

Will the wind blow so as to avoid certain persons?





* * *





kāṟṟu uḷḷapōtu tūṟṟikkoḷ karumpu uḷḷapōtu āṭṭik koḷ.

Winnow when the wind blows, work the sugar-mill when the cane is ripe.





* * *





kāṟṟaip piṭittuk karakattil aṭaikkalāmā?

Can one seize the wind and confine it in a small vessel?





* * *





kāṟṟaip pārttuk kappal nāṭṭu.

Anchor a ship with reference to the wind.





* * *





kāṉalai nīreṉṟu eṇṇi māṉ ōṭi iḷaittatupōla.

Like the deer that wearied itself in pursuit of a mirage imagining it to be water.





* * *





ki.



kiṭakkiṟatu kiṭakkaṭṭum kiḻavaṉai eṭuttu maṇaiyilē vai.

Leave the things as they are, and take the old man and put him on the stool to bathe.

The first thing done before a corpse is removed from the homestead is to bathe it.





* * *





kiṭakkiṟatu kuṭṭiccuvar kaṉāk kāṇkiṟatu maccuvīṭu.

Dwelling in a ruinous hut and dreaming of a palace.





* * *





kiṭṭa vā nāyē eṉṟāl mūñciyai nakkukiṟatu.

When called, the dog licks the face.





* * *





kiṭṭāta oṉṟai veṭṭeṉa maṟa.

Forget at once what cannot be obtained.





* * *





kiṇaṟu veṭṭap pūtam puṟappaṭṭāṟpōla āyiṟṟē.

It has happened as if a demon had sprung out of a well just dug.





* * *





kiṇaṟum veṭṭit tavaḷaiyum piṭittu viṭukiṟatā?

What! is it to dig a well and supply it with frogs?





* * *





kiṇaṟu kiṭakka malai kallātē.

Whilst there is a well do not excavate a mountain-for water.





* * *





kiṇaṟu veṭṭit tākam tīrkkalāmā?

Must one dig a well to quench his thirst?





* * *





kiṇaṟu mettiṉāṟ kīḻvarai pociyum.

If there be water the undermost ring of the well will be wet.





* * *





kiṇaṟu iṟaikka iṟaikkac curakkum.

The more a well is drawn, the better the spring.





* * *





kiṇaṟu tappit turavil viḻalāmā?

Having escaped falling into the well, shall we fall into a tank?





* * *





kiṇaṟṟait tūrttāl vayiṟṟait tūrkkum.

If the well is to be filled up, the belly must be filled up.





* * *





kiṇaṟṟut tavaḷai taṇṇīr kuṭittataik kaṇṭatu ār, kuṭiyātataik kaṇṭatu ār?

Who knows whether the frog in the well did or did not drink water?





* * *





kiṇaṟṟut tavaḷaikku ēṉ nāṭṭu vaḷappam?

What has the frog in the well to do with the news of the country?





* * *





kiṇaṟṟukkut tappit tīyilē pāyntāṉ.

Having escaped falling into the well he jumped into the fire.





* * *





kiṇaṟṟaik kaṇṭu kaṭal otuṅkip pōkumā?

Will the sea shrink at the sight of a well?





* * *





kiṇaṟṟut taṇṇīrai veḷḷam koṇṭupōkumā?

Will the flood carry away the water of the well?





* * *





kiṇaṟṟilēt taṇṇīr utittatu.

Water sprang up in the well.





* * *





kiṇaṟṟu āḻamum kayiṟṟu nīḷamum pārkkavēṇṭum.

The depth of the well and the length of the cord must be ascertained.





* * *





kiṇaṟṟil viḻuntavaṉ maṟupaṭiyum viḻuvāṉā?

Will he who has fallen into a well once fall in again?





* * *





kiṇaṟṟil kal pōṭṭatupōla.

Like dropping a stone into a well.





* * *





kiṇṭak kiṇṭa ampaṭṭaṉ kuppai mayirē puṟappaṭum.

The more you dig in the refuse heap of the barber, the more will hair turn up.





* * *





kiṇṭak kiṇṭak kīraiyum mayirum.

The more you dig, the more will greens and hair appear.





* * *





kiraka cāntikkuc cavarampaṇṇik koḷḷukiṟatā?

Will shaving counteract the evil influence of a planet?





* * *





kiriyai aṟṟōṉ maṟai cāṟṟuvatu ēṉ?

What! is it for an evil-doer to teach religious precepts?





* * *





kirupā natiyē cruvā niti.

The stream of grace is the source of all treasures.





* * *





kiḻak kuṭalukkuc cōṟum iṭic cuvarukku maṇṇum iṭu.

Give rice to the aged and add mud to a ruinous wall.





* * *





kiḻap pēccu capaikku ēṟumā?

Will the words of an old man go up to the assembly?





* * *





kiḻavaṉ āṉālum keṭṭai āṉālum kaṭṭik koṇṭavaḷ piḻaippāḷ.

She who marries will do well whether her husband be old or poor.





* * *





kiḻavaṉukku vāḻkkaippaṭukiṟatilum kiṇaṟṟil viḻalām.

It is better to fall into a well than to marry an old man.





* * *





kiḻavi pēccaik kiṉṉarakkāraṉ kēṭpāṉō?

Will a musician listen to the speech of an old woman?





* * *





kiḻaviyum kātam kutiraiyum kātam.

The old woman is a katham from the horse, and the horse a katham from the old woman.





* * *





kiḻavi irunta vīṭum kiḷi irunta kāṭum īṭēṟa māṭṭātu.

Neither the house of an old woman, nor the grove in which there are parrots, will be saved from ruin.





* * *





kiḻinta cīlai kācukku iraṇṭu.

Two rags for a cash.





* * *





kiḻinta pamparam kācukku reṇṭu.

Two split tops for a cash.





* * *





kiḷiyai vaḷarttup pūṉai kaiyil koṭukkalāmā?

Is it proper to train a parrot and give it into the paw of a cat?





* * *





kiḷiyaippōla pēccum mayilaippōla naṭaiyum.

Speech like that of a parrot, gait like that of a peacock.





* * *





kiḷḷap paḻukkumām kiḷi iruntu koñcamām.

It is said that it ripens by being pinched, and that parrots will nibble it leisurely.





* * *





kiḷḷuvārkīḻ iruppatilum aḷḷuvārkīḻ irukkalām.

One may endure those who rob, but not those who pilfer.





* * *





kī.



kīri kaṭitta pāmpupōlē.

Like a snake bit by a mungoose.





* * *





kīrikkum pāmpukkum tīrāp pakai.

Inveterate enmity exists between the mungoose and snakes.





* * *





kīriyum pāmpumpōla.

Like a mungoose and a snake.





* * *





kīraik kaṭaikkum etirkkaṭai vēṇṭum.

An opposition shop is good even among green grocers.





* * *





kīraikkup pulluruvi kīḻē muḷaittāṟpōlē.

As if a parasite should spring from the lower part of a vegetable.





* * *





kīraik kaṭṭai veṭṭac coṉṉāl tōraṇam kaṭṭukiṟatā?

When I order the greens to be pruned, is a garland put up?





* * *





kīrait taṇṭu piṭuṅka ēlēlappāṭṭu ēṉ?

Why sing eléla when plucking up greens?





* * *





kīraiyai iraṇṭu kaṟi paṇṇātē.

Do not make two curries of a vegetable.





* * *





kīrttiyāl paci tīrumā?

Will hunger be appeased by fame?





* * *





kīrtti peṟṟum kilēcam eṉṉa?

Whence sorrow to him who hag obtained praise?





* * *





kīḻē pāmpu eṉṟāl mēlē pārkkiṟāṉ.

If it is said that a snake is below, he looks up.





* * *





kīḻkulattāṉ āṉālum kaṟṟavaṉ mēlkulattāṉ.

Though of low origin, the learned ranks with the highest class.





* * *





kīḻkkātu mēṟkātu mūḷi, caṇṭaikku raṇapattirakāḷi.

Her ears are rent at both ends; in an affray she is equal to Durga.





* * *





kīṟi āṟṟiṉāl puṇ āṟum.

If lanced, the sore will heal.





* * *





ku.



kuṅkiliyat tūpam kāṭṭic caṉṉatam kulaintāl kumpiṭu eṅkē?

If indeed the incense has expelled the demon, where is the sign?-uplifted hands.





* * *





kuṅkumam cumanta kaḻutai parimaḷam aṟiyumā?

Can the ass which carried the kungumam-a fragrant resin-appreciate scents?





* * *





kuṅkumak kōtaikku añcu paṇam, kuruṭṭuk kaṇṇikkum añcu paṇamā?

The donation to the perfumed dame being five fanams, is five fanams to be assigned to the blind woman also?





* * *





kuñcirippukku maruntu cāppiṭa uḷḷa cirippum pōṉāṟpōla.

As if one lost his natural smile by taking medicine to induee a simpering expression.





* * *





kuṭattil ēṟṟiya viḷakku.

A lamp lit in an earthen pot.





* * *





kuṭattiṟ poṉ kūttu āṭumā?

Will gold in a pot dance?





* * *





kuṭappāl kaṟantālum kutirai ōṭṭam ōṭamāṭṭātu.

Though the cow gives a large pot of milk, it is not equal to the horse in speed.





* * *





kuṭappāl kaṟantālum kūrai piṭuṅkukiṟa māṭu ākātu.

Though it may yield a large pot of milk, a cow that destroys the roof is not desirable.





* * *





kuṭam taṇṇīriṟ koḷḷi vaittāṟpōla.

As if a firebrand were put into a pot of water.





* * *





kuṭalum kūntalum koṇṭatu koḷkai.

Habit is the rule of the stomach and the hair.





* * *





kuṭal kūḻukku aḻukiṟatām koṇṭai pūvukku aḻukiṟatām.

It is said that the stomach cries for gruel, and the tresses for flowers.





* * *





kuṭal aṟunta nari entamaṭṭum ōṭum?

How far can a jackal run after its bowels are dislodged?





* * *





kuṭal aṟunta kōḻi eṅkē pōkum?

Whither will a fowl go after its entrails have been exposed?





* * *





kuṭi iruntu aṟi vaḻi naṭantu aṟi.

Learn the character of a person by dwelling with him, and the condition of a road by walking in it.





* * *





kuṭi iruntu pār kūṭṭuppayir iṭṭup pār.

Test his social qualities by living with him, and his honesty by joint tillage.





* * *





kuṭi iruppatu kuccuvīṭu, kaṉāk kaṇṭatu maccumāḷikai.

He who dwells in a humble cottage dreams of ceiled apartments.





* * *





kuṭi illā vīṭṭiṟ kuṇṭupperuccāḷi ulāvum.

Bandycootes will run about in an uninhabited house.





* * *





kuṭi illā ūrilē aṭi iṭa ākātu.

It is a risk to set a foot in an uninhabited village.





* * *





kuṭi illā ūrukku nari irācaṉ.

A jackal is king in a deserted village.





* * *





kuṭi uṭaiyāṉē muṭi uṭaiyāṉ.

He is fit for kingship who is approved by the people.





* * *





kuṭikkiṟatu kūḻām koppaḷikkiṟatu paṉṉīrām.

He has gruel to drink, and washes his mouth with rose water.





* * *





kuṭikkiṟa pālai veṭippilē vārkkiṟatā?

What, is it to pour down the drinkable milk into a crevice?





* * *





kuṭikkiṟatu kāṭi nīr, ataṟkut taṅkavaṭṭilā?

What you drink is sour gruel, do you require a cup of fine gold for it?





* * *





kuṭikkiṟatu kūḻām irukkiṟatu ciṅkācaṉamām.

Living on gruel and sitting on a throne.





* * *





kuṭikkiṟa vīṭu viṭiyumā?

Will the family of a drunkard prosper?





* * *





kuṭittaṉamō turaittaṉamō etu peritu?

Whether is greater, the government of a family or of a state?





* * *





kuṭittaṉam mēliṭa vēṇṭi piṭāriyaip peṇṭuvaittuk koṇṭāṉ.

Wishing to elevate his family he married a Pidari-village goddess.





* * *





kuṭittaṉameṉṟu paṇṇiṉāl naṉmaiyum varum tīmaiyum varum.

If one becomes a householder, he must expect both good and evil.





* * *





kuṭipōṉa vīṭṭilē vaṟaṭṭu nāy kāttatupōla.

As a lean dog watched a deserted house.





* * *





kuṭimatam aṭipaṭat tīrum.

The madness of a drunkard may be cured by beating.





* * *





kuṭiyaṉum veṟiyaṉum cari.

A drunkard and a mad man are alike.





* * *





kuṭiyāta vīṭu viṭiyātu.

The family of a drunkard is always benighted.





* * *





kuṭiyiṟ peṇ vayiṟu erintāl koṭiyiṟ cēlai niṉṟu eriyum.

If the bowels of the house-wife burn, her cloth hanging on the lino will burn also.





* * *





kuṭiyillā ūrilē oṟṟaip paṇakkāraṉ.

He is the only wealthy man in a deserted village.





* * *





kuṭiyiṟ piṟantu kuraṅku āṭṭam āṭutal.

Born in a good family, and playing the monkey.





* * *





kuṭiyiṟ piṟantu ceṭiyil viḻuntāṉ.

Born in a good family and falling into a bush.





* * *





kuṭi vaittuk koṇṭāyō koḷḷi vaittuk koṇṭāyō?

Have you taken a family to dwell with you? Or have you taken firebrands?





* * *





kuṭi vaitta vīṭṭiṟ koḷḷi vaikkalāmā?

Is it proper to burn down a house after admitting a family?





* * *





kuṭṭik kiṭaiyilē ōṉāy pukuntatupōl.

As a wolf entered a fold of lambs.





* * *





kuṭṭik kalakam paṇṇukiṟavaṉ kuṭṭuppaṭṭuc cāvāṉ.

He who breeds divisions in a family shall be cuffed to death.





* * *





kuṭṭik karaṇam pōṭṭālum lōpaṉ koṭāṉ.

The miser will give nothing though you tumble heels overhead.





* * *





kuṭṭic cuvarilē tēḷ koṭṭa, kaṭṭut taṟiyilē neṟi iṭumā?

If a scorpion sting in a ruinous dwarf wall, will the glandular swelling appear in the stall?





* * *





kuṭṭic cuvarilē tēḷ koṭṭat taṇṇīr miṭāvilē neṟi kaṭṭumā?

Does the sting of a scorpion in a ruinous wall, produce a glandular swelling in a water-pot?





* * *





kuṭṭic cuvarai muṭṭa veḷḷeḻuttā?

Is he so dim-sighted as to stumble against a ruinous wall?





* * *





kuṭṭi pōṭṭa pūṉai pōl alaikiṟāṉ.

He wanders about like a cat that has kittened.





* * *





kuṭṭiṉa kuṭṭum kuṇṭiṟ pāynta taṇṇīrum varumā?

Can a cuff already inflicted and water that has run into a pit be recalled?





* * *





kuṭṭi āṭu koḻuttālum vaḻuvaḻuppup pōkātu.

Though it become fat, the flesh of a kid is always clammy.





* * *





kuṭṭi kulaittu nāypēril vaikkiṟatu pōla.

Like pups barking and creating a quarrel among dogs.





* * *





kuṭṭi nāykoṇṭu vēṭṭai āṭiṉatu pōla.

Like hunting with pups.





* * *





kuṭṭu māṉam tappik kucavaṉōṭē pēciṉāl, caṭṭaiyum paṇṇāṉ, caṭṭiyum koṭāṉ.

If yon speak familiarly with a potter, he will not respect you, nor will he supply you with chatties.





* * *





kuṇattai māṟṟak kuru illai.

No guroo-a religious teacher-can change a man's temperament.

A Guru among the Hindus is regarded as a spiritual parent from whom the youth receives the initiatory mantra or prayer, and who conducts the ceremonies necessary at various seasons of infancy and youth, up to the period of investiture with the sacred thread or string: this person may be the natural parent or the religious preceptor. The word Guru is also used for a teacher or preceptor generally.





* * *





kuṇattiṟku aḻukiṟatā, piṇattiṟku aḻukiṟatā?

Is the weeping on account of the virtue of the deceased or does it relate to his corpse?





* * *





kuṇam uṟṟavaṉ maṇam uṟṟavaṉ, kuṇam aṟṟavaṉ maṇam aṟṟavaṉ.

A good-natured man has an engaging exterior, an ill-natured man has a repulsive aspect.





* * *





kuṇam pāti koṇṭa nōvu pāti.

His suffering is due partly to his faint-heartedness, and partly to a disease.





* * *





kuṇṭāk karaṇam pōṭṭālum piṇṭāc cōṟṟukku vaḻi illai.

Though he makes somersaults to all he sees, he has no food to eat.





* * *





kuṇṭi kāyntāl kutiraiyum vaikkōl tiṉṉum.

If starved even a horse will eat straw.





* * *





kuṇṭi ettaṉai kōṇaṟ kōṇiṉālum cumai vīṭṭiṟ pōyc cērntālē cari.

No matter how the body may be bent if the burden be carried home.





* * *





kuṇṭi aṟunta paruntupōla.

Like a kite with a broken tail.





* * *





kuṇṭuc caṭṭiyilē kutirai ōṭṭukiṟāṉ.

He displays his horsemanship in an earthen pot.





* * *





kuṇṭu paṭṭuc cākātavaṉ vaṇṭu kaṭittuc cettāṉām.

It is said that he who survived a gunshot wound, died of the bite of an insect.





* * *





kuṇṭuc caṭṭiyiṟ karaṇam pōṭalāmā?

Can one turn a somersault in an earthen pot?





* * *





kuṇṭup peruccāḷiyum vaṇṭupōla.

The bandycoote is also like a beetle.





* * *





kuṇṭuṇi collukiṟavaṉukku irunākku kaṭṭuviriyaṉukkum irunākku.

A slanderer and a snake of deadly poison have each two tongues.





* * *





kuṇṭai palattāl kuṭi palakkum.

As kine increase, a household increases.





* * *





kuti kuti eṉpārkaḷ ellārum, kūṭak kutippār illai.

They will all say leap, leap into the pyre, but there is none willing to leap with me.





* * *





kutittuk kutittu mā iṭittālum puḻukkaikku oru koḻukkaṭṭaiyē.

Though she may leap joyously and pound the grain, the slave woman gets but one cake.





* * *





kutirai kuruṭāṉālum koḷḷut tiṉkiṟatiṟ kuṟaiccal illai.

Though blind, the horse does not eat the less grain.





* * *





kutirai koṇṭāl kaliṉattukku vaḻakkā?

When you have bought the horse, is there any occasion for disputing about its bit?





* * *





kutiraiyiṉ kuṇam aṟintallavō tampirāṉ kompu koṭukkavillai?

Was it not because the Creator knew the nature of the horse that he did not provide him with horns?





* * *





kutirai cettatallāmaṟ cēṇam cumakka vēlai āyiṟṟu.

Besides the death of the horse there is the saddle also to be carried.





* * *





kutiraikkum nāykkum kuṭipōkac cantōṣam.

The horse and the dog like a change of place.





* * *





kutirai ēṟa atiṣṭam iruntāṟ kuṇṭiyiṉ kīḻ vantu nuḻaiyātā?

If it be your good fortune to ride, will not your horse come and place itself under your seat?





* * *





kutirai nallatutāṉ cuḻi keṭṭatu.

It is certainly a good horse, but its circular marks are bad.

The circlets of hair on a horse are generally observed by dealers and owners very carefully.





* * *





kutirai ēṟi eṉṉa, kōṇak kompu ūti eṉṉa, vīṇarkkum kīrttikkum veku tūram.

What if you do ride a horse, and have a horn blown before you? There is a great distance between the worthless and reputation.





* * *





kutirai pākaṉait taḷḷuvatum allāmal kuḻiyum tōṇṭukiṟatām.

It is said that the horse has not only thrown its rider, but is digging his grave.





* * *





kutirai cettatu allāmal kuḻi tōṇṭa mūṉṟu paṇamā?

Besides losing the horse, have I to pay three fanams to bury him?





* * *





kutiraiyum kātam kiḻaviyum kātam.

The horse is ten miles before you, and the old woman is ten miles behind you.





* * *





kutirai piṭikkac cammaṭṭi aṭikka kūppiṭṭak kuralukku ēṉeṉṉa.

You will attend to my horse, ply the sledge hammer and answer my calls.





* * *





kutirai iruppu aṟiyum koṇṭa peṇṭāṭṭi kuṇam aṟivāḷ.

The horse knows his rider, and a wife her husband.





* * *





kutti vaṭittālum campā kuppaiyilē pōṭṭālum taṅkam.

Though pounded and boiled it is samba-superior rice-though cast on the rubbish heap it is gold.





* * *





kuttuppaṭṭup poṟuttālum kuṟaivayiṟu poṟukkum?

A cuff may be borne, can hunger?





* * *





kunti iruntu tiṉṟāl kuṉṟu māḷum.

If you eat properly seated you may consume a heap of rice.





* * *





kupēraṉ paṭṭaṇattilum viṟaku cumakkiṟavaṉ uṇṭu.

Even in the city of Kubèran there were firewood carriers.





* * *





kupēraṉ paṭṭaṇam koḷḷaipōṉālum atiṣṭavīṉaṉukku akappaik kāmpum akappaṭumā?

Though the city of Kubèran be despoiled, will the unfortunate obtain even the handle of a wooden spoon?





* * *





kuppuṟa viḻuntu tavam ceytālum kurukkaḷukku mōṭcam illai.

Though they may prostrate themselves and perform penance, religious teachers do not attain heaven.





* * *





kuppuṟa viḻuntum mīcaiyilē maṇ paṭavillai eṉkiṟāṉ.

Though he fell flat on his face, he says that his mustache did not touch the ground.





* * *





kuppaiyil muḷaitta kīrai kappalukkuk kālākumā?

Will a plant grown on a dunghill answer for the mast of a ship?





* * *





kuppaiyil muḷaitta koṭi kūraiyil ēṟiṉatupōla.

As a creeper springing on a dunghill climbed on a roof.





* * *





kuppaiyiṉṟip payir viḷaiyātu.

Without manure the growing grain will not yield a good crop.





* * *





kuppaiyiṟ kiṭantālum kuṉṟimaṇi kuṉṟimaṇitāṉ.

Though it lies on the dunghill the kunrimani-abrus precatorius-remains unchanged.





* * *





kuppaiyiṟ putaittālum kuṉṟimaṇi niṟam pōkātu.

Though the kunrimani be buried in the dunghill its colour will not change.





* * *





kuppai uyarntatu kōpuram tāḻntatu.

The rubbish heap has risen, the tower has sunk.





* * *





kuppai uyarntateṉṉa kōpuram tāḻntateṉṉa?

How is it that the rubbish heap has risen, and the tower sunk?





* * *





kuppaiyum kōḻiyumpōlak kuruvum cīṣaṉum.

The teacher and his disciple are like the dunghill and the fowl.





* * *





kumari oru piḷḷai kōṭi oru veḷḷai.

A woman is young till she bears a child, and cloth is new till it is washed.





* * *





kumari taṉivaḻiyē pōṉālum koṭṭāvi taṉivaḻiyē pōkātu.

Though a young woman may go on her way alone, yawning is never alone.





* * *





kumariyāy irukkaiyiṟ koṇṭāṭṭam kiḻaviyāy irukkiṟa pōtu tiṇṭāṭṭam.

When young, a woman is full of hilarity, when old her portion is vexation.





* * *





kumpiṭṭuk kaṭaṉ koṭātē kumpiṭṭuk kaṭaṉ vāṅkātē.

Neither lend nor borrow beseechingly.





* * *





kumpiṭap pōṉa iṭattil kōyil iṭintu viḻuntatu.

When I went to worship the temple fell.





* * *





kumpiṭap pōṉa teyvam kuṟukkē vantatupōl.

As the deity one went to worship came to meet one cross wise.





* * *





kumpiṭṭa teyvam kulateyvam.

The deity I worship is my hereditary deity.





* * *





kumpiṭum kaḷḷar kuḻaittiṭum kaḷḷar.

Obsequious rogues, deceitful friends.





* * *





kuyavaṉukkup pala nāḷai vēlai, taṭi aṭikāraṉukku oru nimiṣa vēlai.

That which cost the potter many days labour, is but the labour of a moment to the breaker.





* * *





kuyavā kalacam koṇṭuvā, iṭaiyā pāl koṭu eṉṟāṟ pōla.

Like saying to the potter bring a vessel, and to the shepherd fill it with milk.





* * *





kuyil kūviṉāṟpōla.

Like the warbling of the Indian cuckoo.





* * *





kuyiṟ kuralum mayil aḻakumpōla.

Like the voice of the Indian cuckoo, and the beauty of the peacock.





* * *





kuraṅkiṉ kaip pūmālai.

A garland of flowers in the hand of a monkey.





* * *





kuraṅkukkup putticollit tūkkaṇaṅkuruvi kūṇṭu iḻantatu.

The advice which the pendulous bird Loxia, gave to the monkey ended in the destruction of its own nest.





* * *





kuraṅkukkum taṉkuṭṭi poṉkuṭṭi.

Even to the monkey its own young is precious.





* * *





kuraṅkup puṇ āṟātu.

A sore on a monkey never heals.





* * *





kuraṅkup puṇ piramāṇṭam.

A sore of the monkey is enormously large.





* * *





kuraṅkup piṇamum kuṟap piṇamum kaṇṭavar illai.

No one ever saw the dead body of a monkey, or the corpse of a kuravan-a mountaineer.





* * *





kuraṅku taṉ kuṭṭiyiṉ kaiyaikkoṇṭu patam pārkkiṟatu pōlap pārkkiṟāṉ.

He tests it by another as a monkey tests an object by the hand of its young.





* * *





kuraṅkup piṭipōl piṭikkavēṇṭum.

Lay hold of a thing as firmly as a monkey.





* * *





kuraṅkiṉ pīyai maruntukkuk kēṭṭāl koppu kiḷai ellām tattip pāyum.

If a monkey be asked for his droppings for medicinal purposes, he will leap from branch to branch.





* * *





kuraṅku ellām oru mukam.

All monkeys have faces alike.





* * *





kuraṅku āṉālum kulattil koḷḷavēṇṭum.

Though she be a mere monkey, one should take a wife in one's own tribe.





* * *





kuraṅku ēṟāta kompu uṇṭā?

Is there a branch the monkey has not climbed?





* * *





kuraṅku ellām kūṭṭam kūṭṭamāka irukkum.

All monkeys go about in troops.





* * *





kuraṅkiṉ kaiyil koḷḷi akappaṭṭa katai.

The story of a firebrand in the hand of a monkey.





* * *





kuru illāta cīṣaṉ uṇṭā?

Is there a disciple without a Guru-teacher?





* * *





kuru illārkku vittaiyum illai mutal illārkku lāpamum illai.

There is no art without a teacher, and no profit without capital.





* * *





kuru eṉa vantāṉ tiru urai tantāṉ.

He came as a Guru; he gave sacred instruction.





* * *





kurukkaḷ piḻaittatu maṟupiṟappu.

The escape of the religious teacher was as it were a second birth.





* * *





kuruṭaṉ kaiyil vilāṅku akappaṭṭatupōla.

As an eel was caught in the hand of a blind man.





* * *





kuruṭaṉukku vēṇṭiyatu kōl.

The need of a blind man is a staff.





* * *





kuruṭaṉukku vēṇṭiyatu kaṇ.

What the blind want is eye-sight.





* * *





kuruṭaṉukkuk kuruṭaṉ kōl piṭikkalāmā?

Can the blind lead the blind with a staff?





* * *





kuruṭaṉ āṭu mēykka eṭṭāḷukku vēlaiyā?

Is it a work for eight men to help a blind man to feed sheep?





* * *





kuruṭaṉ kūttup pārkkap pōṉāl payaṉ eṉṉa?

What advantage will the blind man gain by attending a comedy?





* * *





kuruṭaṉukkup pāl kokkup pōṉṟatu.

To the blind milk was like a heron.





* * *





kuruṭaṉ peṇṭāṭṭi kūṉaṉōṭu uṟavāṭiṉāḷ.

The wife of a blind man became intimate with a hunch-backed man.





* * *





kuruṭaṉ peṇṭirai aṭittāṟpōla.

As if a blind man beat his wife.





* * *





kuruṭaṉaip pārttu irāca viḻi viḻikkac coṉṉāl viḻippāṉā?

If requested to assume the looks of a king, can a blind man do so?





* * *





kuruṭaṉai nōṭṭam pārkkac coṉṉāṟpōla.

As if a blind man was asked to assay metals.





* * *





kuruṭaṉ kaik kōlaip piṭuṅkiṉatupōla.

Like snatching away the staff of the blind.





* * *





kuruṭaṉukku kuruṭaṉ vaḻi kāṭṭiṉāl iruvarum kuḻiyilē viḻuvār.

if the blind lead the blind, both will fall into the pit.





* * *





kuruṭi taṇṇīrkkup pōka eṭṭāḷ miṉakeṭṭatupōl.

As the work of eight persons was stopped by reason of a blind woman going to fetch water.





* * *





kuruṭum ceviṭum kūttup pārttatupōla.

As the blind and the deaf attended a comedy.





* * *





kuruṭṭuk kutiraikkuc caṟukkiṉatu cākku.

The excuse of a blind horse was his stumbling.





* * *





kuruṭṭup pūṉai viṭṭattiṟ pāyntatupōla.

As a blind cat leaped on the cross beam.





* * *





kuruṭṭukkokkiṟku ūrkkuḷamē cāṭci.

The village tank is the witness of the blind crane.





* * *





kuruṭṭuk kōḻi taviṭṭukku vīṅkiyatupōla.

As a blind fowl was over anxious to feed on bran.





* * *





kuruṭṭuk kaṇṇukkuk kuṟuṇi mai iṭṭu āvatu eṉṉa?

Why adorn blind eyes with collyrium?





* * *





kuru moḻi kēḷātavaṉum tāy vārttaikku aṭaṅkātavaṉum caṇṭi.

He who will not bear the words of this Guru-religious teacher-and he who disobeys his mother, are worthless.





* * *





kuru moḻi maṟantōṉ tiruvaḻintu aḻivāṉ.

He who forgets the words of his Guru will forfeit the divine favor and perish.





* * *





kuruviyiṉ kaḻuttilē paṉaṅkāyaik kaṭṭit tūkkalāmā?

Can a palmyra fruit be suspended from the neck of a small bird?





* * *





kuruvikkut takka irāma attiram allavō?

Is not the weapon of Rama suited to the bird?





* * *





kuruvikkut takka rāmēcuram.

Raméswaram suited to the bird.

This is I think a perversion of the foregoing proverb. An aged or feeble person may say, my Raméswaram, pilgrimage, efforts, must be moderate.





* * *





kuruvikkut takka pāram.

A burden suited to the bird.





* * *





kuruvik kūṭṭaik kōlāṟ kalaikkātē.

Never disturb the nest of a bird with a stick.





* * *





kuruvukku miñciṉa cīṣaṉā?

Is there a disciple who is above his Guru?





* * *





kuruvukku nāmam kuḻaittup pōṭuvāṉ.

He will deceive his own Guru.





* * *





kuruvukkut turōkam ceytālum kuṭalukkut turōkam ceyyakkūṭātu.

Though a Guru may be deceived, the stomach cannot.





* * *





kuru vēṣam koṇṭavaṉ ellām kuru āvāṉā?

Are all religious teachers who have assumed the garb?





* * *





kulattaik keṭutta kōṭālikkāmpu.

The handle of an axe that destroyed its own species.

The tree from which its handle was taken, was felled by the axe.





* * *





kulattaik keṭukkumām kuraṅku.

It is said that a monkey will destroy its own species.





* * *





kulamakaṭku aḻaku taṉ koḻunaṉaip pēṇutal.

It becomes a lawful wife to honour her husband.





* * *





kulamakaṉ kulattukku aḻuvāṉ, mūkkaṟaiyaṉ mūkkukku aḻuvāṉ.

The high-born mourns the loss of caste, and the man who has lost his nose, his nose.





* * *





kulamum oṉṟu kuṟiyum oṉṟu.

Birth and external sign are at variance.





* * *





kulam pukuntum kuṟai tīravillai.

Though he has entered another tribe he is still in want.





* * *





kulam kulattōṭē veḷḷam āṟṟōṭē.

Tribe goes with tribe, the flood with the river.





* * *





kulam kulattōṭē veḷḷāṭu taṉṉōṭē.

A tribe associates with its own tribe, and goats follow their own kind.





* * *





kulam kuppaiyilē paṇam pantiyilē.

High birth lies on the dunghill, while wealth is at the festive board.





* * *





kulavittai kaṟṟup pāti kallāmaṟ pāti.

Arts appropriate to a family are partly intuitive and partly acquired.





* * *





kulaikkiṟa nāy kaṭikka aṟiyātu.

A barking dog does not bite.





* * *





kulaikkiṟa nāykku elumpaip pōṭṭāṟpōla.

Like throwing a bone to a barking dog.





* * *





kulaikkiṟa nāy vēṭṭai piṭikkumā?

Will a barking dog avail in hunting?





* * *





kulaikkiṟa nāyiṉ vāyilē kōlaik koṭuttāl ūr eṅkum koṇṭōṭik kulaikkum.

If a staff be put into the mouth of a noisy dog, it will carry it through the village barking.





* * *





kulaiyāta nāy kutiṅkālaik kaṭikkum.

A silent dog will bite the heels.





* * *





kuḻantai paṭṭiṉiyum kōyil paṭṭiṉiyum illai.

Infants and temple servants do not suffer from hunger.





* * *





kuḻantaikkum nāykkum kuṭipōkac cantōṣam.

Children and dogs are pleased with a change of place.





* * *





kuḻantaiyum teyvamum koṇṭāṭiṉa iṭattilē.

Children and gods best agree with their admirers.





* * *





kuḻantaik kāyccalum kuṇṭaṉi kāyccalum pollātu.

The fever of children and the spite of a slanderer are bad.





* * *





kuḻantai tūṅkukiṟatu ellām ammaiyārukku ilāpam.

The sleep of a child is advantageous to the mother.





* * *





kuḻantaiyiṉ tēkampōl.

Tender as the body of an infant.





* * *





kuḻantāy kuḻiyil amiḻntātē.

My child, do not sink into the pit.





* * *





kuḻappaṭi kaṇṭu tiraikaṭal ēṅkumā?

Has the stormy weather set the whole sea in motion?





* * *





kuḻampup pāl kuṭikkavum kumarā kaṇṭavaliyā?

O my youthful son, is your throat pained by drinking thick milk?





* * *





kuḻiyiṟ payirai eṭuttuk kūraimēl iṭalāmā?

Is it right to train a parasite to the roof?





* * *





kuḻiyiṟ piḷḷaiyai nari cuṟṟukiṟāppōlē.

Like a jackal going round the grave of a child.





* * *





kuḷattaik kalakkip paruntukku irai iṭalāmā?

Is it proper to agitate the tank to supply prey for kites?





* * *





kuḷattukku maḻai kuntāṇiyā?

Are rain drops the weight of a mortar to a tank?





* * *





kuḷattiṟ pōṭṭuk kiṇaṟṟil tēṭalāmā?

Having put it into the tank, do you seek it in the well?





* * *





kuḷattōṭu kōpittuk kāl kaḻuvātavaṉpōl.

Like the man who would not wash his feet in the tank because he was angry with it.





* * *





kuḷap paṭi nīr iṟaittālum kaṭaṟ paḷḷam nirampumā?

Will the hollow of the sea be filled by draining a tank down to the lowest step?





* * *





kuḷam uṭaintu pōkumpōtu muṟaivītamā?

When a tank bursts, do they dispute about turns?





* * *





kuḷam kākkiṟavaṉ taṇṇīraik kuṭiyāṉō?

Will not he who watches the tank drink?





* * *





kuḷam vaṟṟiyum muṟaivītam uṇṭā?

Why dispute about your turn for drawing water seeing that the tank is dried up?





* * *





kuḷam veṭṭumuṉṉē mutalai kuṭi irukkumā?

Before the tank is dug will the alligator go to dwell therein?





* * *





kuḷavik kūṭṭaik kōlāl kulaittāṟpōla.

Like poking a wasp's nest with a stick.





* * *





kuḷavik kūṭṭilē kalluviṭṭu eṟikiṟatā?

What! throw a stone at a wasp's nest?





* * *





kuḷavikkup paccaip puḻu piḷḷai.

A green caterpillar is the offspring of a wasp.





* * *





kuḷavi puḻuvait taṉ niṟam ākkuvatupōla.

Like a wasp changing worms to its own colour.





* * *





kuḷikkappōyc cēṟṟaip pūcikkoṇṭatu pōla.

As one smeared himself with mud After bathing.





* * *





kuḷirnta koḷḷiyāy iruntu kuṭiyaik keṭukkalāmā?

Pretending to be an extinguished firebrand, is it proper to destroy the house?





* * *





kuḷḷap pārppāṉ paḷḷattil viḻuntāṉ taṇṭu eṭu taṭi eṭu.

The dwarf Brahman has fallen into a pit, take a staff, take a stick.





* * *





kuḷḷaṉaik koṇṭu āḻam pārkkiṟāṉ.

He sounds the depth by means of a dwarf.





* * *





kuḷḷaṉ kuṭi keṭuppāṉ kuḷḷaṉ peṇcāti ūraik keṭuppāḷ.

A dwarf will destroy a family; his wife will destroy the whole village.





* * *





kuṟatti piḷḷai peṟṟāl kuṟavaṉ kāyam tiṉpāṉ.

If the wife of a mountaineer is brought to bed, her husband take the prescribed stimulant.





* * *





kuṟa vaḻakkum iṭai vaḻakkum koñcattil tīrātu.

Disputes among mountaineers and shepherds are not easily settled.





* * *





kuṟa vaḻakkukkuc cakkili vaḻakku ilēcu.

Disputes among chakliyars are more easily settled than those among mountaineers.





* * *





kuṟuṇip pāl kaṟantapōtilum kūrai piṭuṅkap pārttirukkalāmā?

May one suffer his cow to destroy his roof although she yields a kuruni of milk at a meal?





* * *





kuṟuṇi maitāṉ iṭṭālum kuruṭu kuruṭē.

Though a kuruni of collyrium be applied to the eyes of the blind, the blindness remains.





* * *





kuṟumpiyuḷḷa kātu tiṉavu tiṉṉum.

A ceruminous ear will itch.





* * *





kuṟumpait tavirkkum kuṭitāṅki.

The supporter who puts an end to inhuman acts.





* * *





kuṟai aṟak kaṟṟavaṉ kōṭiyil oruvaṉ.

A thoroughly learned man is one of a lac.





* * *





kuṟai kuṭam taḷampum niṟai kuṭam taḷampātu.

A water pot not full is agitated, a full pot is not.





* * *





kuṟaiyac colli niṟaiya aḷa.

Give more than you profess to sell.





* * *





kuṟaiyuḷḷōrkku uṇṭu kuṟāvutal kaṟaiyuḷḷōrkku uṇṭu karavu.

The guilty are dejected, those that have a tarnished reputation elude observation.





* * *





kuṟai vēlaiyaik kurukkaḷukkum kāṭṭal ākātu.

An unfinished work ought not to be shown even to a Guru-master.





* * *





kuṟai vēlaiyai ampalattiṟ koṇṭuvaralāmā?

Is it proper to expose an unfinished work for sale or otherwise?





* * *





kuṟṟamuḷḷa neñcu kuṟukuṟu eṉṉum kuṟumpiyuḷḷa kātu tiṉavu tiṉṉum.

A guilty conscience is agitated, a foul ear itches.





* * *





kuṟṟam maṟaippatil maṟṟoru kuṟṟamum cērum.

By concealing one fault another may arise.





* * *





kuṟṟa maṉaccāṭci kūṭi vāḻ catturu.

A guilty conscience is an enemy that lives with its possessor.





* * *





kuṟṟam aṭainta kīrtti kuṇaṅkoḷvatu aritu.

Distinction attained by wrong makes reformation difficult.





* * *





kuṟṟat taṇṭaṉaiyilum cutta taṇṭaṉai nallatu.

Undeserved punishment is better than deserved punishment.





* * *





kuṟṟam pārkkiṟ cuṟṟam illai.

If every fault be noticed, all intercourse must cease.





* * *





kuṉṟimaṇikkum kuṇṭiyiṟ kaṟuppu.

Even a kunrimani seed-abrus precatorius-has a black spot on it.





* * *





kū.



kūkaikkup pakalil kaṇ teriyātu.

An owl cannot see in the day-time.





* * *





kūkai viḻittāṟpōla viḻikkiṟāṉ.

He stares like an owl.





* * *





kūṭak kuṭi iruntukoṇṭu koḷḷi corukalāmā?

Whilst residing with one shall we thrust a firebrand into his house?





* * *





kūṭattaik koṭuttālum māṭattaik koṭukkal ākātu.

Although you may give an outer room, it is not proper to give an inner one.





* * *





kūṭam oṉṟu pōṭumuṉṉē, cutti iraṇṭu pōṭum.

Before the big hammer strikes one, the little hammer strikes two.





* * *





kūṭam iṭittāl māṭam.

If the party-wall be broken down, one room only will remain.





* * *





kūṭi iruntu kulāvuvār vīṭṭil ōṭi uṇṇum kūḻum iṉitē.

Gruel served in the house of a united family is enjoyable.





* * *





kūṭṭattiṟ kaṭṭuccōṟu aviḻttatupōla.

As food prepared for a journey was untied in a crowd.





* * *





kūṭṭōṭē pōccutu kuḷirum kāyccalum.

Shivering and fever left with the body.





* * *





kūṇṭilē kuṟuṇi nel iruntāl mūlaiyilē mukkuṟuṇit teyvam kūttāṭum.

When there is a kuruni of paddy in the bin, three kurunies of gods will be dancing in a corner.





* * *





kūttarici kuttukiṟa vīṭṭil vāykku aricikku vaḻi irātu.

In the house where rice is pounded for sale, there is not enough to put into the mouth of a corpse.

Before a corpse is removed the females of the family place a little raw rice near the mouth, the males do the same in the cemetry before the body is laid on the pyre.





* * *





kūttāṭṭuc cilampam paṭaiveṭṭukku ākumā?

Will the art of fencing avail in a battle-field?





* * *





kūttāṭikkuk kīḻē kaṇ kūlikkāraṉukku mēlē kaṇ.

A rope-dancer directs his eye downwards, one who bears a burden upwards.





* * *





kūttikku iṭṭuk kuraṅku āṉāṉ vēcikku iṭṭu viṟaku āṉāṉ.

By wasting his substance on concubines he has become spare as a monkey, and by giving to common women he has become as dry as a stick.





* * *





kūttip piḷḷaikkut takappaṉ ār?

Who is the legal father of the child of a concubine?





* * *





kūttiyār āttāḷ cettāl koṭṭum muḻakkum kūttiyār cettāl oṉṟum illai.

When the mother of one's concubine dies, there will be beating of drums, but when the concubine dies, there will be no such display.





* * *





kūttiyār cettāl piṇam, avaḷ tāy cettāl maṇam.

When a concubine dies she is a mere corpse, whereas if her mother dies, her remains are honoured.

By honouring the remains of her mother it is supposed that a man secures greater respect from his concubine.





* * *





kūttiyār vīṭṭukku nāypōl alaikiṟāṉ.

He frequents his concubine's house as a dog wandering about.





* * *





kūttup pārkkap pōṉa iṭattil pēy piṭittatupōla.

As one was seized by a demon when he went to see a comedy.





* * *





kūriya collāṉ ārilum vallaṉ.

He whose words are keen, is of all the most powerful.





* * *





kūrukku etir utaittal cūreḻa varuttum.

Kicking against thorns will cause pain.





* * *





kūrai ēṟik kōḻi piṭikkamāṭṭāta kurukkaḷ vāṉam kīṟi vaikuṇṭam kāṭṭuvārā?

If a guru is not able to go to the house-top to catch a fowl, how can he rend the heavens and show vikundam to his disciple?





* * *





kūraimēlē cōṟu pōṭṭāl āyiram kākam varum.

If rice is thrown on the roof, a thousand crows will come.





* * *





kūrmaiyāḷaṉē nērmaiyāḷaṉ.

The acute man is the upright man.





* * *





kūli kuṟaittāl vēlai keṭum.

If the hire be diminished, the work will be spoiled.





* * *





kūli kuṟaittāyē kuṟai marakkāl iṭṭāyē.

Thou hast reduced my wages, and used false measures when paying me.





* * *





kūlikkāraṉ peṇṭāṭṭi piḷḷai peṟappōkiṟāḷām kuppaiyilē āmaṇakku muḷaikkap pōkiṟatām.

It is said that the wife of a labourer is about to be confined, and that a castor plant will spring upon the midden.





* * *





kūlikku nāṟṟu naṭa vantavaṉukku ellaikku vaḻakkō?

Does he who came on hire to transplant grain institute a boundary suit?





* * *





kūlikkup pāvi kuṟukkē vantāṉē.

A vicious fellow prevented the payment of my wages.





* * *





kūlikkuk kaḻu ēṟuvārkaḷā?

Will they hire themselves to be impaled?





* * *





kūlip paṭai veṭṭumā?

Will mere hirelings conquer?





* * *





kūḻ āṉālum kuppai āṉālum kuṭittukkoṇṭavaṉ piḻaippāṉ.

Whether gruel or refuse, he who has drunk it will live.





* * *





kūḻukku māṅkāy tōṟkumā?

Will an unripe mango fail before gruel?





* * *





kūḻukku māṅkāy koṇṭāṭṭam kuraṅkukkuk tēṅkāy koṇṭāṭṭam.

An unripe mango improves the gruel, the kernel of a coconut is a feast to a monkey.





* * *





kūḻukkum ācai mīcaikkum ācai.

He wishes to drink the gruel, and he also wants a nice mustache.

Spoken of things incompatible.





* * *





kūḻukkuk koṟaṭā miḷakāy.

A chilly serves as a whip to gruel.

It makes it pungent.





* * *





kūḻ eṉṟālum vāḻvukkut takkatāka kuṭikkattāṉ vēṇṭum.

Though only gruel, it must be drunk agreeably to one's circumstances.





* * *





kūḻ eṉṟālum kuṭittavaṉ piḻaippāṉ.

If only gruel, he who drinks it will live.





* * *





kūḻ kuṭikkilum kūṭṭu ākātu.

Fellowship is undesirable even in drinking gruel.





* * *





kūḻ puḷittateṉṟum māṅkāy puḷittateṉṟum uṇarāmaṟ collalāmā?

Is it proper to say thoughtlessly that the gruel was sour, and then again that it was the mango that was sour?





* * *





kūṉaṉaik koṇṭu kuḻappaṭi māmi kāṇikkup piḷḷai peṟa.

O, mother-in-law, imcite a quarrel through the hunchbacked that an heir may be born.





* * *





kūṉaṉ kuṟai tīrntāṉ koṭiyōṉ viṉai peyarntāṉ.

The hunchbacked is relieved, the cruel man has escaped.





* * *





kūṉi āṉālum kūṭai cumantutāṉ kūli peṟa vēṇṭum.

Although her back is crooked, she will have to carry the basket before she gets her hire.





* * *





kūṉi vāyāṟ keṭṭāṟpōla.

As she (Kaikesi) was demoralized by listening to the words of Kuni.





* * *





ke.



keṭu kālattiṟkuk keṭṭōr puttiyaik kēṭpār.

They will listen to the advice of the wicked when their star is not favourable.





* * *





keṭu kuṭi coṟ kēḷātu.

A decaying family will not listen to advic.e.





* * *





keṭukka niṉaikkil aṭukkak kēṭuṟum.

When you mediate another's ruin, your own will soon follow.





* * *





keṭukkiṉum kalvi kēṭupaṭātu.

Learning can suffer no damage.





* * *





keṭuppārait teyvam keṭukkum.

The deity will destroy those that injure others.





* * *





keṭuppatum vāyāl paṭippatum vāyāl.

It is with the mouth we injure others; it is with the mouth we read.





* * *





keṭumati kaṇṇukkut tōṉṟātu.

One is blind to his own imprudence.





* * *





keṭumatikkup paṭukuḻiyai veṭṭu.

Make a pitfall for imprudence.





* * *





keṭuvāy kēṭu niṉaiyātē.

Thou wilt be ruined, think not of destroying another.





* * *





keṭuvāṉ kēṭu niṉaippāṉ.

He who is on the way to ruin, will mediate another's ruin.





* * *





keṭṭa kālattukku nārai keḷiṟṟai eṭuttu viḻuṅkiṉatu pōla.

As a crane in an evil time seized and swallowed a keliru fish.





* * *





keṭṭa nāykkup paṭṭatu pirīti.

The wretched dog feels satisfied with the beating he received.





* * *





keṭṭa ūrukku eṭṭu vārttai.

A village doomed to ruin profits not by repeated precaution.





* * *





keṭṭa kēṭṭukku vaṭṭam kāṟpaṇamā?

Is a premium of half a fanam demanded of one who is reduced in circumstances?





* * *





keṭṭa pāl nalla pāl ākumā?

Will curdled milk become sweet again?





* * *





keṭṭatum paṭṭatum kīraikku iṟaittatum pōtum.

I have had enough of loss, sufferings and toil in watering the vegetable garden.





* * *





keṭṭa kuṭikku oru tuṣṭap piḷḷai.

A vicious child in a poor family.





* * *





keṭṭa kuṭikku oru kēṭṭai piṟantatu.

A child is born under the star kèttai in a poor family.





* * *





keṭṭa kaḻutaikkut tuṣṭaputti.

A vicious donkey has mischievous propensities.





* * *





keṭṭavaṉ kuṭi keṭṭatu vaṭṭi naṣṭam illāmal vāṅkavē vāṅku.

The family is ruined, do not fail to get the interest without reduction.





* * *





keṭṭavaṉ kuṭi keṭaṭṭum nī kuṭi miḷakucāṟṟai.

No matter whose family is ruined, you drink the pepper water.





* * *





keṭṭa kuṭiyē keṭum paṭṭa kālilē paṭum.

The decaying family will be ruined. It will hit the leg which is already struck.





* * *





keṭṭa māṭu tēṭumuṉṉam eṭṭu māṭu tēṭalām.

You may procure eight cows before you can recover one that has strayed.





* * *





keṭṭa mārkkattilē irukkiṟa oruvaṉ maṟṟavarkaḷaiyum atil iḻukkap pirayācappaṭuvāṉ.

He who pursues a vicious course will try to lead others into the same.





* * *





keṭṭārkku uṟṟār kiḷaiyilum illai.

The impoverished have no friends even among their own kindred.





* * *





keṭṭār vāḻntāl kiḷaikiḷaiyāyt taḷirppār vāḻntār keṭṭāl vaṟaiyōṭṭukkum ākār.

When the ruined in circumstances flourish they cast out innumerable branches; when the prosperous are reduced to poverty they are not worth a potsherd.





* * *





keṭṭāṉ payal poṭṭalilē, viḻuntāṉ payal caṟukkalilē.

The boy suffered by walking in the arid tract, he fell in the slippery ground.





* * *





keṭṭālum ceṭṭi ceṭṭiyē kiḻintālum paṭṭu paṭṭē.

Though reduced in circumstances a chetty is a chetty; silk is silk though never so torn.





* * *





keṭṭikkāraṉ puḷuku eṭṭu nāḷukkuḷ teriyum.

The deception of the clever will be manifest in eight days.





* * *





keṭṭikkāraṉukkup payam illai maṭṭittaṉaṉukku nayam illai.

The clever fear nothing; the fool gains nothing.





* * *





keṭṭikkāraṉ poṭṭu eṭṭu nāḷ aḷavum.

The reputation of the adroit lasts only eight days.





* * *





keṭṭum paṭṭaṇam cēr.

Though you are ruined, cling to the city.





* * *





keṭṭuppōkiṟa kālam vantāl tuṣṭa putti tōṉṟātā?

When the time of destruction comes, will not evil devices operate?





* * *





keṭṭuppōṉa pārppāṉukkuc cettupōkiṟa pacuvait tāṉam paṇṇiṉatupōla.

Like giving a dying cow to a poor Brahman.





* * *





keṭṭuk keṭṭuk kuṭi ākiṟatā?

Can a family survive repeated misfortunes?





* * *





keṇṭaiyaippōṭṭu varālai iḻukkiṟatupōla.

Baiting with a small fish and catching a large one.





* * *





keraṭi kaṟṟavaṉ iṭaṟi viḻuntāl atuvum oru varicai eṉpāṉ.

If the fencer fall, he will pretend he did so according to the rules of his art.





* * *





keliyaṉ pāṟ cōṟu kaṇṭatupōla.

As the greedy saw milk and rice.





* * *





kē.



kēṭatu illāṉ pāṭatu illāṉ.

One free from loss, free from suffering.





* * *





kēṭu kālattukku ōṭu kappaṟai.

A bad time is followed by an alms-dish.





* * *





kēṭu keṭṭa nāyē vīṭu viṭṭup pōvēṉ.

You worthless dog quit my house.





* * *





kēṭu varum piṉṉē mati keṭṭu varum muṉṉē, āṉaivarum piṉṉē maṇi ōcai varum muṉṉē.

Indigence follows indiscretion, the elephent makes its appearance after the sound of its bells reaches the ear.





* * *





kēṭṭatu ellām nampātē nampiṉatu tellām collātē.

Believe not all you hear, tell not all you believe.





* * *





kēṭṭil uṟuti kūṭṭum uṭaimai.

Fortitude under privations will tend to restore lost fortune.





* * *





kēṭpār collaik kēṭṭuk keṭātē.

Be not deceived by hearsay reports.





* * *





kēṇmait tāṉavā vāymaik kāṉavā.

Thou the self-existent, thou art the powerful.





* * *





kēḷum kiḷaiyum keṭṭōrkku illai.

Friends and relations are denied to the ruined.





* * *





kēḷvic ceviyaṉ ūraik keṭuttāṉ.

He who acted on hearsay destroyed the village.





* * *





kēḷvi uṭamai kīrttiyē kalvi.

Instruction is wealth, and learning is fame.





* * *





kēḷvip pēccil pātitāṉ nicam.

But half of what one hears is true.





* * *





kēḷvi muyal

Endeavour to obtain instruction.





* * *





kai.



kai aḻuttamāṉavaṉ karaiyēṟa māṭṭāṉ.

The miser will not prosper.

Literally will not “get ashore.” The marriage of a girl is phrased in the same way.





* * *





kai illātavaṉ karaṇam pōṭalāmā, kāl illātavaṉ ōṭalāmā?

Can one who has no hands turn a somersault, can one who has no feet run about?





* * *





kai uṇṭāvatu kaṟṟavarkku āmē.

It is the learned that have hands.





* * *





kai ūṉṟik karaṇam pōṭavēṇṭum.

One has to fix his hands on the ground before he makes a somersault.





* * *





kai kāyntāṟ kamuku kāykkum.

If the palm of the hand becomes callous by drawing water, the areca will bear.





* * *





kaikku eṭṭiṉatu vāykku eṭṭavillai.

That which reaches the hand does not always reach the mouth.





* * *





kaikkōḷaṉukkuk kāṟpuṇṇum nāykkut talaippuṇṇum pōla.

Like a sore on the leg of a weaver, and one on the head of a dog.





* * *





kai tappik kaṇṇilē paṭṭāṟ kaiyait taṟittup pōṭuvārkaḷā?

If the hand by accident strike the eye, will they cut it off?





* * *





kai tappiṉāṟ karaṇam.

If the hand missess its hold, a somersault.





* * *





kai niṟainta poṉ illāviṭṭālum kaṇ niṟainta kaṇavaṉ irukka vēṇṭum.

Though not possessed of a handfull of gold, one should have a husband that fills the eye.





* * *





kai paṭṭāl kaṇṇāṭi.

Handling makes a thing shine as glass.





* * *





kaippaḻattaik koṭuttut tuṟaṭṭip paḻattukku aṇṇāntu niṟpāṉēṉ.

Why give the fruit in your hand and stand gazing at that which can only be reached by a hook?





* * *





kaipporuḷ taṉṉil meypporuḷ kalvi.

Learning is better than riches.





* * *





kaipporuḷ illā vaḻippōkkaṉ kaḷvar muṉ paṭalām.

The wayfaring man who has no money may encounter thieves with impunity.





* * *





kaipporuḷ aṟṟāl kaṭṭukkaḻuttiyum pārāḷ.

If destitute of wealth, even one’s own married wife will not regard him.





* * *





kaimpeṇṭāṭṭi peṟṟa kaḻikaṭai.

The refuse that the widow has brought forth.





* * *





kaimpeṇcāti erumaiyil kai paḻaka vantāṉ.

He came to the buffalo of the widow to learn to milk.





* * *





kaiyāl kiḷḷi eṟiyum vēlaikkuk kaṉatta kōṭāli vēṇṭumō?

Is a heavy axe required for a work that may be easily effected by the hand?





* * *





kaiyāl kiḻikkum paṉaṅkiḻaṅkiṟku āppum vallīṭṭukkuṟṟiyum ēṉ?

Why a wedge and a mallet to split the edible root of the palmyra?





* * *





kaiyilum illai ceṭṭiyār paiyilum illai kācu.

There is no money either in the hands or purse of the chetty.





* * *





kaiyilum maṭiyilum illātavaṉaik kaḷvaṉ eṉṉa ceyyalām?

What harm can a thief do to him who has nothing in hand or in his belt?





* * *





kaiyilē mañcaḷ āṉāl kāriyam mañcūrtāṉ.

If the hands are dipped in turmeric water, the undertaking will prove successful.





* * *





kaiyil irukka neyyilē kaiyiṭuvāṉēṉ?

When it (the stolen thing) is in the hand, why put the hand in (hot) ghee?





* * *





kaiyil illātavaṉ kaḷḷaṉ.

He who has nothing in hand is a thief.





* * *





kaiyil ariciyum kamaṇṭalattil taṇṇīrum.

Rice in the hand and water in the pot.





* * *





kaiyil eṭukkumuṉṉam kōḻi mōcameṉṟu aṟiyātu.

The fowl is not aware of its danger before it is caught.





* * *





kaiyiṟ puṇṇukkuk kaṇṇāṭi vēṇṭumā?

Is a mirror required for a sore in the hand?





* * *





kaiyiṟ paṟavaiyai viṭṭuk kāṭṭup paṟavaikkuk kaṇṇi vaikkalāmā?

Why let a bird in the hand go and snare one in the jungle?





* * *





kaiyiṟ piḷḷaiyōṭu kaṭalil viḻuntēṉ.

I have fallen into the sea with the child in my arms.





* * *





kaiyiṟ kuṭaiyum kāliṟ cōṭum vēṇṭum.

One must have an umbrella in the hand, and shoes on the feet.





* * *





kaiyiṟ kiṭaitta amutaik kamaril ūṟṟalāmā?

May one pour into the cracked ground the nectar he has in his hand?





* * *





kaiyiṟ kācu vāyil tōcai āmā?

Will the money in the hand become a cake in the mouth?





* * *





kaiyum kaṇakkum cari.

The money in hand is just sufficient for current expenses.





* * *





kaiyaip pār mukattaip pār eṉṟu iruntāṟ kāriyam ākumā?

If you say look at the hands and look at the face, will the work be accomplished?





* * *





kaiviṉai karavēl.

Neglect not a work in which you are skilful.





* * *





ko.



kokkarippārkkuc cuvarkkamō neruppiṟ kutippārkkuc cuvarkkamō?

Which attains swerga the mere boaster, or the self-immolated?





* * *





kokku iḷaṅkuñcum kōṇāta teṅkum kaṇṭatu illai.

No one has seen a young crane, not a straight cocoanut tree.





* * *





kokkukku uṇṭā vīra caivam?

Does the stork observe the rules of Linga-worship?

The strict Saivite is a rigid vegetarian.





* * *





kokkuk kūṭṭattil rājāḷi viḻuntāṟpōla.

As if a falcon fell among herons.





* * *





kokkut tiṉṉap peruccāḷi pāyntāṟpōl.

As if a bandycoote rushed on to prey on a stork.





* * *





kokkukkut teriyumā kōḻikkuñcaik koṇṭupōka?

Does the crane know how to carry away a chicken?





* * *





kokkeṉṟu niṉaittāyō koṅkaṇā?

O devotee, did you mistake me for a stork?





* * *





koṅkilē kuṟuṇi viṟkiṟatu, iṅku eṉṉattiṟku.

Why remain here when grain sells cheap in the Kongu country.





* * *





kocukiṉ mutukilē piḷavai vantatupōla.

As a carbuncle appeared on the back of a mosquito.





* * *





kocukilē kuṟuṇi pāl kaṟakkalāmā?

Can you draw from a mosquito a kuruni of milk?





* * *





kocukilē piḷavai atilē nīraḻivu, aṟukkiṟatu eṅkē aṭṭai viṭukiṟatu eṅkē?

A mosquito suffering from a carbuncle, has also diabetes: where shall we put in the lancet, and how apply leeches?





* * *





kocukē kocukē talai muḻuku nāṉ māṭṭēṉ caṉikkiḻamai.

O mosquito, mosquito, bathe your head; I will not, it is Saturday.





* * *





kocukaip poruṭṭāy eṇṇik karuṭaṉ etirttāṟpōla.

As if a hawk assailed a mosquito imagining it to be a rival.





* * *





kocukai vaṭikaṭṭi oṭṭakattai viḻuṅkukiṟatā?

What! do you strain out a gnat and swallow a camel?





* * *





kocukai vaṭippār, acukaippaṭuvār.

Those who strain out gnats are naturally suspected.





* * *





koccikkup pōṉavaṉ ceytiyaip pār, taṉ kuruvai viṟṟavaṉ ceytiyaip pār.

See the result of his having gone to Cochin, and of one who betrayed his Guru.





* * *





koñcattil irukkiṟatā kuraṅku miḷakunīr kuṭikkiṟatu?

What, is it a small thing for a monkey to drink pepper water?





* * *





koñcattil uṇmai illātavaṉ kōṭiyilum irukka māṭṭāṉ.

Not one even of ten millions is unfaithful in a little thing.





* * *





koṭikaḷ arukāṉa marattilē paṭarum.

Creepers spread over the trees that grow near them.





* * *





koṭikkuk kāy kaṉattirukkumā?

Is its fruit burdensome to the creeper?





* * *





koṭi cuṟṟip peṇ piṟantāl kōttirattiṟku ākātu.

It is an evil omen to a tribe for a girl to be born with her navel-string round the body.





* * *





koṭukkiṟāṉ paḻaṉiyāṇṭi, tiṉkiṟāṉ cuppaṉāṇṭi.

Palaniándi gives and Subbanándi eats.





* * *





koṭukka māṭṭātavaṉ kūttaip paḻittāṉ.

He who was not disposed to contribute to the drama spoke disparagingly of it.





* * *





koṭukkiṟataiyum koṭuttuk kuṣṭarōki kālil viḻuvāṉēṉ?

Whilst bestowing gifts why fall prostrate at the feet of a leper?





* * *





koṭukkāta iṭaiyaṉ ciṉaiyāṭṭaik kāṭṭiṉatupōla.

As a niggardly shepherd pointed to a sheep that was with young.





* * *





koṭuṅkōl maṉṉaṉ āḷum nāṭṭiṟ kaṭumpuli vāḻum kāṭu naṉṟu.

A jungle inhabited by fierce tigers is better than a country ruled by a cruel tyrant.





* * *





koṭuṅkōl aracaṉkīḻ kuṭiyirukkal ākātu.

It is not good to live under a tyrant king.





* * *





koṭuttataik kēṭṭāl aṭuttu varum pakai.

If that which has been given be demanded hatred ensues.





* * *





koṭuttavaṉaip pukaḻvār koṭātavaṉai ikaḻvār.

They praise the liberal, but reproach the niggard.





* * *





koṭuttavaṉ appaṉ koṭātavaṉ cappaṉ.

The liberal are fathers, niggards are useless.





* * *





koṭuttataik koṭukkum kuṟaḷip picācu.

The dwarf demon returns only that which had been given to it.

Jugglery is generally attributed by the common people to the power of a dwarf demon which, retaining the materials entrusted to it, gives them one by one to the juggler as he may want them.





* * *





koṭuttavarukku ellām uṇṭu koṭātavarukku oṉṟum illai.

The liberal have all things, the niggardly nothing.





* * *





koṭuttu uṟavukoḷ, kōḷaṉ eṉṟu irēl.

Be liberal and friendly, avoid being called a talebearer.





* * *





koṭuppārait taṭukkātē.

Do not check the liberal.





* * *





koṭumai aṟṟavaṉ kaṭumai aṟṟavaṉ.

The kind-hearted is yielding.





* * *





koṭum pāvi āṉālum koṇṭa māmiyār vēṇṭum.

However cruel a mother-in-law may be, she is nevertheless desirable.





* * *





koṭṭikkiḻaṅku veṭṭukiṟavaḷukkuk kōyilil vantu āṭatteriyumā?

Can the woman who digs up roots dance before a temple idol?





* * *





koṭṭik koṭṭi aḷantālum kuṟuṇi patakku ākātu.

Though measured again and again, a kuruni will not become a pathakku.





* * *





koṭṭiṉāl tēḷ koṭṭā viṭṭāṟ piḷḷaippūcciyā?

If it stings it is scorpion, if it does not sting is it only a gryllus?





* * *





koṇṭavaṉ cīṟiṉāl kaṇṭavaṉukku ellām iḷakkāram.

If despised by her husband, all will slight her.





* * *





koṇṭavaṉuṭaiya palattaik kaṇṭāl kuppaimēṭu ēṟiccaṇṭai ceyvāḷ.

If she finds out the strength of her husband, she will get on the rubbish heap and fight.





* * *





koṇṭār muṉiyiṟ kaṇṭār muṉivar.

If husbands treat their wives angrily, others will do so too.





* * *





koṇṭu kulam pēcukiṟatā?

Do you speak lightly of a family into which you have chosen to marry?





* * *





koṇṭaikkup pū cūṭic caṇṭaikku niṟkiṟatu.

To stand up to quarrel with a chaplet of flowers on the tresses.





* * *





koṇṭaikkup pū cūṭukiṟatā tāṭikkup pū cūṭukiṟatā?

Are flowers for the tresses, or for the beard?





* * *





koṇṭōr ellām peṇṭir alla.

All that are betrothed are not real wives.





* * *





koycakattil koḷḷi vaittukkoṇṭatupōla.

Like keeping a firebrand in the border of her own cloth.





* * *





kollaṉ teruvil ūci vilaipōmā?

Can needles be sold in a street of smiths?





* * *





kollaṉ eḷimai kaṇṭu kuraṅku kālukkup pūṇ kaṭṭac coṉṉatām.

It is said that a monkey seeing the weakness of a blacksmith urged him to adorn his legs with rings.





* * *





kollaṉ piṇam viṟaittāṟpōla.

As the smith's corpse became stiff.





* * *





kollukiṟatum cōṟu piḻaippikkiṟatum cōṟu.

Rice kills and it makes alive.





* * *





kollaik kāṭṭu nari pallaik kāṭṭiṉatupōla.

Like a wild jackal showing his teeth.





* * *





kollaik kāṭṭil nariyaik kuṭi vaittukkoṇṭatupōl.

Like encouraging jackals in a field.





* * *





kollaikkup palli kuṭikkuc cakuṉi.

He is a palli plant,-Bachnera Asiatica-to a garden, and a Saguni to a family.

Both are injurious.





* * *





kollaiyil kuṟṟiyai aṭainta pullu uḻavar uḻupaṭaikkuk keṭumō?

Will the ploughshare destroy the grass at the foot of scarecrows in the field?





* * *





kollai pāḻāṉālum kuruvikku irai pañcamā?

Are small birds famished because the fields lie waste?





* * *





koḻukkaṭṭaikkut talaiyum illai kuṭiyaṉukku muṟaiyum illai.

The steamed cake has no head, nor has a drunkard sense to regard the rules of relationship.





* * *





koḻukkaṭṭaikkut talaiyum illaik kālum illai, kuṟavarukku neṟiyum illai muṟaiyum illai.

The kolukkattai has neither head nor foot, foresters are neither virtuous nor mannerly.





* * *





koḻuttavaṉ kaikku iḷaittavaṉ turumpu.

He who is thin is a mere straw to him who is fat.





* * *





koḷ eṉṟāl vāyait tiṟakkiṟatu, kaṭivāḷam eṉṟāl vāyai mūṭukiṟatā?

What! opening the mouth when one says gram, and covering it when one says bridle?





* * *





koḷvārum illaik koṭuppārum illai.

There are neither buyers nor sellers.





* * *





koḷḷi koṇṭu talai coṟikiṟatā?

Do you scratch your head with a firebrand?





* * *





koḷḷikku etir pōṉālum veḷḷikku etir pōkal ākātu.

Though you may encounter a firebrand, you must not appear before the planet Venus.





* * *





koḷḷik kaṭṭaiyāl cuṭṭāl koppaḷikkumeṉṟu vāḻaippaḻam koṇṭu vaṭuvaṭuvāyc cuṭukiṟāy.

Because burning with a firebrand will produce blisters, you are branding me with a plantain fruit,





* * *





koḷḷumvaraiyil koṇṭāṭṭam koṇṭapiṟaku tiṇṭāṭṭam.

Great pleasure till attained, great misery afterwards.





* * *





koḷḷaikkup pōṉālum kūṭṭu ākātu.

Even when going to plunder association is bad.





* * *





koḷḷaikkum ūḻikkum tappu.

Escape from plunder and pestilence.





* * *





koṟṟavaṉ taṉṉilum kaṟṟavaṉ mikkōṉ.

The learned are more than kings.





* * *





koṉṟāṟ pāvam tiṉṟāl tīrum.

The sin arising from killing is expiated by eating the flesh so killed.





* * *





kō.



kōṭālikkāmpu kulattukku īṉam.

The handle of the axe is the enemy of its kind.





* * *





kōṭi cīmāṉum kōvaṇa āṇṭiyum cariyā?

Are the wealthy and mendicants on a par?





* * *





kōṭi cīmāṉ tuṇiyavēṇṭum, allatu kōvaṇa āṇṭi tuṇiyavēṇṭum.

An attempt is to be made either by the wealthy, or by a beggar that has only a span-long cloth.





* * *





kōṭi taṉam iruntālum kuṇam illā maṅkaiyai maṇam muṭikkal ākātu.

It is not good to marry a girl, no matter how great her wealth, if her natural temper is exceptionable.





* * *





kōṭīcuvaraṉ ākavēṇṭumō, allatu lakṣātipati ākavēṇṭumō?

Which is to be preferred as a possession, ten millions, or a hundred thousand?





* * *





kōṭai iṭi iṭittāṟpōla.

As it thundered in summer.





* * *





kōṭ colpavaṉaik koṭum tēḷ eṉa niṉai.

Treat him who carries tales, as a scorpion.





* * *





kōṭ collum vāy kāṟṟuṭaṉ neruppu.

The mouth of the slanderer is as fire exposed to the wind.





* * *





kōṭṭāṉai maṭiyiṟ kaṭṭikkoṇṭatupōla.

Like concealing an ominous owl in the lap.





* * *





kōṭṭuc campā ākkivaittāl pōṭṭuc cāppiṭa varuvārkaḷ.

If samba rice is boiled, they will come and eat.





* * *





kōṭṭaiyil peṇ piṟantālum pōṭṭa eḻuttup pōkumā?

Will destiny be averted because the female was born in a fort?





* * *





kōṭṭaikkuḷḷē kuttum veṭṭumā?

Can a great battle be fought in a fort?





* * *





kōṇak kompu ēṟi eṉṉa kutirai ēṟi eṉṉa vīṇarkkum kīrttikkum veku tūram.

Whether they mount a palanquin or a horse, the distance between the vain and reputation is very great.





* * *





kōṇi koṇṭatu erutu cumantatu.

That which the sack contained the buffalo carried.





* * *





kōṇik kōṇik kōṭi koṭuppatilum kōṇāmaṟ kāṇi koṭuppatu nallatu.

It is better to bestow a small gift freely than lacs with a wry face.





* * *





kōttiram aṟintu peṇ koṭu, pāttiram aṟintu piccai iṭu
