nilai illāṉ vārttai nīrmēl eḻuttu.

The words of the unstable are letters on water.





* * *





nilaiyiṟ piriyēl.

Swerve not from rectitude.





* * *





nilaiyai viṭṭāl nīccu.

When out of your depth, swimming.





* * *





nillāta kālaṭi nīṇṭa tūram pōkum.

Ceaseless walking accomplishes great distances.





* * *





nillātu ētum nilaiyē kalvi.

Learning alone is enduring, all else is evanescent.





* * *





niḻalukkum kaḷavukkum ottatu ākkai.

The body may be compared to a shadow, and to theft,





* * *





niḻal arumai veyilil teriyum.

The salubrity of shade is realized in sunshine.





* * *





niḻal nallatu muciṟu pollātatu.

The shade is good, the ants are bad.





* * *





niṟainta āṟṟil peruṅkāyam karaittatupōla.

Like dissolving assafœtida in a flooded river.





* * *





niṟai potiyilē kaḻutai vāy vaittāṟpōla.

As an ass put its mouth into a full sack.





* * *





niṟaiyak kuṟuṇi vēṇṭām talai taṭavik kuṟuṇi koṭu.

I do not want a kuruni heaped up, give me a kuruni of grain level with the brim.





* * *





niṉaikkumuṉ varuvāṉ niṉaippatum taruvāṉ.

He will come before you think of him, he will give what you intend applying for.





* * *





niṉaitta nēram neṭumaḻai peyyumā?

Will heavy rain fall as we may wish?





* * *





niṉaittatu irukka niṉaiyātatu eytum, niṉaittatu vantālum vantu nērum.

When one thing is expected another may come, and that which is thought of may possibly come.





* * *





niṉṟa marattil neṭu maram pōṉāl niṟkum maramē neṭumaram.

When the lofty trees are felled, the remaining trees look tall.





* * *





niṉṟa veḷḷattaiyum vanta veḷḷam koṇṭu pōyiṟṟu.

The flood that was, has been swept away by the flood that followed.





* * *





niṉṟāl neṭu maram viḻuntāl paṉaimaram.

When standing, a tall tree, when fallen, a palmyrah tree.





* * *





niṉṟāṟpōl viḻuntāl talai uṭaiyum.

If you fall as you stand, your head will be broken.





* * *





nī.



nīcarāṉavar nilaipeṟak kallār.

The base do not persevere in study.





* * *





nīṭṭu vittai ēṟātu.

Boasted learning will not avail.





* * *





nīṇṭa kai neruppu aḷḷum.

The stretched hand will lade out fire.





* * *





nīṇṭa taccum kuṟukiya kollum.

Length to the carpenter, shortness to the smith.

The former can easily shorten wood by cutting, the smith can lengthen iron by heating.





* * *





nīṇṭa pul niṟka niḻalāmā?

Will long grass afford a shade?

A ready affirmation would answer this in central Africa.





* * *





nīti aṟṟa paṭṭaṇattil niṟaimaḻai peyyumā?

Will sufficient rain fall in a city where justice cannot be obtained?





* * *





nīti kēḷāmal talai veṭṭuvārkaḷā?

Will they cut off the head without judicial proceedings?





* * *





nītimāṉ tīviṉai ceyyiṟ piḻaippāṉā, nīti illātavaṉ nīti purintāl marippāṉā?

Will a virtuous man escape death if he do evil, will the unjust die if he do justice?





* * *





nī nīrālē vilakiṉāy nāṉ neruppālē vilakiṉēṉ.

You have escaped an accident by water, I have escaped one by fire.





* * *





nīntamāṭṭātavaṉai āṟu koṇṭupōkiṟatu.

The river carries away him who cannot swim.





* * *





nī piṟarkku utavi ceytāl teyvam uṉakku utavi ceyyum.

If you help others, God will help you.





* * *





nīrakam poruntiya ūrakattiru.

Live in a village where there is a good supply of water.





* * *





nīril eḻuttākum ākkai.

The body is an inscription on water.





* * *





nīriṟ kumiḻi carīram.

The body is a bubble on water.





* * *





nīrum kollum neruppum kollum.

Water kills, and fire also kills.





* * *





nīrai aṭittāl vēṟāmā?

Can water be divided by a stroke?





* * *





nīraic cintiṉaiyō cīraic cintiṉaiyō?

Did you spill water, or did you spill your character?





* * *





nīr āḻam kāṇalām neñcu āḻam kāṇappaṭātu.

The depth of water may be ascertained, but not the depth of the heart.





* * *





nīr uyara nel uyarum.

As the water rises, the rice plant rises.





* * *





nīr uḷḷa maṭṭum mīṉkuñcu tuḷḷum.

The young fish will sport as long as the water lasts.





* * *





nīr eṉṟu coṉṉāl neruppu aviyumā?

Can fire be quenched by pronouncing the word water?





* * *





nīr eṉṟu colla neruppāy muṭintatu.

When it was said to be water, it turned out to be fire.





* * *





nīrkkuṭ pācipōl vērkkoḷḷātu.

As moss in water, it will not take root.





* * *





nīr pōṉāl mīṉ tuḷḷumā?

When the water goes, will the fish leap about?





* * *





nīrmēṟ kumiḻipōl nilai illāk kāyam.

The body is unstable as a bubble on water.





* * *





nīr viḷaiyāṭēl.

Do not play in water.





* * *





nīlattukkuk kaṟuppu ūṭṭavēṇṭumā?

Is black dyed in blue?





* * *





nīlam kaṭṭuppaṭap pēcukiṟatu.

To speak so as to confine the blue dye to the skirts of the cloth.





* * *





nīlikkuk kaṇṇīr nimaiyilē.

A termagant has her tears in the eyelids.





* * *





nu.



nuṭpa puttimāṉ tiṭpa cittivāṉ.

The shrewed are successful.





* * *





nuṇṇiya ñāṉam uraippārkaḷ coṉṉatil oṉṟum naṭavārkaḷ.

They give wise counsels, but they themselves walk not according to any one of them.





* * *





nuṇṇiya karumamum eṇṇit tuṇi.

Although a small thing, do it after due thought.





* * *





nuṇporuḷ koṭuttu nuṇṇiyarāvar.

They become possessed of discrimination by imparting instruction on abstruse subjects.





* * *





nuṇmai nukarēl.

Be not particular in food.





* * *





nurai ottatuvē taraiyiṟ pavuṣu.

The affluence of this world is as froth.





* * *





nuraiyait tiṉṟāl pacipōkātu.

Hunger cannot be satisfied by eating froth.





* * *





nuḻai viṭṭu cey nūl kaṟṟu aṭaṅku.

Build a house with an entrance, acquire learning and be humble.





* * *





nuḷaiyaṉ aṟivāṉā rattiṉattiṉ perumai?

Does a fisherman understand the value of gems?





* * *





tuḷaiyaṉ pēccu ampalam ēṟātu.

The words of a fisherman will not reach the assembly.





* * *





nuṉikkompil ēṟi aṭikkompu veṭṭuvārkaḷā?

Having ascended to the extremity of the bough, will they sever it from the tree?





* * *





nū.



nūl illāmal mālai kōttatupōla.

Like making a garland of flowers without a string.





* * *





nūl kaṟṟavaṉē mēlavaṉ āvāṉ.

The learned only will gain eminence.





* * *





nūṟu āṇṭu ākilum kalviyai nōkku.

Though a hundred years old, diligently acquire knowledge.





* * *





nūṟu kuṟṟam āṟu piḻai koṇṭu poṟukkavēṇṭum.

A hundred offences must be forgiven as if only six faults.





* * *





nūṟunāḷ ōti āṟunāḷ viṭat tīrum.

The learning acquired in a hundred days, will be lost by six days neglect.





* * *





nūṟōṭu nūṟu ākiṟatu neyyilē cuṭaṭi paṇikāram.

Though the debt may increase to another hundred, bake the cakes in ghee.





* * *





nūṟṟukku iruntālum kūṟṟukku arikīrai.

Though a hundred years old, he is only a young plant to the regent of the dead.





* * *





nūṟṟukku iruppār aimpatil cākār.

Those who will live to be a hundred, will not die at fifty.





* * *





nūṟṟukkumēl ūṟṟu, āyirattukkumēl āṟṟupperukku.

After a hundred a spring, after a thousand a flooded river.





* * *





nūṟṟukku oru pēccu āyirattukku oru talai acaippu.

When he is worth a hundred, a word, when a thousand, a mere nod.





* * *





nūṟṟaik keṭuttatu kuṟuṇi.

One measure of chaff spoiled a hundred of grain,





* * *





nūṉmuṟai terintu cīlattoḻuku.

Ascertaining the rules of the Veda, live virtuously.





* * *





ne.



neñcu aṟiyap poy collalāmō?

May one tell a lie knowingly?





* * *





neñcai oḷittu oru vañcakam illai.

There is no deceit which can be concealed from the mind.





* * *





neṭiyār kuṟiyārai āṟṟilē teriyum.

It will be known in the river, who is tall and who is short.





* * *





neṭum kaṭal ōṭiyum nilaiyē kalvi.

Though you may cross the broad sea your learning will remain with you.





* * *





neṭum pakalukkum astamaṉam uṇṭu.

Even a long day has a sunset.





* * *





neykiṉṟavaṉukku ēṉ kuraṅkuk kuṭṭi?

What has a weaver to do with a young monkey?





* * *





neykkuṭattil eṟumpu moyttatu pōla.

As the ants swarm on a ghee pot.





* * *





neykkuṭam uṭaintāl nāykku viruntu.

If the ghee pot is broken, the dog has a feast.





* * *





neykkuṭam uṭaintatu nāykku vēṭṭai.

The breaking of the ghee pot, is a hunting excursion to the dog.





* * *





neyyai urukkit tayiraip perukkic cāppiṭa vēṇṭum.

Use ghee after melting, and curds diluted.





* * *





neruñci muḷ taittālum kuṉintallavā piṭuṅka vēṇṭum?

Though it be but a thistle that has entered your foot, you must sit down, must you not, to pull it out?





* * *





neruppil ī moykkumā?

Will flies swarm in fire?





* * *





neruppiṉum pollātu karippiṉ vātai.

The distress of famine is worse than that of fire.





* * *





neruppilum pollāc ceruppu.

Shoes worse than fire.





* * *





neruppilē puḻup paṟṟumā?

Will worms breed in fire?





* * *





neruppu āṟum mayirp pālamum.

A river of fire, and a bridge of hair.





* * *





neruppu illāmaṟ pukai pukaiyumā?

Will there be smoke where there is no fire?





* * *





neruppu niṉṟa kāṭṭilē ētāvatu niṉṟālum niṟkum; nīr niṉṟa kāṭṭil oṉṟum niṟkātu.

Something may possibly remain in a forest after a fire, but nothing remains after a flood.





* * *





neruppu eṉṟāl vāy vēkumā?

By pronouncing the word fire, will the mouth be burnt?





* * *





neruppaik kaṇṭu mitittālum cuṭum, kāṇāmal mitittālum cuṭum.

Whether you tread on fire wittingly or unawares, it will burn you.





* * *





neruppaic cērnta yāvum ataṉ niṟamākum; atupōla, periyōraic cērntavarum āvār.

Things put into fire partake of its colour, in like manner will it happen to those who join the great.





* * *





neruppuc ciṟitu eṉṟu muṉṟāṉaiyil muṭiyalāmā?

May you tie fire in the skirt of your cloth because a mere spark?





* * *





neruppait talai kīḻāyp piṭittālum atiṉ cuvālai kīḻākumā?

Although you carry fire head downwards, will the flame burn in that direction?





* * *





nel eṭukkavum pul eṭukkavum āccō?

Am I destined to carry both paddy and grass?





* * *





nellum uppum picaintu uṇṇak kūṭumā?

Will a mixture of paddy and salt be agreeable to the palate?





* * *





nellu vakai eṇṇiṉālum paḷḷa vakai eṇṇak kūṭātu.

Though one may enumerate the various kinds of rice, he cannot enumerate the varieties of the palla caste.





* * *





nel viḷainta pūmiyum aṟiyāy, nilā eṟinta iṭamum aṟiyāy.

You do not know a rice crop, nor a moonlight space.





* * *





neṟi tappuvārkku aṟivippatu vīṇ.

It is useless to teach those who diviate from rectitude.





* * *





neṟpayir viḷai.

Cultivate rice.





* * *





nē.



nēttira maṇiyē cūttira aṇiyē.

The apple of the eye, is an ingenious piece of mechanism.





* * *





nēyamē niṟkum.

Love alone will abide.





* * *





nērā nōṉpu cīr ākātu.

Fasting without a vow is not good.





* * *





nērntu nērntu coṉṉālum nīcak kacaṭar vacam ākār.

Though repeatedly told, the base take no heed.





* * *





nērpaṭa oḻuku.

Behave evenly or agreeably.





* * *





nēṟṟu vanta moṭṭaicci ney vārttu uṇṇac ciṇuṅkukiṟāḷ.

The bald headed woman who came but yesterday, is whining for ghee to eat with her rice.





* * *





nēṟṟu vantāṉām kuṭi; avaṉ talaiyil viḻuntatām iṭi.

It is said that he took up his abode only yesterday, and he has been struck by lightning.





* * *





nai.



naiṣatam pulavarkku auṣatam.

Naishadha is a cordial to poets.





* * *





naipavar eṉiṉum noyya uraiyēl.

Speak not roughly even to a beggar.





* * *





naiyak kaṟṟiṉum noyya naṉku urai.

Though mollified by learning, speak effectively.





* * *





naiviṉai naṇukēl.

Go not near that which is pernicious.





* * *





no.



noṭippōtum vīṇiṭēl.

Lose not even a moment of time.





* * *





noṇṭi noṇṭi naṭappāṉēṉ kaṇṭataṟku ellām paṭaippāṉēṉ?

Why walk limping, and why offer oblations, to every god you see?





* * *





noṇṭik kutiraikkuc caṟukkiṉatu cākku.

To slip is the excuse of the lame horse.





* * *





nonta kaṇ irukka nōkāk kaṇṇukku maruntu.

Applying medicine to the sound eye, instead of to the one diseased.





* * *





nontu aṟiyātavar centamiḻ kaṟṟōr.

Those who are well versed in classical Tamil know not want.





* * *





noy yarici koti poṟukkumā?

Will bruised rice bear boiling?





* * *





noṟuṅkat tiṉṟārkku nūṟu vayatu.

Those who masticate their food, live a hundred years.





* * *





nō.



nōkātu uṇarvōr kalviyai nōṟpār.

Those who study unmindful of the pains attending it are devoted to learning.





* * *





nōkāmal aṭikkiṟēṉ ōyāmal aḻu.

I will beat you without giving pain, set up an unceasing cry.





* * *





nōkkattotuṅku.

Be careful to secure your object.





* * *





nōkka nōkkuva nōkkāmuṉ nōkkuvaṉ.

He whom we seek to see, has already seen us.





* * *





nōyaṟṟa vāḻvē vāḻvu kuṟaivaṟṟa celvamē celvam.

Freedom from sickness is true happiness, and competence is true riches.





* * *





nōyāḷikku ācai vārttai coṉṉāṟpōla.

Like uttering soothing words to a sick person.





* * *





nōyāḷi vitiyāḷi āṉāl parikāri pērāḷi āvāṉ.

If destiny favours the patient, his doctor will obtain fame.





* * *





nōyāḷikkut teriyum nōyiṉ varuttam.

The sick person knows the intensity of his suffering.





* * *





nōy koṇṭār pēykoṇṭār.

The sick are like those possessed of demons.





* * *





nōyñcaṟ pūṉai mattai nakkumāpōlē.

As a lean cat licks the churnstaff.





* * *





nōytta puli ākilum māṭṭukku valitu.

Though the chetah is sick, it is stronger than on ox.





* * *





nōlāmaiyiṉāl mēlāṉatupōm.

By neglecting religious austerities supreme good will be lost.





* * *





nōvu oru pakkam irukkac cūṭu oru pakkam pōṭṭāṟpōla.

Like branding the side that is not affected by disease.





* * *





nōṉpu eṉpatu koṉṟu tiṉṉāmai

Not to take life for the purpose of eating, is to fast.





* * *





nau.



nauvit toḻil nalam.

Youthful education will prove beneficial.





* * *





nauviyiṟṟāṉē teyvaṟivaik kal.

Know God when you are young.





* * *





nauviyum mutumaiyum naṭuvu maṟṟavaṉ.

He who is without youth, old age, or manhood.





* * *





nauviyum vāḻkkaiyum aḻakalla, naṟkuṇam oṉṟē aḻaku.

Youth and affluence do not form one’s beauty, goodness alone does.





* * *





pa.



pakaliṟ pakkam pārttup pēcu, arttarāttiriyil atutāṉum pēcātē.

By day, look round and speak, at midnight do not utter a word.





* * *





pakaliṟ pacu teriyavillai iravil erumai teriyumā?

Not being able to distinguish a cow by day, how can he distinguish a buffalo by night?





* * *





pakiṭikkup pattuk kācu tiruppāṭṭukku oru kācu.

Ten cash for a comedy, one cash for a sacred song.





* * *





pakiṭiyaip pāmpu kaṭittatupōla.

As the snake bit the jester.





* * *





pakuttal illāta tuṇivu pāram illāta kappal.

Daring without prudence, is as a ship without ballast.





* * *





pakuttu aṟiyāmal tuṇiyātē paṭapaṭappākac ceyyātē.

Attempt nothing without consideration, and do nothing hastily.





* * *





pakaikkac ceyyēl maṟu jeṉaṉappaṭu.

Do not create enemies, act discreetly.





* * *





pakaiyāḷi kuṭiyai uṟavāṭik keṭukka vēṇṭum.

You must ruin the family of your enemy by feigned friendship.





* * *





pakaivar uṟavu pukai eḻu neruppu.

The friendship of foes is like fire concealed in smoke.





* * *





paṅkāḷi kuṭi keṭukka veṅkāyam kuḷipōṭac coṉṉatu pōla.

Like advising his partner to plant onions in order to effect his ruin.





* * *





paṅkāḷi vīṭu vēkiṟatu cuṅkāṉkoṇṭu taṇṇīr viṭu.

The house of my partner is on fire, pour water with a pipe.





* * *





paṅkāḷiyum paṉaṅkāyum patampārttu veṭṭavēṇṭum.

Observe the proper time for cutting a partner and palmyrah fruit.





* * *





paṅkuṉi eṉṟu paruppatum illai, cittirai eṉṟu ciṟuppatum illai.

It wont grow larger because it is Panguni,-March-nor smaller because it is Chittarai-April.





* * *





paṅkuṉimātam pakalvaḻi naṭantavaṉ perumpāvi.

He that goes abroad in the day time in March, is a great sinner.





* * *





paci illātavaṉukkuk karippu mayir māttiram.

He who is not hungry cares not a hair about famine.





* * *





paci ēppakkāraṉum puḷi ēppakkāraṉum kūṭṭuppayir iṭṭatupōla.

As two men, one belching through hunger, and the other by indigestion, were associated in joint tillage.





* * *





pacu ēṟuvālum erutu kūḻaivālum.

A cow with a long tail, an ox with a short one.





* * *





pacikkuk kaṟi vēṇṭām, tūkkattukkup pāy vēṇṭām.

When hungry curry is not needed, nor a mat when sleepy.





* * *





pacikkup paṉam paḻam tiṉṟāl pittam paṭṭa pāṭu paṭaṭṭum.

Eat palmyrah fruit to satisfy hunger, no matter about the bile.

Palmyrah fruit is believed to be very bilious.





* * *





paci tīrntatāṉāl pāṭṭu iṉpamām.

Songs are pleasant after refection.





* * *





pacitta paṟaiyaṉum kuḷitta caivaṉum cāppiṭātu irār.

A hungry pariah and a saiva that has bathed, must have eaten first.





* * *





pacittavaṉ tiṉṉātatum illai. pakaittavaṉ collātatum illai.

There is nothing edible which the hungry will not eat, there is no reproach which the malevolent has not uttered.





* * *





pacittavaṉ mēl nampikkai vaikkalāmā?

Can confidence be placed in a hungry person?





* * *





pacitta ceṭṭi pākkut tiṉṟāṉām.

It is said that a hungry merchant chewed betel and arica-nut.





* * *





pacittavaṉ payaṟṟai vitaikkaṭṭum.

Let the hungry sow peas.





* * *





pacittavaṉ paḻam kaṇakkaip pārppatupōl.

As a hungry man looks at his old accounts.





* * *





pacittār poḻutum pōm pāluṭaṉ aṉṉam pucittār poḻutum pōm.

The sun goes down on the hungry, and also on those who eat their rice and milk.





* * *





paciyāmal maruntu koṭukkiṟēṉ paḻaiyatu iruntāl pōṭu eṉpatupōla.

Like saying, put before me some old rice if there be any, and I will give you medicine that will prevent your hungering again.





* * *





paci ruci aṟiyumā nittirai cukam aṟiyumā?

Does hunger appreciate flavours, or sleep enjoyment?





* * *





paci ruci aṟiyātu, nittirai cukam aṟiyātu.

Hunger is ignorant of flavours, sleep is ignorant of enjoyment.





* * *





paci vantiṭil pattum paṟantupōm.

Hunger dissipates the ten.

The ten are the five senses and their organs.





* * *





pacukaṟuppeṉṟu pālum kaṟuppā?

Because the cow is black, is her milk also black?





* * *





pacuc cātum pārppāṉ ēḻaiyum uṇṭā?

Are there any gentle cows, or poor brahmans?





* * *





pacut tōl pōrttup pulip pāyccal pāykiṟatu.

To wear a cow skin, and spring like a tiger.





* * *





pacuppōlē iruntu pulippōlē pāykiṟāy.

Gentle as a cow, you spring as a tiger.





* * *





pacu marattil taitta āṇipōla.

Like a nail driven into a green tree.





* * *





pacu viḻuntatu pulikkut tāyam.

The falling of the cow, is a gain to the tiger.





* * *





pacuvukku irai koṭuttāl maturamāṉa pāl koṭukkum. atupōla, nallavarkaḷukkuc ceykiṟa upakāram palaṉ aḷikkum.

When a cow is fed she yields sweet milk, so the favours shewn to the good will bring a reward.





* * *





pacuvaik koṉṟāl kaṉṟu piḻaikkumā?

If you slaughter a cow, will its calf live?





* * *





pacuvaik koṉṟu cerupput tāṉam ceytatu pōla.

Like killing the cow and giving shoes made of its hide.





* * *





pacuvai aṭittup pulikkut tāṉam koṭukkiṟatā?

Do you kill a cow, and make an offering of it to a tiger?





* * *





pacuvai viṟṟāṟ kaṉṟukku vaḻakkā?

Having sold the cow, is there a dispute about the calf?





* * *





paccaṭi kaṇṭāl oṭṭaṭimakaḷē.

Daughter, if you find him rich, cling to him.





* * *





paccilaiyum kiḷḷappaṭumō parāparamē?

O God, is a tender plant also to be cut off!





* * *





paccai koṭuttāl pāvam tīrum veḷḷai koṭuttāl viṉai tīrum.

If raw provisions are given, the sins of the present birth will be removed, if white cloths are given, the sins of former births will be cancelled.





* * *





paccaic cirippup pallukkuk kēṭu tūvu parukkai layiṟṟukkuk kēṭu.

Feigned laughter is bad for the teeth, and scattered grains of boiled rice are bad for the stomach.





* * *





paccai cellukkup paṟaiyaṉiṭattiṟ cēvikkalām.

You may serve a pariah for paddy.





* * *





paccai maṇṇum cuṭṭa maṇṇum oṭṭumā?

Will moist and burnt clay unite?





* * *





paccai maram paṭap pārppāṉ.

He can stare so as to make a green tree decay.





* * *





paccai marattiṟku ittaṉai eṉṟāṟ paṭṭamarattiṟku ettaṉai?

If so much to the green tree, how much to the dry?





* * *





pañcam illāk kālattil paci paṟakkum.

In the absence of famine hunger flies away.





* * *





pañcam pōm pañcattiṟ paṭṭa vacai pōkātu.

The famine will end, but a scar then made will not pass away.





* * *





pañcāṉum kuñcum paṟakkat tavikkiṟatu.

The little ones starve excessively.





* * *





pañcāṅkam pōṉālum naṭcattiram pōkātu.

Though astrological calculations prove false, the stars will not.





* * *





pañcu paṭinta paḻam cittiram.

An old picture covered with dust.





* * *





pañcup potiyil neruppup paṭṭatupōla.

Like fire touching a bale of cotton.





* * *





pañcuppotiyiṟ paṭṭa ampupōla.

Like an arrow striking a bale of cotton.





* * *





pañcum neruppum oṉṟāyk kiṭakkumō?

Can cotton and fire be placed together?





* * *





pañcum neruppumpōlē.

Like cotton and fire.





* * *





pañcai paṇikāram cuṭṭatum, vīṅkal vicārappaṭṭatum.

The poor baked the cakes, the greedy longed for them.





* * *





paṭi āḷvār nīti tappiṟ kuṭi ār iruppavar kuvalayattil ār?

If rulers err, who can abide on the earth?





* * *





paṭikku ācāṉ iruntāl kuṭikkuc cētam illai.

If the giver of the daily allowance be alive, no loss to the family.





* * *





paṭikkiṟatu tiruvāymoḻi, iṭikkiṟatu perumāḷ kōvil.

To read Tiruváymoli, and to break down the temple of Vishnu.





* * *





paṭikkiṟa piḷḷai pākkup pōṭṭāl nākku taṭippāy pōm.

If a school boy chew betel, his pronunciation will be spoilt.





* * *





paṭittu muṭṭāḷāy irukkiṟāṉ.

He is a learned fool.





* * *





paṭittavaṉukkum paṭiyātavaṉukkum, kokkukkum aṉṉattukkum vittiyācampōla.

The difference between the learned and the unlearned, is as great as that of a crane and a swan.





* * *





paṭukaḷappaṭṭa paṉṉāṭai.

A palm network driven in a battle-field.





* * *





paṭukaḷattil oppāriyā?

Do they sing elegies in a battle-field?





* * *





paṭukkaic cukam mettai aṟiyātu.

The comfort of the bed is not experienced by the mattress.





* * *





paṭuvatu paṭṭum paṭṭattukku irukkavēṇṭum.

Whatever hardships he may have to endure, he must have the rank to which he is entitled.





* * *





paṭai keṭṭu ōṭukaiyil narai mayir piṭuṅkukiṟatā? .

When an army is routed, are grey hairs pulled out?





* * *





paṭaikku oruvaṉ koṭaikku oruvaṉ.

One to fight, and one to give.





* * *





paṭai mikuntāl araṇ illai.

If an army be large, a fortress is not required.





* * *





paṭai mukattilum aṟimukam vēṇṭum.

An acquaintance is necessary even in a battle-field.





* * *





paṭaiyātu paṭaitta marumakaḷē uṉṉaip paṟaiyaṉ aṟukkak kaṉāk kaṇṭēṉ.

O, my daughter-in-law, who art entertaining me sumptuously, I dreamt that thou wast being dissected by a pariah.





* * *





paṭci pacittālum eṭṭikkaṉiyait tiṉṉātu.

Though a bird is hungry, it will not eat poisonous berries.





* * *





paṭṭa kālilē paṭum, keṭṭa kuṭiyēk keṭum.

It will hit the foot already injured, the decayed family will be ruined.





* * *





paṭṭaṇattāḷ peṟṟa kuṭṭi paṇam paṟikka valla kuṭṭi.

A young one born of a city mother is clever at thieving.





* * *





paṭṭaṇattu vācalaip paṭalālē mūṭukiṟatō?

Can you cover up a city gate with a hurdle?





* * *





paṭṭaṇattu vācalaip paṭṭālē mūṭi irukkiṟatā?

Is the gate of the city veiled with silk?





* * *





paṭṭaṭai vāyttāl paṇi vāykkum.

If the anvil is good, the work will be good.





* * *





paṭṭavarkku uṇṭu palaṉ.

Those who have taken pains will enjoy the gains.





* * *





paṭṭavaḷukku patavi uṇṭu.

She who suffered will attain advantage.





* * *





paṭṭāl aṟivāṉ caṇṭāḷaṉ, maḻai peytāl aṟivāṉ veḷḷāḷaṉ.

A rogue is wise when punished, the farmer when it rains.





* * *





paṭṭiṉattu nariyaip paṉaṅkāṭṭu nari ēyttatām.

It is said that the jackal of the palmyrah grove cheated the city fox.





* * *





paṭṭik kāṭṭukkuc civapput tuppaṭṭip pītāmparam.

In a common village, a red garment is regarded as a sumptuous garment.





* * *





paṭṭu aṟi keṭṭu aṟi pattu eṭṭu iṟuttu aṟi.

Be wise by suffering, by poverty, and by paying ten and eight fines.





* * *





paṭṭu kulaintāl poṭṭu.

When silk thread is untwisted, it becomes useless.





* * *





paṭṭuk kattarittatukpōlap pēcavēṇṭum.

You must speak carefully as silk is cut by the scissors.





* * *





paṭṭuk kōṭṭaikku vaḻi eṅkē eṉṟāl koṭṭaippākku vilai nūṟṟaimpatu eṉkiṟāṉ.

When he was asked which was the way to Pattucotta, he said the price of arica-nuts is a hundred and fifty fanams.





* * *





paṭṭukku aḻuvār paṇikku aḻuvār vaiyakattil pākkukku aḻuta pāratattaik kaṇṭatu illai.

There are who cry for a silk cloth, and there are who cry for ornaments; I never saw any one in the world cry for betel nut.





* * *





paṭṭup puṭaivai iraval koṭuttu maṇai eṭuttukkoṇṭu tirivatupōla.

Like lending a silk cloth to another, and wandering about carrying a seat with her.





* * *





paṭṭum paṭṭāvaḷiyum peṭṭiyil irukkum kāṟ kācuk kantai ōṭi ulāvum.

The silk and the velvet are kept in a box, while a rag not worth a cash walks about the street.





* * *





paṭṭaikkut takka paḻam kayiṟu.

An old rope suited to the well-bucket.





* * *





paṇakkaḷḷi pāyil paṭāḷ.

A niggardly woman will not lie on a mat.





* * *





paṇakkāraṉ piṉṉum pattup pēr, payittiyakkāraṉ piṉṉum pattup pēr.

Ten follow after a moneyed man, and ten after a fool.





* * *





paṇattukkup peyar āṭkolli.

Money is called a man-slayer.





* * *





paṇattukku oru ampu koṇṭu pāḻil eykiṟatupōla.

Like buying arrows at a fanam each, and wasting them.





* * *





paṇattaik koṭuttup paṇikārattai vāṅkip paṟṟaikkuḷḷē iruntu tiṉkavēṇṭumō?

Is it necessary for you to hide yourself in a bush, and eat the cakes for which you have paid?





* * *





paṇam iruntāl pākṣā, paṇam illāviṭṭāl pakkiri.

If I have money, Páchcha; if not money, Pakkiri.





* * *





paṇam illātavaṉ piṇam.

He who has no money is a corpse.





* * *





paṇam eṉṟāl piṇamum vāy tiṟakkum.

If the word money be uttered, even a corpse will open its mouth.





* * *





paṇam eṉṉa ceyyum pattu vitam ceyyum.

What can money effect? it can do ten kinds of things.





* * *





paṇam eṉṉa pāṣāṇam, kuṇam oṉṟē pōtum.

Why money? it is poison; a good disposition is enough.





* * *





paṇam kulam ākum, paci kaṟi ākum.

Money, is rank; hunger, is curry.





* * *





paṇam pantiyilē kulam kuppaiyilē.

Money at the festive board, and rank on the dunghill.





* * *





paṇikāramō ciluciluppō?

Is the sound that of baking cakes, or is it mere frizzle?





* * *





paṇṭāram piṇṭattukku aḻukiṟāṉ, liṅkam pāl cōttukku aḻukiṟatu.

The religious mendicant is crying for a mouthfull of food, the linga, he worships, for rice and milk.





* * *





paṇṭai paṭṭa pāṭṭaip paḻaṅkiṭukil pōṭṭuviṭṭuc campā nelkuttip poṅkal iṭukiṟāḷ.

Having put aside her former troubles on an old cadjan, she husked chamba rice and boiled it.





* * *





paṇṇap paṇṇap pala vitam ākum.

The more he makes, the more varied the forms.





* * *





paṇṇiya payiril puṇṇiyam teriyum.

A man’s merit may be seen in the grain he grows.





* * *





pataṟāta kāriyam citaṟātu.

A thing done without haste never fails.





* * *





pataṟic ceykiṟa kāriyam citaṟik keṭṭuppōkum.

A hasty proceeding will go to ruin.





* * *





patiṉāṟum peṟṟup peruvāḻvu vāḻavēṇṭum.

May you bear sixteen children, and live very prosperously.





* * *





patti koḷpavaṉ mutti uḷḷavaṉ.

The devout attain heaven.





* * *





pattiyattukku muruṅkaikkāy koṇṭuvarac coṉṉāl, pāl teḷikka akattikkīrai koṇṭuvaruvāṉ.

When requested to bring murunga fruit for diet, he brings coronilla grandiflora leaves for the milk-sprinkling ceremony.

See note under proverb 4684.





* * *





patti illāp pūṉai paramaṇṭalattukku ēṟumā?

Will an ungodly cat ascend to heaven?





* * *





patti illāta putti acētaṉam.

Reason without devotion is folly.





* * *





patti uṇṭāṉāl mutti uṇṭām.

If there be piety, there will be final happiness.





* * *





pattiyōṭē pākaṟkāy caṭṭiyōṭē tīyutu.

Pagal fruits in clusters are being charred in the chatty.





* * *





pattilē viḻunta pāmpum cāvātu.

Even a snake that has fallen among ten, will not die.





* * *





pattilē pacalai irupatilē iṭumpu.

At ten childishness, at twenty arrogance.





* * *





pattiṉiyait toṭṭatum turiyōtaṉaṉ paṭṭatum.

By touching the virtuous woman, Dropati, Duriyodhana was ruined.





* * *





pattu iṟuttapiṉ pārac cantēkam tīrntatu.

After I paid a fine of ten, doubt was removed.





* * *





pattu ariciyum vēkavillai, pāvi pirāṇaṇum pōkavillai.

Ten grains of rice wont boil, the life of the wretch wont go.





* * *





pattukku mēlē oru paṟaiyaṉukkum taḷḷavēṇṭum.

After ten years of age, a girl should be affianced if even to a pariah.





* * *





pattup paṇam koṭuttālum ittaṉai pataippu ākātu.

Though you give ten fanams, such haste is bad.





* * *





pattup pērilē patiṉorām pērāy irukkavēṇṭum.

Of the ten, you must pass as the eleventh.





* * *





pattu varākaṉ iṟuttōm, eṉṟālum cantēkam nivartti āyiṟṟē.

We were fined ten pagodas, however our doubts were removed.





* * *





pattu vitattilum paṟaiyaṉai nampalām pārppāṉai nampakkūṭātu.

In ten ways a Pariah is trustworthy, but a brahman is not.





* * *





pattu ēr vaittup paṭaimaramum tōṟṟēṉ ettaṉai ēr vaittuk kōvaṇamum tōṟṟāy?

Possessed of ten yoke of oxen I lost the plough share; how many yoke did you possess before you lost your waist cloth?





* * *





pantam coṉṉār paṭaikkākār.

Those who regard relationships are unfit for military service.





* * *





pantikku muntavēṇṭum paṭaikkup pintavēṇṭum.

Be first at a feast, and last at the fight.





* * *





pantiyilē vēṇṭām eṉṟāl ilai pottal eṉkiṟāy.

When rejected at the feast, thou sayest that the leaf is torn.





* * *





payaṇakkāraṉ payittiyakkāraṉ.

A man about to set out on a journey, is a fool.





* * *





payittiyamō paṇṭāramō veṉṟāl ippōtutāṉ toṭarukiṟatu eṉṟāṉ.

When I ask him whether he is mad or a mendicant, he says, it is just following.





* * *





payittiyam māṟippōccu ulakkaittaṭi koṇṭuvā kōvaṇam kaṭṭa.

My madness is gone, bring me a pestle to tie round my waist.





* * *





paraṇi aṭuppup pāḻpōkātu.

A fireplace made on the second lunar day will not be deserted.





* * *





paramparai āṇṭiyō pañcattukku āṇṭiyō?

Is he a hereditary mendicant, or a mendicant, because of the famine?





* * *





parikāri uṟavu teruvācal maṭṭum.

The friendship of the doctor ends at the threshold.





* * *





pariyattukku añcik kuruṭṭuk kaṇṇiyai koṇṭatu pōla.

Like marrying a blind woman to avoid paying the nuptial present.





* * *





parivu illāp pōcaṉattiṟ paṭṭiṉi naṉṟu, piriyam illāp peṇṭiṟ pēy naṉṟi.

Hunger is preferrable to eating food given without good feeling, a demoness is better than an unkind wife.





* * *





paruttik kaṭaiyilē nāykku aluval eṉṉa?

What can a dog have to do in a cotton bazaar?





* * *





paruttikkāṭu uḻukiṟataṟku muṉṉē pommaṉukku ēḻu muḻam timmaṉukku ēḻu muḻam.

Ere the cotton fields are ploughed, Pomman asks for a cloth of seven cubits, and Timman wants one of the same length.





* * *





parutti puṭaivaiyāyk kāyttatuk.

The cotton tree produced cloth.





* * *





paruttip potikku oru neruppup poṟipōla.

As a single spark to a bale of cotton.





* * *





paruppilē ney viṭṭatupōla.

As ghee was poured on beans.





* * *





parumarattai aṇṭiya palliyum cākātu.

If sheltered near a large tree even a lizard will not die.





* * *





pala umi tiṉṟāl oru aviḻ taṭṭum.

If one consume plenty of husks, a grain may turn up.





* * *





pala carakkukāraṉaip payittiyam piṭittatupōla.

As the vendor of curry stuff was seized with madness.





* * *





pala tīṭṭukku oru muḻukku.

One ablution for many defilements.





* * *





pala tuḷi peru veḷḷam.

Many drops make great flood.





* * *





paruvattē payir cey.

Cultivate in due time.





* * *





parōpakāramē peritu.

Benevolence is indeed great.





* * *





palattavaṉukku maruntu coṉṉāṟ piṭuṅkik koṭuttut tīra vēṇṭum.

If you prescribe medicine for the strong, you must procure the medicinal plants yourself.





* * *





palattavaṉ kaikku iḷaittavaṉ turumpu.

The feeble is a straw in the hands of the powerful.





* * *





palanāḷait tiruṭaṉ oru nāḷaikku akappaṭuvāṉ.

An old thief will one day be caught.





* * *





pala pāvam tīra oru puṇṇiyam ākilum paṇṇa vēṇṭum.

One virtuous deed at least, is required to expiate numerous sins.





* * *





pala maram kaṇṭa taccaṉ orumaram veṭṭa māṭṭāṉ.

The carpenter who is acquainted with various sorts of wood, is not able to fell a tree.





* * *





pala muyaṟci ceyyiṉum pakavaṉ mēl cintai vai.

After making every effort, fix your mind on God.





* * *





pala vīṭṭup piccai āṟāyp perukum.

The alms of many houses may flow as a river.





* * *





palaṉ illāp pala nāḷilum, aṟam cey oru nāḷ peritu.

One day spent in alms giving is greater than many spent in doing nothing.





* * *





palā uttamam, mā mattipam, pātiri atamam.

The jack tree is the best, the mango is good, the pathri tree is indifferent.





* * *





pal āṭap paci āṟum.

As the teeth move hunger is appeased.





* * *





pallakku ēṟa yōkam uṇṭu uṉṉi ēṟac cīvaṉ illai.

He has a palanquin, but is not able to mount it.





* * *





pallakkukku mēlmūṭi illātavaṉukkum, kālukkuc ceruppu illātavaṉukkum vicāram oṉṟē.

He whose palanquin has no top, and he who has no shoes for his feet are equal.





* * *





pallu viḻunta puṭaiyaṉ.

A snake without fangs.





* * *





pallu viḻunta puṭaiyaṉukku kirutā?

Is the rage of a fangless snake dangerous?





* * *





pavucu keṭṭa pākkaṭṭikku iraṇṭu pakkamum tīvaṭṭiyām.

A torch on either side of a lump of worthless sugar.





* * *





paḻaka paḻakap pālum puḷikkum.

By constant use even milk will taste sour.





* * *





paḻaṅkāl tūrkkavēṇṭām putukkāl veṭṭa vēṇṭām.

Do not fill up an old channel; do not cut a new one.





* * *





paḻattilē paḻam miḷakāyppaḻam.

The chief of berries is the chilli.





* * *





paḻattēṅkāyilē tāṉ eṇṇey.

Old cocoanuts yield oil.





* * *





paḻam naḻuvip pālil viḻuntatupōla.

Like fruit slipping and falling into milk.





* * *





paḻam puṇṇāḷi pātivaittiyaṉ.

He who has an old sore is half a doctor.





* * *





paḻikku āṉōr cilar paḻi paṭuvōr cilar.

One hasty commits the crime, and another suffers the penalty.





* * *





paḻippāṉa kalvi pāvattiṉ ūṟṟu.

Despicable learning is a fountain of sin.





* * *





paḻippaṉa pakarēl

Utter no reproach.





* * *





paḻutu ceytatai aṟikkai iṭal pāti nivirtti.

The confession of a fault removes half its guilt.





* * *





paḻutta ōlaiyaip pārttuk kuruttu ōlai cirikkiṟatām.

It is said that the young palm leaf is laughing at the dry leaf because it is falling off.





* * *





paḻutta paḻam kompil irātu.

Ripe fruit does not remain on the branch.





* * *





paḻaiya kaṟuppaṉ kaṟuppaṉē paḻaiya maṇkiṇṇi maṇkiṇṇiyē.

My name old Karuppan is still my name, and my earthen basin is still my eating dish.





* * *





paḻaiya kuruṭi katavai tiṟavaṭi.

O thou blind old woman, open the door.





* * *





paḻaiyatu mīnta iṭam kāṇiyāṭci.

The place where one gets plenty of cold rice is the right one.





* * *





paḷḷam iṟaittavaṉ paṅku koṇṭupōkiṟāṉ.

He who irrigated the low ground takes his share of the produce.





* * *





paḷḷam uḷḷa iṭattilē taṇṇīr niṟkum.

Water will stand in a hollow.





* * *





paḷḷik kaṇakkup puḷḷikku utavātu.

Accounts learnt at school will not be practically useful.





* * *





paḷḷik kuppattukku ampaṭṭa vāttiyār.

A barber school master for a village inhabited by Pallis.





* * *





paḷḷi pākkut tiṉṟāl pattu viralum cuṇṇāmpu.

If a Palli chews betel, his ten fingers will be smeared with chunam.





* * *





paḷḷip piḷḷai eṉṟāl celvam kuṟaiyumā ?

If you admit that you are a school boy, will your wealth decrease?





* * *





paḷḷikku vaikkāmaṟ koḷḷikkuk kuṟaittu vaittār.

My father not only neglected to put me to school, but left me to bear the expenses of the fire brand.

Under this proverb a general note on funeral rites may not be out of place. The particulars given relate to Hindu families generally, not to brahmans.

When a person dies, the grief of the females in the family bursts out into loud lamentations. On its being made known by a conch-blower that a death has occurred, the neighbouring females go to the house, add the expression of their sympathy, and all unite in one general wail. Frequently one of the company, the mother, or it may be a professional person, breaks out into an ascription of praise regarding the deceased uttering a sort of elegy in measured cadence. In this case when a climax is reached, all join in a chorus of grief, and some may smite their breasts and tear their hair. In such elegies, which are often of a touching kind, the personal beauty, the talents, the learning and the prowess of the deceased are dwelt on.

The body being taken out is bathed and dressed as in life. In the case of females the gold and pearls and precious stones, if she possessed them, will be put on. The sectarial marks will be added. The chárpoy on which the corpse has been laid out is then carried into the house. Again the females seat themselves around it and pour forth a torrent of grief, or at any rate make a loud noise. A kinsman whose business it is to attend to the customary observances spreads a cloth on the chárpoy to hold rice, called,—rice for the mouth. The females of the family take a little of the grain and put it on the corpse near the mouth. The same ceremony is performed by the male members of the family at the place of cremation. The family barber appropriates the remaining rice. At the burning ground the kinsman who conducts the ceremonies carries a pot of water round the pyre and the conch-blower follows him and makes an incision in the pot with his conch, when the barber puts four bits of sandal or other wood into the hands of the kinsman and points out the places in the pyre where they are to be inserted. The leaking pot is then taken by the barber to be broken near the head of the corpse. The kinsman hastens away that he may not hear the noise of the pot when broken, nor see the burning pyre. Then the barber receives permission to break the pot and to ignite the pyre.

On the next or on an early day the male friends of the deceased assemble at the place of cremation. The skull, the breast-bone and the bones of the hands having been abstracted from the ashes and deposited in a convenient place covered with a plantain leaf, are by the nearest of kin smeared with oil, ghee and honey and covered with flowers, whilst a priest recites appropriate incantations in the presence of the relatives. A pot of milk having been provided for this ceremony, the relatives take a sprig of coronilla grandiflora and having dipped it in the milk, sprinkle the relics, which are then put into an unburnt earthen vessel, covered with cloth to be cast into a sacred stream or bestowed on the ocean wave. Of course these elaborate rites are dispensed with by the poor.

I have seen Hindus, not by any means in abject circumstances, convey a corpse to the seaside, where a grave had been hastily dug, take off the cloth and ornaments, wrap the body in a common mat, and bury it with the burial of a dog.

In the year 1830 when travelling in Bengal I saw, on the bank of the Damooda, a sorrowing brahmanical father with two or three attendants dispose of a corpse with but little ceremony. The father himself who was much affected ignited the pyre.





* * *





paḷḷi oḷittirāṉ pārppāṉ kuḷittirāṉ.

A palli does not like to be secluded, nor does a brahman fast after bathing.





* * *





paṟakkiṟa paṟavaikku etu tūram?

What is distance to a bird on the wing?





* * *





paṟakkum kākam irukkum kompu aṟiyātatupōla.

As a crow on the wing knows not on what branch it will alight.





* * *





paṟakkum kuruvikkum irukkum kompu teriyātu, paratēcikkut taṅkum iṭam teriyātu.

A bird on the wing knows not the branch on which it may alight, a stranger knows not his halting place.





* * *





paṟantupōkiṟa ecciṟ kallaimēl kallait tūkki vaittāṟpōla.

Like one placing a stone on a flying leaf that has been used for a rice-plate.





* * *





paṟappāṉ payir iḻantāṉ aṟakkāñci peṇṭu iḻantāṉ.

A hasty man loses the produce of his field, and the jealous man his wife.





* * *





paṟitta kāṭṭukkup payam illai.

No further fear in a jungle, where one has been already robbed.





* * *





paṟaicci piḷḷaiyaip paḷḷikku vaittalum pēccilē ayyē eṉṉumām.

Though a pariah child be sent to school, he will still call his father ayyè.





* * *





paṟai taṭṭiṉāṟpōla.

Like striking grain to the level of the measure.





* * *





paṟait teruvilē vilvam mulaittatupōla

As a vilva tree sprang up in a pariah street.





* * *





paṟaippāṭṭukkum paṟaippēccukkum curaippūviṟkum maṇam illai.

A pariah’s song, his dialect, and a surai flower, have no fragrance in them.





* * *





paṟaiyaṉum pārppāṉum pōla.

Like a pariah and a brahman.





* * *





paṟaiyaṉ poṅkal iṭṭāl pakavāṉukku ēṟātō?

If a pariah boil rice as an offering to God, will it not be accepted?





* * *





paṟaiyai paḷḷikku vaittālum tuṟaippēccup pōmā?

Though a pariah is schooled, will his vulgar brogue be altered?





* * *





paṟaivēlai araivēlai.

The work of a pariah is only half done.





* * *





paṉaṅkāṭṭu nari calacalappukku añcumā?

Will the jackal of the palmyrah grove tremble at the rustling leaves?





* * *





paṉikkaṇ tiṟantāl maḻaikkaṇ tiṟakkum.

If there be dew, there will be no rain.





* * *





paṉi peytu kaṭal niṟaiyumā?

Will the sea be filled by the falling dew?





* * *





paṉi peytu kuḷam nirampumā?

Will the falling dew fill the tank?





* * *





paṉi peytāl maḻai illai, paḻam iruntāl pū illai.

When there is dew there is no rain, when fruit is ripe there is no blossom.





* * *





paṉip perukkilē kappal ōṭṭalāmā?

Can you sail a ship in heavy dew?





* * *





paṉiyai nampi ēr pūṭṭiṉatupōla.

Like beginning to plough depending on the dew.





* * *





paṉai ēṟiyum pāḷaitoṭātu iṟaṅkiṉāṟpōla.

Like descending from a palmyrah tree without touching the flower-spathe.





* * *





paṉai ēṟi viḻuntavaṉaik kaṭā ēṟi mitittatupōla.

Like a buffalo trampling on a person who has fallen from a palmyrah tree.





* * *





paṉai marattiṉ kīḻē pālaik kuṭittālum kaḷ eṉṟu niṉaippār.

Though what you drink in a palmyrah grove be milk, every one will believe it to be toddy.





* * *





paṉaimarattiṟku niḻal illai paṟaiyaṉukku muṟai illai

A palmyrah tree casts no shade, a pariah has no rules.





* * *





paṉaimaram ēṟukiṟavaṉai etuvaraikkum tāṅkalām?

How far can you support a man who is climbing a palmyrah tree?





* * *





paṉaiyiṉ niḻalum niḻalō pakaivar uṟavum uṟavō?

Is the shadow of a palmyrah tree a shade, is the acquaintance of an enemy friendship?





* * *





paṉiyil iruntu viḻuntavaṉaip pāmpu kaṭittatupōla

Like a snake biting one who has fallen from a palmyrah tree.





* * *





paṉai veṭṭiṉa iṭattilē kaḻutai vaṭṭam pōṭṭatupōla.

Like an ass going round a place where a palmyrah tree had been felled.





* * *





paṉṟikkuṭṭikku orucanti ētu?

Does a young pig observe fasts?





* * *





paṉṟikkuṭṭi āṉai āmā?

Will the young pig become an elephant?





* * *





paṉṟipaṭṭāl avaṉōṭē, kāṭṭāṉai paṭṭāl paṅku.

If a hog be shot he takes the whole, if an elephant be shot I shall have a share.





* * *





paṉṟi pala kuṭṭi, ciṅkam oru kuṭṭi.

The sow has many young ones at a time, the lioness only one.





* * *





paṉṟi pala kuṭṭi pōṭṭu eṉṉa?

What, if a sow has a numerous litter?





* * *





paṉṟiyōṭu kūṭiya kaṉṟum malam tiṉṉum.

A calf that goes with a pig will eat excrement.





* * *





paṉṉakkāraṉ peṇṭil paṇiyak kiṭantu cettāḷām.

It is said that the wife of a mat-maker died on the bare ground.





* * *





paṉṉip paṉṉip paḻaṅkatai paṭiyātē.

Do not tell old stories with affectation.





* * *





pā.



pākaṟ koṭṭai putaikkac curaikkoṭṭai muḷaikkumā?

Will a bottle gourd spring from a págal seed?





* * *





pācam aṟṟavaṉ paratēci.

He who has no ties is like a foreigner.





* * *





pāṭakakkāli vāḻntāl pattu eṭṭuc caṉam piḻaikkum.

If the foot-ringed lady prosper, eight or ten people may be supported thereby.





* * *





pāṭam ēṟiṉum ēṭu kaiviṭēl.

Though you have learnt your lesson, do not throw away your book.





* * *





pāṭip pāṭik kuttiṉālum pataril arici illai.

Though pounded with a song, chaff will not yield rice.





* * *





pāṭu eṉṟāl pāṇaṉum pāṭāṉ.

When pressed to sing, even the professional singer refuses.





* * *





pāṭu paṭāmaṟ pōṉāl palaṉ illāmaṟ pōkum.

Where there is no labour, there is no profit.





* * *





pāṭum pulavar kaiyiṟ paṭṭōlai āṉēṉē .

I am become an ola in the band of a poet.





* * *





pāṭai ēṟiṉum ōṭu kaiviṭēl.

Though you may ascend the bier, forsake not your alms-dish.





* * *





pāṇam toṭuttāṟ pōlap pēcukiṟāṉ.

He speaks as if arrows were darting forth.





* * *





pātakar paḻakkam pāmpoṭu paḻakal pōla.

Friendship with the wicked, is like playing with snakes.





* * *





pātic curaikkāy kaṟikkum pātic curaikkāy vitaikkumā?

Can you take half a churaikáy for curry, and use the other half as seed?





* * *





pāttiram aṟintu piccai iṭu, kōttiram aṟintu peṇ koṭu.

Give alms to the worthy, and your daughter to one of a good family.





* * *





pāppukku mūppu illai.

Brahmans have no chiefs.





* * *





pāmaṇi āṟṟilē pallai viḷakku, mullai āṟṟilē mukam kaḻuvu.

Begin to clean your teeth at Pámani river, wash your face in Mullai river.





* * *





pāmpāṭṭi pāmpilē, kaḷḷaṉ kaḷavilē

A snake charmer understands snakes, a thief understands thieving.





* * *





pāmpilum pāmpukkuṭṭi viṣam atikam vīriyamum atikam.

A young snake is more poisonous and vigorous than an old one.





* * *





pāmpiṉ kuṭṭi pāmpu ataṉ kuṭṭi naṭṭuvākkāli.

The young one of a snake is a snake, and its young one is a scorpion.





* * *





pāmpiṉ vāyt tēraipōla.

Like a toad in the jaws of a snake.





* * *





pāmpukku mūppu illai.

Snakes have no chiefs.





* * *





pāmpu kaṭikkat tēḷukkup pārkkiṟatō?

When bitten by a snake, will the incantations suited to scorpion bites avail aught?





* * *





pāmpukku irācā mūṅkil taṭi.

A bambu stick is king to a snake.





* * *





pāmpukkuc catturu pañcamā?

Are the enemies of snakes few?





* * *





pāmpukkut talaiyaik kāṭṭi mīṉukku vālaik kāṭṭi.

Showing the head to snakes, and the tail to fish.

Spoken of a two-faced person, in allusion to an eel that shows its serpent-like head to snakes, and its fish-like tail to fish.





* * *





pāmpukkup pāl vārttu vaḷarttālum viṣattaik koṭukkum.

Though you feed a snake with milk, it will yield poison.





* * *





pāmpukkup pāl vārttatupōla.

Like pouring out milk to a snake.





* * *





pāmpum kīriyumpōla.

Lik a snake and a mungoose.





* * *





pāmpum kīriyumpōlap pala kālam vāḻntēṉ.

I lived a long time with him or her as a snake with a mungoose.





* * *





pāmpu taṉ paciyai niṉaikkum tērai taṉ vitiyai niṉaikkum.

The snake has regard to its hunger, the frog thinks of its fate.





* * *





pāmpu pacikkil tēraiyaip piṭikkum. atupōla, ciṟiyōr



ciṟiya kāriyaṅkaḷaiyē ceyvār.

When a snake is hungry it will catch a frog, so the vile perform only mean acts.





* * *





pāmpu tiṉkiṟa ūrilē pōṉāl naṭu muṟi namakku eṉṟu irukkavēṇṭum.

If we go to a country of snake eaters, we must be prepared for the middle bit.





* * *





pāmpu pakaiyum tōl uṟavumā?

Is the snake hostile, and its skin friendly?





* * *





pāmpukkup pakai karuṭaṉ.

The hawk is inimical to the snake.





* * *





pāmpum nōvāmaṟ pāmpu aṭitta kōlum nōvāmal irukkavēṇṭum.

So strike that neither the snake, nor the staff with which you strike, shall be hurt or injured.





* * *





pāmpu eṉṟāl paṭaiyum naṭuṅkum.

Even an army will tremble at the word snake.





* * *





pāmpoṭu paḻakēl.

Have nothing to do with snakes.





* * *





pāykiṟa māṭṭukku muṉṉē vētam coṉṉāṟpōla.

Like reciting a portion of the vedas, to a cow about to gore you.





* * *





pāymaram cērnta kākampōl āṉēṉ.

I have become like a crow on the top of a mast.





* * *





pāymaram illā marakkalampōla.

Like a vessel without a mast.





* * *





pāraputti uḷḷa paṟavai patarāl piṭikkappaṭṭatu.

A wise bird has been caught with chaff.





* * *





pārāta uṭaimai pāḻ.

Property not looked after perishes.





* * *





pāraikku ūṭāṭāp pāṟai pacumarattiṉ vērukku ūṭāṭiviṭum.

The rock that resists-a’ crowbar, gives way to the roots of a tender plant.





* * *





pār āḷalāmeṉṟu pāl kuṭikkiṟāy.

Thou drinkest milk, hoping to govern the world.





* * *





pārkkak koṭutta paṇattukku veḷḷikkiḻamaiyā?

Is Friday a sufficient excuse for not returning the coin given you to look at?

It is believed by many that, though it is fortunate to receive money on Friday, it is unfortunate to pay it.





* * *





pārkkiṟa kaṇṇukkuk kēṭkiṟa cevi pollātu.

A listening ear, is worse than a seeing eye.





* * *





pārtta kaṇṇum pūttup pakalum iravu āyiṟṟu.

The eyes have failed by looking too long, and night has followed the day.





* * *





pārtta mukam ellām vēṟṟumukam.

All the faces we see are diverse from one another.





* * *





pārttāṟ pacuppōla, pāyntāl pulippōla.

In appearance like a cow, in action like a tiger.





* * *





pārttirukkat tiṉṟu viḻittirukkak kai kaḻuvuvāṉ.

Gazed at-by starving beggars-he eats and washes his hands.





* * *





pārttiruntavaḷ paccai kuttiṉāḷ, kēṭṭiruntavaḷ vaṟuttuk kuttiṉāḷ.

She who had seen the process pounded it undried, she who had heard of it pounded roasted.





* * *





pārppataṟku ariya parap piramam.

It is impossible to comprehend the supreme Brahma.





* * *





pārppātti ammā māṭu vantatu.

Brahman matron, the cow has come.





* * *





pārppātti uppukkaṇṭam pōkkaṭittatupōla.

As a brahman woman lost her salted mutton.





* * *





pārppār cēvakamum veḷḷaik kutiraic cēvakamum ākātu.

The service of a brahman, and the care of a white horse are bad.





* * *





pārppāṉukku vāypōkkātē āṇṭikku atutāṉum collātē.

Do not waste your breath on a a brahman, nor converse with a mendicant.





* * *





pārppāṉ kaṟuppum paṟaiyaṉ civappum ākātu.

A black brahman, and a fair pariah are not trustworthy.





* * *





pārppāṉ ēḻaiyō pacu ēḻaiyō?

Which is the more helpless, a brahman or a cow?





* * *





pālarukkup palam aḻukai, maṭcattukkup palam utakam.

The strength of a child is crying, and that of fish is water.





* * *





pālar moḻi kēḷātār kuḻal iṉitu yāḻ iṉitu eṉpār.

Those who have not heard the lisping of their own children say, that the flute is sweet, the stringed instrument is sweet.





* * *





pālāṉa neñcu ellām pakai ākkiṉāṉ.

He has made enemies of all whose hearts are pure as milk.





* * *





pālukkum kāval pūṉaikkum tōḻaṉ.

He is the guard of the milk, and also the friend of the cat.





* * *





pālukku vanta pūṉai mōraik kuṭikkumā?

Will the cat that has come for new milk, drink buttermilk?





* * *





pālukkuc cīṉi illai eṉṟōrkkum kūḻukkuk kaṟi illai eṉṟōrkkum vicāram oṉṟē.

The care of those who want sugar with milk, and of those who want curry for their rice, are the same.





* * *





pālumām maruntumām.

It is of use both as milk and medicine.





* * *





pālum patakku , mōrum patakkō ?

Is the rate of fresh milk that of buttermilk?





* * *





pālum veḷḷai mōrum veḷḷai.

Milk is white and buttermilk is also white.





* * *





pālai ūṭṭuvārkaḷ pākkiyattai ūṭṭuvārkaḷā?

They may feed him with milk; can they feed him with good fortune?





* * *





pālaikkuṭittavaṉukkup pāl ēppam varum, kaḷḷaik kuṭittavaṉukkuk kaḷ ēppam varum.

He who has drank milk will belch milk, and he who has drank toddy will belch toddy.





* * *





pālaip pārkkiṟatā pāṉaiyaip pārkkiṟatā?

Do you examine the milk or the vessel containing it?





* * *





pāloṭu kalanta nīrum pālākum.

Water mixed with milk looks like milk.





* * *





pālōṭāyiṉum kālam aṟintu uṇ.

Though with milk, take your meals at the proper time.





* * *





pāl āriyaṉukku, pacu rāmanātacuvāmikku.

Milk to an Aryan, and a cow for Rámanádaswámi.





* * *





pāl irukkiṟatu pākkiyam irukkiṟatu, pālilē pōṭṭuk kuṭikkap pattup parukkaikku vaḻi illai.

There is milk and money, but no means for procuring ten grains of rice.





* * *





pāl toṭṭup pāl kaṟakkavēṇṭum.

One must draw milk after moistening the fingers with milk.





* * *





pāl nakkāta pūṉaiyum paritāṉam vāṅkāta pirāmaṇaṉum uṇṭā ?

Is there a cat that will not lap milk, or a brahman that will refuse a bribe?





* * *





pāl pacuvaik kaṉṟilē teriyum, pākkiyavāṉ piḷḷaiyai mukattilē teriyum.

The milch cow is known by its calf, and the child of the wealthy by its face.





* * *





pāl vārttu muḻukuvāṉ.

He will bathe after applying milk to the head.

Intimating that a person inimical has been got rid of.





* * *





pāvaṭṭam pūppōla naraittum putti illai.

Though as grey as a pávattam flower, he has no sense.





* * *





pāvattiṟku iṭam koṭātavaṉ pāvattaic ceyaṅkoḷvāṉ.

He who does not give place to sin, will conquer it.





* * *





pāvam ceyyātiru, maṉamē!

O my heart, do not sin!





* * *





pāvalar arumai nāvalar aṟivār.

The ability of a poet is best known to the learned.





* * *





pāviyār pōṉa iṭam ellām paḷḷamum tiṭṭiyum.

Whithersoever the simple go, they meet with ups and downs.





* * *





pāviyaip piṭittup pāmpu āṭṭukiṟatu.

Seizing a simpleton and making him dance like a snake.





* * *





pāvil irukkiṟatu pārttum irukkiṟatu.

That which is described in poetry is also seen in nature.





* * *





pāḻāyp pōkiṟatu pacuviṉ vāyilē.

That which is spoiled is put in the mouth of a cow.





* * *





pāḻ ūrukku nari rājā

The jackal is king in a deserted village.





* * *





pāṉaiyil uṇṭāṉāl akappaiyila varum.

If in the pot, it will come into the spoon.





* * *





pāṉaiyilē patakku nel iruntāl mūlaiyilē mukkuṟuṇi teyvam kūttāṭum.

If there be a pathaku of rice in the pot, three kurunies of gods will dance in the corner.





* * *





pi.



piciṉi taṉṉai vacaṉippatu vīṇ.

It is useless to extol a miser.





* * *





piccaṉ vāḻait tōṭṭattilē pukuntatupōla.

As a madman entered the plantain grove.





* * *





piccaikkāraṉukkup payappaṭṭu aṭuppu mūṭṭāmal viṭukiṟatā?

Through fear of beggars do you refrain from lighting your fire?





* * *





piccaikkāraṉai aṭittāṉām cōḷaiyaippōṭṭu uṭaittāṉām.

It is said that he beat the mendicant and broke his alms-dish.





* * *





piccaikkāraṉ cōṟṟilē caṉīccuraṉ pukuntatupōla.

As Saturn entered into the rice of the mendicant.





* * *





piccaic cōṟṟilum ecciṟ cōṟā?

What! scrupulous about leavings in rice got by begging?





* * *





piccaikkāraṉmēlē piramāstiram toṭukkiṟatā?

Is an enchanted arrow discharged at a mendicant?





* * *





piccaikku vantavaṉ peṇṇukku māppiḷḷaiyā?

Do you propose giving your daughter in marriage to one who came to ask alms?





* * *





piccaikku mūttatu kaccavaṭam.

Merchandize is a little better than begging.





* * *





piccaic cōṟṟilum kuḻainta cōṟā?

Is rice offered in charity refused because it is overboiled?





* * *





piccaic cōṟṟiṟkup pañcam uṇṭā?

Is rice given in charity ever scarce?





* * *





piccai pōṭṭatu pōtum nāyaik kaṭṭu.

The alms given are sufficient; tie up the dog.





* * *





piccai pukiṉum kaṟkai naṉṟē.

Learning is good even when one is reduced to begging.





* * *





piccai iṭṭuk keṭṭavaṉum uṇṭā?

Has any one been ruined by giving alms?





* * *





piccai iṭṭuk keṭṭavaṉum illai, piḷḷai peṟṟuk keṭṭavaṉum illai.

None is ruined by giving alms; none is ruined because he has a family.





* * *





piccai eṭuttum catturuviṉ kuṭi keṭu.

Though you have to beg, destroy the family of your enemy.





* * *





piṭāram periteṉṟu puṟṟilē kai vaikkalāmā?

Relying on the efficacy of the prescribed remedy, will you put your hand in a snake hole?





* * *





piṭāraṉukku añcip pāmpu elikku uṟavu āccutām.

It is said that a snake afraid of the charmer, sought the friendship of a rat.





* * *





piṭāriyārē kaṭā vantatu.

O, demon, the bull has come.





* * *





piṭāri varam koṭuttālum occaṉ varam koṭukkiṟatu aritu.

Though a pidári may grant a favour, it will be difficult to obtain it through the priest.





* * *





piṭi aḻaki pukuntāl peṇ aḻaki āvāḷ

If a woman ornamented with jewels enter, she will be regarded as a beautiful woman.





* * *





piṭikkuppiṭi namacivāyamā.

Are incantations to be used again and again.





* * *





piṭitta kompum viṭṭēṉ mititta kompum viṭṭēṉ.

I have left the branch I had seized, and also the one on which I was standing.





* * *





piṭitta kompum oṭintatu mititta kompum muṟintāṟpōl āṉēṉ.

I have become as helpless as if the branch I seized, and the one I stood upon, both broke at the same time.





* * *





piṭittāṟ kaṟṟai, viṭṭāṟ kūḷam

If tied, a bundle; if loose, bits of straw.





* * *





pikṣāpatiyō lakṣāpatiyō?

Is he the prince of beggars, or the first as possessing lacs?





* * *





piṇattai mūṭi maṇattaic cey.

Bury the corpse, and then celebrate the marriage.





* * *





piṇam pōkiṟa iṭattē tukkamum pōkiṟatu.

Sorrow goes away to the place whither the corpse has gone.





* * *





piṇaippaṭṭuk koḷḷātē perumpavattai uttarippāy.

Do not stand security, it will lead to endless evils.





* * *





piṇṭattukkuk kiṭaiyātatu teṇṭattukku akappaṭum

That which cannot be obtained for sustenance, will be found to pay a fine.





* * *





pittam pattu vitam.

Madness is of ten kinds i. e., many kinds.





* * *





pittaḷai nāṟṟam aṟiyātu.

Brass is innocent of its own odour.





* * *





pittarkkuc cila puttikaḷ coṉṉāl pēccaik kēṭpārā?

If advice be given to fools will they iisten to it?





* * *





pittaṉukkut taṉ kuṇam nūliṉum cevvai.

The madman thinks his own character straighter than a line.





* * *





piramacāri ōṭam kaviḻttatupōla.

As the brahmachari upset the boat.





* * *





piramā niṉaittāl āyucukkuk kuṟaiyā?

If Brahma wills it, is there any chance of your life being short?





* * *





piḻaikkappōṉa iṭattilē piḻaimōcam vantatupōla.

As a grave occurrence befell one in the place to which he had gone for a livelihood.





* * *





piḷḷaikkāraṉ piḷḷaikku aḻukiṟaṉ paṇiceyvōṉ kācukku aḻukiṟāṉ.

The parent weeps on account of his child, the servant weeps for his hire.





* * *





piḷḷaikku viḷaiyāṭṭuc cuṇṭelikkup pirāṇa caṅkaṭam.

That which is sport to the child, is death to the mouse.





* * *





piḷḷai patiṉāṟu peṟuvāḷeṉṟu eḻuti iruntālum, puruṣacaṉ illāmal eppaṭip peṟuvāḷ?

Though it were written in the horoscope that she would have sixteen children, how could that be without a husband?





* * *





piḷḷai peṟṟavaṉukkum māṭu paṭaittavaṉukkum veṭkam illai.

A parent and a cowherd know no shame.





* * *





piḷḷai peṟṟavaḷaip pārttup perumūccu viṭṭu āvatu eṉṉa?

What if you look at her who has borne a child, and sigh?





* * *





piḷḷai arumai malaṭi aṟivāḷā?

Does a barren woman understand the endearments of a child?





* * *





piḷḷaiyārukkup peṇ koḷvatupōla.

Like obtaining a wife for Ganésha.





* * *





piḷḷaiyāraip piṭitta caṉi aracamarattaiyum piṭittatupōla.

As Saturn who had seized Ganésha seized also the tree-ficus Indica.





* * *





piḷḷaiyār kōvilil tiruṭaṉ iruppāṉ, coṉṉālum kōḷ.

The thief has taken refuge in the temple of Pillaiyár, it would however be a slander to mention it.





* * *





piḷḷaiyār piṭikkak kuraṅkāy muṭintatu.

The attempt to form an image of Ganésha, ended in the formation of a monkey.





* * *





piḷḷai illā vīṭṭil kiḻavaṉ tuḷḷi viḷaiyāṭukiṟāṉām.

It is said that in the house in which there are no children, an old man plays like a child.





* * *





piḷḷaiyum puḻukkaiyum cari, piḷḷaikkum puḻukkaikkum iṭaṅkoṭātē.

A child and a slave are alike, be not indulgent to a child or a slave.





* * *





piḷḷaiyum kiḷḷit toṭṭilum āṭṭukiṟatā?

Do you rock the cradle, while pinching the child?





* * *





piḷḷaiyaic cākkiṭṭup pūtam viḻuṅkukiṟatu.

Pretending to feed the child, the goblin swallows it.





* * *





piḷḷaivarattiṟkup pōy puruṣaṉaip paṟikoṭuttatupōla.

Like losing her husband, when she went to ask the gift of offspring.





* * *





piḷḷaivaruttam peṟṟavaḷukkut teriyum maṟṟavaḷukkut teriyumā?

A mother knows the pain of travail, is it known to others?





* * *





piḷḷaivīṭṭukkārar cammatittāl pāti vivākam muṭintatupōla.

If the family of the bridegroom consent, half the ceremony of marriage is over.





* * *





piṟakkiṟapoḻutē muṭam āṉāl pēykku iṭṭup paṭaittāl tīrumā?

If a person be a cripple from his birth, can he be cured by offerings made to demons?





* * *





piṟanta piḷḷai piṭi cōṟṟiṟku aḻukiṟatu, piṟakkappōkiṟa piḷḷaikkut taṇṭai cataṅkai tēṭukiṟārkaḷām.

It is said that they are making silver bells for the child about to be born, while the child on the lap is crying for a handful of rice.





* * *





piṟantavaṉ iṟappatē nicam.

It is certain that he who is born will die.





* * *





piṟantaṉa iṟakkum tōṉṟiṉa maṟaiyum.

Those who are born will die, what is visible will vanish.





* * *





piṟanta aṉṟē iṟakkavēṇṭum.

The day of birth leads to death.





* * *





piṟanta ūrukkuc cēlai vēṇṭām, peṇṭu irunta ūrukkut tālivēṇṭām.

A sumptuous cloth is not required in one’s native village, nor a tàli where one is known as a wife.





* * *





piṟar poruḷai iccippāṉa taṉ poruḷai iḻappāṉ.

He who covets the property of others, will lose his own.





* * *





piṟarmaṉait turumpukoḷḷāṉ pirāmaṇaṉ taṇṭukoṇṭāṉ

He who would not carry off a rush belonging to another's roof, robbed a brahman his master of his staff.





* * *





piṟavic ceviṭaṉukkup pēcat tiṟamuṇṭā?

Are there any clever of speech who were born deaf?





* * *





piṟavik kuruṭaṉukkuk kaṇ kiṭaittatupōla.

As one born blind received sight.





* * *





piṟaṉ maṉai pukāmai aṟam eṉat takum.

Not going to another man’s house, deserves to be called virtue.





* * *





piṉṉālē varum palākkāyiṉum muṉṉālē varum kaḷākkāy nalam.

A kalàkkày is better in hand, than a jack fruit in prospect.





* * *





piḷḷai eṉpatum pēcātiruppatum, illai eṉpataṟku aṭaiyāḷam.

By and by, and silence, are tantamount to a refusal.





* * *





pī.



pīram pēṇi pāram tāṅku.

Preserve your strength and bear the burden.





* * *





pīṟiṉa puṭailai perunāḷ irātu.

A ragged cloth will not wear long.





* * *





pīṟṟaṟ paṭṭaikku aṟutaṟkoṭi.

A broken cord, and a ragged basket (well-bucket.)





* * *





pu.



pukaḻcciyāṉukku īntatu pūtakkaṇṇāṭi.

That which was given to the praise-worthy, is a microscope.





* * *





pukaḻntāraip pōṟṟi vāḻ.

Defend those who acknowledge your merit.





* * *





pukaikkiṉum kārakil pollāṅku eḻātu.

Though eaglewood produces smoke, it will do no harm.





* * *





puṅkappukaḻē taṅkattikaḻē.

The contempt of riches is the highest praise.





* * *





puṇṇiyattukkuk kiṇaṟu veṭṭa pūtam puṟappaṭṭatupōla.

As a demon came out of a well that had been dug as an act of religious merit.





* * *





puṇṇiyattukku uḻuta māṭṭaip pallaip piṭittup pārttatupōla.

Like looking at the teeth of a bullock that is ploughing for nothing.





* * *





puṇṇiyam pārkkap pōyp pāvam piṉṉē vantatām.

It is said that evil followed the man who attempted a kind act.





* * *





puṇṇiyam illāta vaḻikāṭṭi vīṇ.

A leader not virtuous is worthless.





* * *





puṇṇilē kōl iṭṭatupōla.

Like thrusting a stick into a wound.





* * *





puṇṇilē puḷi paṭṭatupōlē.

As if a sore had become acidified.





* * *





puṇṇukkō maruntukkō vīcam.

Whether calls for immediate action, the sore or its remedy?





* * *





putiya kāriyaṅkaḷiṟ putiya yōcaṉai vēṇṭum.

New things require fresh consideration.





* * *





putiya vaṇṇāṇum paḻaiya ampaṭṭaṉum tēṭavēṇṭum.

Employ a new washerman, but an old barber.





* * *





putiya vaṇṇāṉ pontukaṭṭi veḷuppāṉ.

A new washerman will wash with great care.





* * *





putiyārai nampip paḻaiyāraik kaiviṭalāmā?

Is it right to forsake old friends in reliance on new ones?





* * *





putuk kuṭattilē vārtta taṇṇīr.

Water poured into a new pot.





* * *





putup pāṉaikku ī cērātu.

Flies do not swarm on a new pot.





* * *





putup peṇṇē putup peṇṇē neruppu eṭuttuvā, uṉakkup piṉṉālē irukkiṟatu ceruppu aṭi.

O bride, O bride bring some fire, what awaits you is slippering.





* * *





putumaiyāṉa kāriyam tāṉ intak kaliyukattil.

Certainly it is a strange occurrence in kaliyuga.





* * *





putumaikku vaṇṇāṉ paṟai taṭṭi veḷuttāṉ.

The washerman inaugurated his washing by beat of tomtom.





* * *





putaiyal eṭuttavaṉaippōlē.

Like one that has discovered buried treasure.





* * *





putti keṭṭa rājāvukku mati keṭṭa mantiri.

A foolish king and an ignorant minister.





* * *





putti aṟṟōrum tūṅkiṉar, putti uṟṟōrum tūṅkiṉar.

Both the foolish and the wise slept.





* * *





puttimāṉ palavāṉ.

An intelligent man is strong.





* * *





putti muṟṟiṉavarkkuc cittiyātatu oṉṟum illai.

There is nothing in which the mature in judgment fail.





* * *





putti uṟap pukaḻ.

As soundness of judgment increases, praise will follow.





* * *





putti aṟṟavarkaḷ pattiyāyc ceyvatum viparītamām.

Even that which fools perform earnestly is wrong.





* * *





putti īṉarkaḷ kuḷikkap pōyc cēṟṟaip pūcik koḷvārkaḷ.

When the foolish go to wash, they will daub themselves with mud.





* * *





puraṭṭip puraṭṭip utaikkiṟapōtum, mīcaiyil maṇ paṭavillai eṉkiṟāṉ.

Even when kicked about and rolling in the dust, he affirms that his mustache did not touch the earth.





* * *





puruṣaṉ aṭikkak koḻuntaṉ kōpittatupōla.

Like the younger brother of the husband, rebuking him for beating his wife.





* * *





puruṣaṉukku ēṟṟa mārāppu.

The cloth covering the breast suited to the rank of her husband.





* * *





puruvattiṟ paṭṭāl karikkumō kaṇṇiṟ paṭṭāl karikkumō?

When does acid cause smarting, when it is applied to the eye-brow or the eyes?





* * *





puli aṭikkumuṉṉē kili aṭikkum.

Struck with fear before struck by the tiger.





* * *





pulikkup piṟantu nakam illāmaṟ pōkumā?

Being born a tiger, will it be without claws?





* * *





pulikkut taṉ kāṭu eṉṉa vēṟṟukkāṭu eṉṉa?

What matters it to the tiger whether he is in his native jungle or another?





* * *





pulikkup piṟantu pūṉaiyāyppōkumā?

Being born a tiger, will it become a cat?





* * *





pulik kūṭṭattil māṉ akappaṭṭatupōla.

As the deer was caught among tigers.





* * *





puli cevi tirukiya mata kaḷiṟu.

An elephant in rut whose ears were wrung off by a tiger.





* * *





puli nakam paṭāviṭṭālum ataṉ mīcai kuttiṉālum viṣam

Though one may escape the claws of the tiger, even the pricking of its whiskers will prove malignant.





* * *





puli pacittāl pullut tiṉṉumā?

When the tiger is hungry, will he eat grass?





* * *





puli patuṅkuvatu pāyccalukku iṭam.

The crouching of the tiger is the prelude to a spring.





* * *





pulip pāl kuṭittavaṉ pōl irukkiṟāṉ.

He is like one fed on tiger’s milk.





* * *





puliyaip pārttu nari cūṭu iṭṭuk koṇṭatupōl.

As the jackal branded itself in imitation of the stripes of a tiger.





* * *





pulaiyaṉukku vākkuc cuttiyum āṇaiyum illai.

The base do not talk decently, nor do they heed oaths.





* * *





pulaiyum kolaiyum kaḷavum tavir.

Avoid lies, murder and theft.





* * *





pul uḷḷa iṭattil māṭṭai āṭṭai mēya oṭṭātu.

It will not allow either sheep or cows to graze where there is grass.





* * *





pullum pūmiyum uḷḷamaṭṭum

As long as vegetation and the earth exist.





* * *





pullum pūmiyum kallum kāvēriyum uḷḷamaṭṭum.

As long as vegetation, earth, rocks and the Cauvery exist.





* * *





pullu viṟkiṟa kaṭaiyilē pū viṟkiṟatu.

Selling flowers in a grass market.





* * *





pullait tiṉṉum māṭupōlap puliyait tiṉṉum cennāy utavumā?

Will the red dog that eats tiger's flesh prove as useful as the cow that eats grass?





* * *





puḻukkai kalam kaḻuvi tiṉṉātu.

The low-born does not eat from a washed plate.





* * *





puḻukkai oḻukkam aṟiyumā piṇṇākku caṭṭi patam aṟiyumā?

Does the low-born appreciate good manners, does the oil cake pan know when the cake is baked?





* * *





puḻukkai oḻukkam aṟiyātu pittaḷai nāṟṟam aṟiyātu.

Menials are ignorant of manners, brass is unconscious of its ill odour.





* * *





puḻukkaikkup putti piṭariyilē.

The sensibility of menials is in the neck.





* * *





puḻuṅkip puḻuṅkip mā iṭittālum puḻukkaiccikku oru koḻukkaṭṭai.

Though the slave girl who pounds rice labours so as to perspire, her share is only a cake.





* * *





puḻutta carakkukkuk koḻutta paṇam.

Fat money for rotten articles.





* * *





puḷiya marattil ēṟiṉavaṉ pal kūciṉāl iṟaṅkuvāṉ.

He who has climbed a tamarind tree will come down when his teeth are set on edge.





* * *





puḷiyam paḻattukkup puḷippup pukutaviṭa varukiṟāyō?

Do you come to infuse acidity into the tamarind fruit?





* * *





puḷukiṉālum poruntap puḷuka vēṇṭum.

Even when boasting do it so as to secure belief.





* * *





puḷḷik kaṇakkaṉ paḷḷikku āvāṉā?

Is a practical arithmetician fit to teach the theory of arithmetic?





* * *





puṟakkaṭai maruntu kavaikku utavātu.

The medicinal plant in the backyard will not avail in an emergency.





* * *





puṟakkuṭattut taṇṇīrpōla.

Like water on the outside of a pot.





* * *





puṟṟilē āntai viḻippatupōla.

Blinking like an owl on an ant-hill.





* * *





puṟṟilē īcal puṟappaṭṭatupōla.

Coming forth like a swarm of winged white ants from an ant-hill.





* * *





puṟṟilē kiṭanta puṭaiyaṉ eḻuppiṉatupōla.

Rushing as a beaver snake from its hole.





* * *





puṟṟilē īcal puṟappaṭṭālum, maṇṇilē kaṟaiyāṉ kaṭṭiṉālum maḻai varavē varum.

When winged white ants issue out of a hole, and white ants swarm, it will certainly rain.





* * *





pū.



pū aracu irukkap poṉṉukku aḻuvāṉēṉ?

Why weep for gold while you have the tulip tree?





* * *





pūcaṇikkāy attaṉai muttuk kātil ēṟṟukiṟatā mūkkiṟ ēṟṟukiṟatā?

If a pearl be as large as a pumpkin, where is it to be worn, in the ear or in the nose?





* * *





pūcaṇikkāy eṭuttavaṉait tōḷilē kāṇalām.

If one has taken a pumpkin, its mark may be seen on his shoulder.





* * *





pūcaṇikkāy aḻukiṉatupōla.

Like a decayed pumpkin.





* * *





pūcap pūcap poṉ niṟam.

The more you gild it, the more like gold will it appear.





* * *





pūcāri pū muṭikkap pōṉāṉām, pū ālaṅkāṭu palāk kāṭāyp pōccutām.

The priest began to wear flowers, and the flower garden became a grove of jack trees.





* * *





pūcai mukattilē karaṭiyai viṭṭu ōṭṭiṉatupōla.

Like driving a bear among those engaged in worship.





* * *





pūcci marikkiṟatillai, puḻuvum cākiṟatillai.

Insects do not perish, nor do worms die.





* * *





pūṭṭip pucikkāmaṟ putaippār īyaippōl īṭṭi iḻappār.

Those who lock up their treasure and refuse to enjoy it will be deprived of it, as bees are deprived of their honey.





* * *





pūṭṭum tiṟappumpōla.

As a lock and its key.





* * *





pūtalam yāvum pōṟṟu muccuṭar.

The three lights which the whole world extol.





* * *





pūttāṉamāṉa piḷḷai āttāḷait tālik kaṭṭiṟṟām.

It is said that an indulged child tied a tali on his mother.





* * *





pūttuc coriyap poṟuppārkaḷ pūṭṭik kaṭṭak kalaṅkuvārkaḷ.

They can endure seeing their tree shedding its blossoms, but will be disquieted at seeing others string and wear them.





* * *





pūmi atira naṭavāta puṇṇiyavāṉ.

A virtuous person under whose footsteps the earth trembles not.





* * *





pūmi tirutti uṇ.

Till the soil, and enjoy its produce.





* * *





pūrāṭakkāraṉōṭu pōrāṭa muṭiyātu.

One cannot strive with one born under the star purádam.





* * *





pūriyōrkku illaic cīriya oḻukkam.

The base are void of good manners.





* * *





pūrvōttaram mēru kāttiram pōl irukkiṟatu.

His pedigree is weighty as mount Meru.





* * *





pūlōkamutaliyār paṭṭam, pukuntu pārttāl poṭṭal.

His title is lord of the world, but when examined he is found empty.





* * *





pū viṟkum kaṭaiyil pul viṟpatupōla.

As grass is sold in a flower market.





* * *





pū viṟṟa kaṭaiyil pul viṟkalāmā?

May grass be sold in the flower market?





* * *





pū viṟṟa kācu maṇakkumā pulāl viṟṟa kācu nāṟumā?

Is the money obtained from the sale of flowers fragrant, does that obtained from the sale of flesh stink?





* * *





pū viṟṟavaṉaip poṉ viṟkappaṇṇuvēṉ.

I will enable him who sold flowers to sell gold.





* * *





pūvuṭaṉ kūṭi nārum maṇam peṟṟatupōla.

As a fibre used for stringing flowers partook of their fragrance.





* * *





pūvuḷ maṅkaiyām poṟkoṭiyām pōṉa iṭam ellām ceruppaṭiyām.

It is said that she is Lackshmi residing in flowers, and she is a golden creeper, yet whithersoever she goes she is slippered.





* * *





pūṉai uḷḷa iṭattilē eli pēraṉ pērtti eṭukkiṟatām.

The rat lives to see its grand-sons and grand-daughters in a place where there is a cat.





* * *





pūṉai kaṭṭum toḻuvattil āṉai kaṭṭalāmā?

Can you tether an elephant in a place suited for tying up a cat?





* * *





pūṉaikku illai tāṉamum tavamum.

Alms-giving and penance are not prribed to cats.





* * *





pūṉaickuk koṇṭāṭṭam elikkut tiṇṭāṭṭam.

Amusement to the cat, and agony to the rat.





* * *





pūṉaikkuc cimāḷam vantāl pīṟṟaṟpāyil puraḷumām.

It is said that if a cat be merry, she will roll about on an old mat.





* * *





pūṉai pāl kuṭikkiṟatupōla.

As a cat drinks milk.





* * *





pūṉaipōl aṭaṅkiṉāṉ pulipōl pāyntāṉ.

He was quiet as a cat, and sprang like a tiger.





* * *





pūṉaiyaik kaṇṭa kiḷipōlap pulampi aḻukiṟāṉ.

He cries as a parrot encountered by a cat.





* * *





pūṉaiyaittāṉ vīṭṭup puliyeṉṟum, eli aracaṉeṉṟum colvārkaḷ.

A cat is called a domestic tiger, and the king of rats.





* * *





pe.



peṭṭiyiṟ pāmpupōl aṭaṅkiṉāṉ.

He was as quiet as a snake in a box.





* * *





peṭṭi pīṟṟal vāyk kaṭṭut tiṟam.

The basket is torn, but the rim is strong.





* * *





peṭṭaik kōḻi kūviyō viṭikiṟatu?

Does the day break at the crowing of a hen?





* * *





peṭṭaik kōḻi taṭṭik kūvumā?

Can a hen flap her wings and crow like a cock?





* * *





peṇ eṉṟāl pēyum iraṅkum.

Even a demon will pity a woman.





* * *





peṇṇācai oru pakkam maṇṇācai oru pakkam.

Love of women on the one hand, and love of property on the other.





* * *





peṇcāti illātavaṉ pēyaik kaṭṭit taḻuviṉatu pōla.

As the man who had no wife embraced a demoness.





* * *





peṇ cātiyait tāy vīṭṭil viṭṭavaṉukku oru coṭṭu.

A cuff for the man who left his wife at her mother’s.





* * *





peṇcāti kāṟkaṭṭu, piḷḷai vāykkaṭṭu.

Fettered with a wife, and muzzled with a child.





* * *





peṇṭāṭṭi koṇṭatum tiṇṭāṭṭam paṭṭatum pōtum.

Enough of taking a wife and suffering the consequences.





* * *





peṇṭirkku aḻaku pēcātiruttal.

Not to remonstrate with her husband is an ornament in a woman.





* * *





peṇṭukaḷ cūttukkup purimaṇai.

A straw twist for women to squat on.





* * *





peṇṭukaḷukku peṟṟār iṭattilum piḷḷaikaḷ iṭattilum mūppu illai.

Women have no influence over parents and children.





* * *





peṇṭukaḷ cōṟṟukkut taṇṭam illai.

Women are not in danger of forfeiting their rice.





* * *





peṇṇarampaik kūttukkup pōy pēyk kūttu āccutē.

The going to a dance of celestials ended as a dance of devils.





* * *





peṇṇiṉ kuṇam aṟivēṉ campanti vāy aṟivēṉ.

I know the character of the bride, and the boisterousness of her mother-in-law.





* * *





peṇṇukkup poṉ iṭṭuppār, cuvarukku maṇ iṭṭuppār.

Look at a woman after adorning her with jewels, and at a wall when you have plastered it with mud.





* * *





peṇṇukkum poṉṉukkum tōṟpu uṇṭā?

Is any one ever tired of women and wealth?





* * *





peṇṇukkup pōyp poṉṉukkup piṉvāṅkalāmā?

Having gone to take a wife, can you turn back because marriage is expensive?





* * *





peṇṇukku māmiyārum, piḷḷaikku vāttiyārum.

For a wife, a mother-in-law, for a boy, a tutor.





* * *





peṇṇaik koṭuttavaḷō kaṇṇaik koṭuttavaḷō?

Did she give one a wife, or did she give one her eye?





* * *





peṇṇaip piḻai poṟukkap peṟṟatāy vēṇṭāmō?

Are you content to lose your mother in order to pardon your wife?





* * *





peṇṇaik koṇṭu paiyaṉ pēyāṉāṉ, piḷḷaip peṟṟuc ciṟukki nāyāṉāḷ.

Having married a wife the boy has become a fool, having given birth to a child the damsel has become mean in appearance.





* * *





peṇṇai vēṇṭum eṉṟāl iḷiyaṟ kaṇṇai nakku.

If you want the woman, lick her bleared eyes.





* * *





peṇ putti kēṭkiṟavaṉ pēy.

He who listens to the advice of women is a fool.





* * *





peṇ putti piṉ putti.

The thoughts of women are after-thoughts.





* * *





peṇ vaḷartti pīrkkaṅkoṭi.

The growing of women is that of a gourd creeper.





* * *





periyārait tuṇaikkoḷ.

Secure the good will of the great.





* * *





periyōr ellām periyarum allar.

All who are high in stature are not great.





* * *





periyōrmuṉ tāḻntu pēcil, nāṇalaippōl nimirntu koḷvārkaḷ.

When speaking submissively to the great, they preserve an erect posture like a reed.





* * *





periyōr muṉ etirttuppēcil, veḷḷattiṟkkumuṉ maraṅkaḷaippōl vīḻvārkaḷ,

If they should contradict the great, they will fall like trees before a flood.





* * *





peruṅ kayiṟu muṭi aḻuntātu.

A tight knot cannot be formed in a thick rope.





* * *





peruṅkāyam irunta pāṇṭam.

An earthen pot in which assafœtida was kept.





* * *





peruṅ kāṟṟil pīḷaippañcu paṟakkiṟatu pōla.

Flying like cotton before a gale of wind.





* * *





peruṅ kulattil piṟantālum putti aṟṟavaṉ karumpup pūppōla.

Though born in a high family, a fool is like a sugar-cane flower.





* * *





peruṅ koṭai piccaikkārarukkut tuṇivu.

Beggars presume on large gifts.





* * *





peru neruppukku īram uṇṭā?

Will moisture affect a great fire.





* * *





peru marattaic cuṟṟiya vaḷḷikkoṭipōla.

Like a creeping plant-Dioscorea sativa-round a large tree.





* * *





peru maḻai viḻuntāṟ kuḷirātu.

No feeling of cold in a heavy fall of rain.





* * *





perumāḷ irukkiṟa varaiyil tirunāḷ uṇṭu.

As long as Perumal exists holy days will be observed.





* * *





perumāḷ eṉkiṟa peyarai māṟṟap periya perumāḷ āccutu.

The name Perumal being changed has become great Perumal.





* * *





perumāṉaic cērntōrkku piṟappu illai, piccaic cōṟṟiṟku eccil illai.

Those who have attained union with God are not subject to future births, rice given in alms is not refused because it is refuse.





* * *





perumāḷ niṉaittāl vāḻvu kuṟaivā, piramā niṉaittāl āyucu kuṟaivā?

If God is pleased, will there be any lack of prosperity, if Brahma favour, will one’s life be short?
